# 18 Ağu 2026 — kaçak ajan turundan kurtarılan araştırma

497 ajan koştu, 423'ü limitte boş öldü. Çıktı üreten ajanların raporları aşağıda.
Konular: dikey AI şirketleri (OpenEvidence/Abridge/Legora), Lovable, launch kanalları,
dev tool pazarlaması, platform-öldürür-mü sorusu (DevDay sonrası vektör DB'ler).

## Rapor 1

## Confirmed quotes (fetched the page myself)

### The single most useful find — Casado holds BOTH positions at once

**Martin Casado (a16z, General Partner)** — a16z podcast "Where Value Will Accrue in AI," posted **27 May 2025**. Transcript fetched at https://podscripts.co/podcasts/a16z-podcast/where-value-will-accrue-in-ai-martin-casado-sarah-wang

Anti-moat:
> "There is no inherent endemic moat in the technology stack to AI other than just overcoming the bootstrap problem."

> "A startup will come and it'll do a model and we'll get a bunch of users on that model and that'll be great, but then they have to kind of revert to traditional software to build traditional mo[a]ts."

Pro-wrapper (same episode, same speaker):
> "GP[T] wrapper was this like derogatory term. I think we'd come as a conclusion, like that's not even a thing."

> "When someone writes software on the cloud, you don't call it a cloud wrapper. Like you have all of the complexity in the software that's on top of these models."

**Caveat, important:** this is a machine transcript with visible ASR errors — it renders "GPT" as "GPG", and "moats" as "modes" in two places. The wording is right but I would not quote it character-for-character without checking the audio.

A cleaned-up version of the same claim was published by **TechCrunch, 14 Sep 2025** (fetched), attributing it to the a16z podcast:
> "As far as we can tell, there is no inherent moat in the technology stack for AI"
https://techcrunch.com/2025/09/14/selling-coffee-beans-to-starbucks-how-the-ai-boom-could-leave-ais-biggest-companies-behind/

a16z's own episode page (fetched, no transcript body) bills the episode as covering "the myth of the GPT wrapper" and "where defensibility is emerging" — so the pro-wrapper framing is a16z's own editorial line, not just a stray remark.

**Sarah Wang (a16z)**, same episode:
> "Where you have complex workflows and a ton of customer data where deep integrations actually are necessary to get that last mile value for the customer... the specialized AI apps are sort of crushing any either foundation model layer."

### a16z, anti-moat and blunt about it

**Bryan Kim (a16z)**, "In Consumer AI, Momentum Is the Moat," **11 Jun 2025**, https://a16z.com/momentum-as-ai-moat/ (fetched)
> "How do you build a moat in consumer AI? Right now, sorry to say, there is none."

> "In AI today, it's often the case that whoever builds first, iterates fastest, and distributes best wins."

> "What matters is velocity: how fast you can launch, gain traction, and seize mindshare."

> "The faster you ship new capabilities, new features, new models, the more distance you put between yourself and the second fastest pigeon, and the third, and the rest of the flock."

Note Kim left a16z around April 2026 to raise ~$100M for a consumer AI fund, Mido Capital (search-snippet level, UNVERIFIED).

### Sequoia — moat exists, but it isn't the data

**Sonya Huang, Pat Grady (and GPT-4, credited as co-author)**, "Generative AI's Act Two," **20 Sep 2023**, https://sequoiacap.com/article/generative-ai-act-two/ (fetched)
> "The moats are in the customers, not the data."

> "the data that application companies generate does not create an insurmountable moat"

> "workflows and user networks seem to be creating more durable sources of competitive advantage."

> "We still believe that there will be a separation between the 'application layer' companies and foundation model providers"

> "the most successful user-facing applications out of the gate have been vertically integrated."

> "Generative AI's first year out the gate—'Act 1'—came from the technology-out. We discovered a new 'hammer'—foundation models—and unleashed a wave of novelty apps"

That last one is the closest thing Sequoia has to a "thin wrapper" jab — "novelty apps" is the euphemism.

**David Cahn (Sequoia)**, "AI's $600B Question," **20 Jun 2024**, https://sequoiacap.com/article/ais-600b-question/ (fetched). This essay is about infra economics, not wrappers, but the value-capture lines are usable:
> "GPU computing is increasingly turning into a commodity, metered per hour."

> "Without a monopoly or oligopoly, high fixed cost + low marginal cost businesses almost always see prices competed down to marginal cost (e.g., airlines)."

> "Company builders focused on delivering value to end users will be rewarded handsomely."

## Partially verified

**Chetan Puttagunta (Benchmark)** — GTM Newsletter, **25 Jun 2026**, https://thegtmnewsletter.substack.com/p/benchmark-ai-startup-investing-chetan-puttagunta (fetched). Only one line is genuinely a direct quote:
> "When the code is the cheap part. When the cost of writing code trends toward zero, the product itself stops being defensible."

The widely-circulated longer version — "...the moat moves to everything that isn't code: the customer research, the trust, the last-mile work of making a business measurably better - and of course, distribution" — is the **newsletter author's paraphrase**, not Chetan's words. A search snippet presented the whole block as one Chetan quote. **Do not use the long version as a quote.** This is exactly the misattribution trap the task warned about.

**Packy McCormick** (Not Boring, not a big-firm VC), via VC Cafe (fetched), dated ~31 May 2024, originally a tweet:
> "Foundation models are converging and will become commoditized, the value is at the app layer"
I did not fetch the original tweet. UNVERIFIED at source.

## UNVERIFIED — listing anyway, per instruction

- **Elad Gil**: I fetched the TechCrunch 15 May 2023 interview. He does **not** discuss wrappers there. The only moat line is "They started scaling before they had a lot of customers, or before it was clear that they had a moat that would create some sort of defensibility for their business." — and it's about scaling prematurely, not wrappers. The widely-repeated "Elad Gil on wrappers" framing (does a better model make your product better or worse?) appears only as search-engine paraphrase. **No verified Gil wrapper quote found.**
- **Bill Gurley**: I found **zero** direct Gurley quotes on wrappers or app-layer moats. Search results attributing wrapper/commoditization views to BG2Pod episodes were AI-generated summaries of podcast pages, with no transcript backing. Anything you see quoted as "Gurley on wrappers" should be treated as fabricated until an actual transcript is found.
- **Sarah Tavel (Benchmark)**: no verified quote found. Snippets described her thesis (consumer AI shifting from technical to social/product-design problem) but no primary source retrieved.
- **Marc Andreessen, Guido Appenzeller, Peter Fenton, Gavin Baker, Brad Gerstner, Jason Lemkin, Tomasz Tunguz**: nothing verified on wrappers specifically.

## Dump — things I saw that you didn't ask for

**The strongest structural finding is the split inside a16z, not between firms.** a16z's position is not "wrappers are bad." It is "the term 'wrapper' is meaningless AND there is no technology moat anyway." Casado and Kim both say the moat doesn't exist in the stack, then locate defensibility in non-technical things — brand, velocity, distribution, workflow depth. Casado explicitly reaches for brand: "Everybody knew Google and everybody knew Amazon and you had these big brand mo[a]ts" and "We're starting to see that come back again in this space." So the honest 2025-26 VC consensus is closer to *"everything is a wrapper, so stop arguing about wrappers and argue about distribution"* than to either pole the task framed.

**Sequoia moved in the opposite direction from where people assume.** Their 2022 "Creative New World" piece implied data flywheels would be the moat; Act Two (2023) explicitly retracts that — "the data that application companies generate does not create an insurmountable moat." That reversal is a better story than the pro/anti wrapper fight.

**Cursor is the load-bearing example on the pro-wrapper side**, cited in both a16z sources I fetched. In the Latent Space interview (19 Feb 2026, https://www.latent.space/p/a16z) Casado's framing is about token-path margin — "How do I extract margin on the tokens that are going through? Like, everybody has to be on the token path" — i.e. the wrapper defense increasingly depends on training your own models down-stack, which arguably concedes the anti-wrapper point.

**Search engines are actively generating fake VC quotes on this topic.** Multiple searches returned confident, well-formed quotes attributed to named partners that vanished on fetching the source. The Chetan case above is a concrete instance. Treat any wrapper/moat quote sourced from a search snippet as fabricated until fetched.

**What I could not access:** a16z.com blocked intermittently (the Casado/Wang episode page has no transcript body at all; the Kim essay did fetch on retry). www.sequoiacap.com is blocked but bare sequoiacap.com works — useful for future runs. Never retrieved: BG2 podcast transcripts, "The Empty Promise of Data Moats" (a16z, would be the best anti-data-moat primary source), Sequoia AI Ascent talk transcripts, "Generative AI's Act o1," Sequoia's "AI's $200B Question," a16z's "The New Business of AI." All of those are likely productive on a second pass.

## Rapor 2

## A. AWS Budget Actions

### A1. IAM service role — trust policy (EXACT)
Source: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/billing-example-policies.html (section `#example-budgets-applySCP`, linked from the budgets-action-role page)

```json
{
"Version":"2012-10-17",
"Statement": [
  {
    "Effect": "Allow",
    "Principal": { "Service": "budgets.amazonaws.com" },
    "Action": "sts:AssumeRole",
    "Condition": {
      "ArnLike": { "aws:SourceArn": "arn:aws:budgets::123456789012:budget/*" },
      "StringEquals": { "aws:SourceAccount": "123456789012" }
    }
  }
]
}
```
Both condition keys are present in the official example (confused-deputy prevention). Note the budget ARN has an **empty region field**: `arn:aws:budgets::ACCOUNT:budget/*`.

### A2. Permissions policy — inline/customer-managed (EXACT)
Same URL. Full action list for "apply IAM policies and SCPs AND target EC2/RDS":
`ec2:DescribeInstanceStatus`, `ec2:StartInstances`, `ec2:StopInstances`, `iam:AttachGroupPolicy`, `iam:AttachRolePolicy`, `iam:AttachUserPolicy`, `iam:DetachGroupPolicy`, `iam:DetachRolePolicy`, `iam:DetachUserPolicy`, `organizations:AttachPolicy`, `organizations:DetachPolicy`, `rds:DescribeDBInstances`, `rds:StartDBInstance`, `rds:StopDBInstance`, `ssm:StartAutomationExecution` — all on `"Resource": "*"`.

The IAM/SCP-only variant (`#example-budgets-IAM-SCP`) is the same list minus the ec2/rds/ssm actions.

### A3. AWS managed policies (names EXACT)
Source: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/billing-permissions-ref.html

| Policy | Attaches to | Grants |
|---|---|---|
| `AWSBudgetsActions_RolePolicyForResourceAdministrationWithSSM` | the **budgets service role** | ec2/rds start-stop-describe gated by `"aws:CalledVia": ["ssm.amazonaws.com"]`, plus `ssm:StartAutomationExecution` scoped to documents `AWS-StartEC2Instance`, `AWS-StopEC2Instance`, `AWS-StartRdsInstance`, `AWS-StopRdsInstance` and their automation-definition/automation-execution ARNs. **This is the policy budgets-action-role.html recommends.** |
| `AWSBudgetsActionsWithAWSResourceControlAccess` | the **human user/admin** | `budgets:*`, `aws-portal:ViewBilling`, `iam:PassRole` conditioned on `"iam:PassedToService": "budgets.amazonaws.com"`, plus `aws-portal:ModifyBilling`, `ec2:DescribeInstances`, `iam:List{Groups,Policies,Roles,Users}`, `organizations:List{Accounts,OrganizationalUnitsForParent,Policies,Roots}`, `rds:DescribeDBInstances`, `sns:ListTopics` |
| `AWSBudgetsReadOnlyAccess` | user | `aws-portal:ViewBilling`, `budgets:ViewBudget`, `budgets:Describe*`, `budgets:ListTagsForResource` |
| `BudgetsServiceRolePolicy` | SLR | `billing:GetBillingViewData` only (added 08/06/2025) |

Key distinction the docs make explicit: the managed policy does NOT cover IAM/SCP attach — it only covers "control AWS resources" (EC2/RDS via SSM). For IAM policy / SCP actions you must add the inline/customer-managed policy from A2. Docs recommend the managed policy because "new functions and capabilities are added to the managed policy by default."

Note: `AWSBudgetsActions_RolePolicyForResourceAdministrationWithSSM` was **updated 04/07/2026** to require automation-execution and document permissions for `ssm:StartAutomationExecution`. If you copy an older snippet you get a stale policy.

### A4. Configuration
Source: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/budgets-action-configure.html

- Actions attach to an **alert threshold** on a cost budget or a usage budget (not to the budget itself).
- Threshold is **Absolute value** or **% of budgeted amount**.
- Trigger type: **"Next to the threshold, choose Actual to create an alert for actual spend. Or, choose Forecasted to create an alert for forecasted spend."** → both ACTUAL and FORECASTED are supported.
- Action types: **apply an IAM policy, attach an SCP, or target specific EC2 or RDS instances**. "Only a management account can apply SCPs."
- **"You can apply multiple budget actions to a single alert."**
- Approval: field is *"Do you want to automatically run this action when this threshold is exceeded"* → **Yes** (auto-execute) or **No** (manual run from the Alert details page).
- Per-action notification: "Use the same alert settings when you defined this threshold" or "Use different alert settings."
- Hard requirement per alert: at least one of {email recipient, SNS topic, budget action}.
- Max 10 email addresses per notification. Chat alerts go through Amazon Q Developer in chat applications (formerly AWS Chatbot) → Slack or Chime.
- Status shown in the **Actions** column: configured count, "Requires approval", completed.

### A5. Manual approval, reset, rollback
Source: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/budgets-action-review.html

- You get a notification whether the action is pending or already ran, **regardless of your action preferences**.
- Approve path: Budget details → Alerts → **Requires approval** → pick alert → Alert details → select action → **Run action** → "Yes, I am sure".
- Statuses: **Standby** (Budgets actively evaluating), **Requires approval** (initiated, waiting), **Completed**, **Reversed**.
- **Reversed** = action undone, and "AWS Budgets will no longer evaluate the action for the remaining budgeted period."
- **Reset** re-arms a reversed action so Budgets re-evaluates it in the same period.
- Console **Action history** shows only the last **60 days**. Full history via CloudTrail or the `DescribeBudgetActionHistories` API.

### A6. Cost
Source: https://aws.amazon.com/aws-cost-management/aws-budgets/pricing/

- Budget monitoring + notifications: **free**.
- **"Your first two action-enabled budgets are free (regardless of the number of actions you configure per budget) per month."**
- **"Each subsequent action-enabled budget will incur a $0.10 daily cost."**
- Budget Reports by email: **$0.01 per delivery** (daily/weekly/monthly).

### A7. Quotas
Source: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/management-limits.html

- Free budgets with actions per account: **2**
- Actions per budget: **10**
- Budget actions per account: **100**
- Budgets per management account: **20,000**
- Budgets using a custom billing view: 150

## B. AWS Cost Anomaly Detection

### B1. Cost
- **Free.** FAQ: *"The default configuration of CAD offers Payer and Regular accounts the benefit of having anomalous spend detection across all deployed AWS services with no effort or cost."* — https://aws.amazon.com/aws-cost-management/aws-cost-anomaly-detection/faqs/
- Neither https://aws.amazon.com/aws-cost-management/aws-cost-anomaly-detection/ nor https://aws.amazon.com/aws-cost-management/pricing/ carries an explicit price line — **there is no CAD pricing page.** The "free" claim rests on the FAQ + the launch blog. Prerequisite: Cost Explorer must be enabled (CAD is a feature inside Cost Explorer) — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/settingup-ad.html

### B2. Auto-enable
Source: https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2023/03/aws-cost-anomaly-detection-configured-cost-explorer-users/

Since **March 27, 2023**, every new Cost Explorer customer with a Payer or Regular account gets automatic configuration: an **AWS services monitor** + a **daily email subscription** to the **primary email address on the account**, alerting when actual spend is **over $100 AND exceeds 40% of expected spend**. Existing/pre-2023 users must configure manually. Opt-out exists (`opting-out-cad.html`).

### B3. Alert channels + frequency
Source: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/getting-started-ad.html

- **Individual alerts** — fires as soon as an anomaly is detected, possibly several per day. **Requires an SNS topic** (email alone is not enough for this mode).
- **Daily summaries** — email, **top 10** alerts from the previous day sorted by cost impact, generated at **00:00 UTC**; an anomaly at 04:30 UTC Jan 14 lands in the Jan 15 00:00 UTC summary. Requires ≥1 email recipient.
- **Weekly summaries** — one email per week. Requires ≥1 email recipient.
- Chat (Slack / Amazon Chime) is not a native channel: you route SNS → **Amazon Q Developer in chat applications** (the renamed AWS Chatbot).
- Thresholds: **absolute** (dollar impact) or **percentage** (total impact %). Up to **two** thresholds per subscription combined with **AND** / **OR**.
- Sub-threshold anomalies still get detected and are visible in the **Detected anomalies** tab; they just don't alert. Recurring anomalies re-alert each day the threshold is still met.
- EventBridge and AWS User Notifications are additional integrations (`cad-eventbridge.html`, `cad-user-notifications.html`).

### B4. Detection latency
Source: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/manage-ad.html

- CAD runs **approximately three times a day** on **net unblended cost**.
- Backed by Cost Explorer data, which lags **up to 24 hours** → **"it can take up to 24 hours to detect an anomaly after a usage occurs."**
- A **new monitor can take 24 hours** to start detecting.
- A **new service subscription needs 10 days of historical usage** before anomalies can be detected for it.
- Quotas page restates: time to detect = up to 24h; historical data required = 10 days minimum.

### B5. Monitors / subscriptions quotas (EXACT, from management-limits.html)

AWS managed monitors:
- AWS services monitor per account (mgmt and member): **1**
- Additional AWS managed monitor (linked account / cost allocation tag / cost category) per management account: **1**
- Total AWS managed monitors: **2** per management account, **1** per member account
- Values tracked per AWS managed monitor: **5,000** (if you exceed, it tracks the top 5,000 by spend)

Customer managed monitors:
- Total per management account: **500**
- Values per monitor (linked accounts or tag values): **10**
- Values per monitor (cost category values): **1**

General:
- Alert subscriptions per account: **100**
- Email recipients per subscription: **10**
- SNS topics per subscription: **1**
- Monitors per alert subscription: **502** (all monitors can be attached)

Monitor dimensions: AWS services, Linked Accounts (mgmt account only), Cost Allocation Tags, Cost Categories. AWS-managed AWS-services monitors track each value **independently**; customer-managed monitors **aggregate** the selected values. **You cannot convert an existing customer managed monitor to an AWS managed monitor.**

## DÖKÜM — sorulmayan ama önemli olan

1. **CAD does not cover Bedrock/Anthropic or any AWS Marketplace spend.** manage-ad.html states verbatim: CAD "does not monitor third-party products and services available through AWS Marketplace. This includes third-party LLMs like Anthropic Claude models on Amazon Bedrock (which appear... under the legal entity 'Anthropic, PBC')... To get alerts for these charges, use AWS Budgets." For an LLM-cost guardrail, CAD is the wrong tool — Budgets with the **Billing entity** filter is the right one. This is likely the single most load-bearing finding here.
2. **CAD unsupported services list** (quotas page): AWS Marketplace, AWS Support, WorkSpaces, Cost Explorer, Budgets, AWS Shield, Route 53, ACM. Only analyzes **Usage** charge type and **NetUnblendedCost**.
3. CAD is unavailable for bill source accounts using **billing transfer**, and doesn't support billing transfer views.
4. Monitors/subscriptions are **only visible to the account that created them**. Linked-account / tag / cost-category monitors can only be created in the **management account**.
5. **Budget actions ≠ hard cap.** Nothing in these pages stops spend; the strongest primitive is attach-a-deny-IAM-policy/SCP or stop EC2/RDS. Serverless (Lambda, Bedrock, S3) is not stoppable by any listed action type.
6. Budget actions EC2/RDS stop runs **through SSM Automation documents** — so SSM must be functional in the account/region, and the managed policy's `aws:CalledVia: ssm.amazonaws.com` condition means direct ec2:StopInstances calls by that role would be denied.
7. `AWSBudgetsActionsWithAWSResourceControlAccess` grants `budgets:*` and a broad `iam:PassRole` (Resource `*`, only service-conditioned) — that's a privilege-escalation-adjacent grant, not something to hand out casually.
8. **DOĞRULANMADI:** whether FORECASTED triggers are permitted for *usage* budgets and for every action type (docs describe the UI generically, without per-type caveats). Also DOĞRULANMADI: the API-level field names/enums for actions (`ActionType`, `NotificationType`, `ApprovalModel`, `ExecutionRoleArn`) — I did not open the API Reference; the userguide pages don't name them.
9. **DOĞRULANMADI:** whether the $0.10/day action-enabled-budget charge is prorated or charged for any day the budget exists. Pricing page says only "each subsequent action-enabled budget will incur a $0.10 daily cost."
10. Note the quota mismatch trap: the "2 free action-enabled budgets" (pricing) and "Number of free budgets with actions per account: 2" (quotas) are the same limit stated twice — it is a **billing** free tier, not a hard cap. The hard caps are 100 budget actions per account, 10 per budget.
11. Retrieval notes: WebFetch never hit the "unable to verify domain" failure — zero retries needed, 11 fetches all succeeded. **However**, my first fetch of `billing-quotas.html` returned fabricated-looking numbers (20,000 budgets / 10 actions / **100 CAD monitors** / 100 subscriptions with an invented "Requesting Quota Increases" section). The canonical `management-limits.html` contradicts it (monitors are 2 managed / 500 customer managed, not 100). **Numbers in section B5 and A7 above come from management-limits.html and supersede that.** Flagging because the wrong page is easy to land on.
12. Not fetched / not checked: `budgets-sns-policy.html` (SNS topic policy required for budget notifications — you will need this if wiring SNS), `cross-service-confused-deputy-prevention.html`, `cad-eventbridge.html`, `cad-user-notifications.html`, `accesscontrol-ad.html` (resource-level/ABAC access for CAD), `transition-monitors.html`, the AWS Budgets API Reference.

# AI vs. SaaS Gross Margin Benchmarks — Research Report

---

## 1. The Primary Source of the 50-60% Claim: a16z (2020)

**PRIMARY SOURCE — VERIFIED**

**Article:** "The New Business of AI (and How It's Different From Traditional Software)"
**Authors:** Martin Casado and Matt Bornstein
**Publisher:** Andreessen Horowitz (a16z)
**Date:** February 16, 2020
**URL:** https://a16z.com/the-new-business-of-ai-and-how-its-different-from-traditional-software/

**Verbatim passage (confirmed by direct page fetch):**

> "gross margins often in the 50-60% range – well below the 60-80%+ benchmark for comparable SaaS businesses"

Additional verbatim from the same article:

> "25% or more of revenue that AI companies often spend on cloud resources"

> "up to 10-15% of revenue on this process" (referring to data labeling)

> "it's not clear that any amount of long-term product or go-to-market optimization can completely solve the issue"

**This is the origin article for the 50-60% figure.** The SaaS benchmark they compared against was **60-80%+**, not 80%+. The 80%+ number came later in popularized retellings.

---

## 2. a16z "Navigating the High Cost of AI Compute" (2023)

**PRIMARY SOURCE — VERIFIED**

**Article:** "Navigating the High Cost of AI Compute"
**Authors:** Guido Appenzeller, Matt Bornstein, Martin Casado
**Publisher:** a16z
**Date:** April 27, 2023
**URL:** https://a16z.com/navigating-the-high-cost-of-ai-compute/

**Content confirmed by direct page fetch:** This article is about infrastructure costs, not gross margins directly. It focuses on GPU supply constraints, training costs (GPT-3 estimate: $560,000 per run), and inference costs ($0.0002–$0.0014 per 1,000 tokens). It does not contain new gross margin percentage benchmarks. The 50-60% figure does not appear here.

**Key verbatim:**

> "we've seen many companies spend more than 80% of their total capital raised on compute resources!"

### 6a. "State of AI: Bi-Annual Snapshot" (January 2026, December 2025 data)

**Publisher:** ICONIQ Venture & Growth Analytics

## OpenAI Financials (Bonus — Publicly Cited)

From search triangulation (multiple sources citing The Information/Wheresyoured.at leaks):

- **OpenAI actual gross margin in 2025: 33%** (down from 40% in 2024; missed internal target of 46%)
- **Inference costs 2025: $8.4B**
- **"Compute margin" (revenue minus server costs only): 70% as of October 2025** — this is what the 70% figure refers to; it is *not* a standard gross margin

---

## Extra Findings (Not Asked, But Material)

**1. Margin improvement trajectory is real but slow.** The ICONIQ data (41% → 45% → 52% projected) and Bessemer's distinction between Supernovas (25%) and Shooting Stars (60%) both point to a wide dispersion, not a single number. The 50-60% figure is a midpoint across a bimodal distribution.

**2. Inference cost deflation is happening but companies aren't capturing it.** The jeffbrokaw.com article cited MIT FutureTech (November 2025): "5-10x cost decline vs. 3-18x frontier price rise" — meaning the inference cost per token is dropping, but frontier model prices are rising, so margin expansion is not automatic.

**3. The a16z 2020 article compared AI to SaaS at 60-80%+, not 80-90%.** The more aggressive 80-90% ceiling that appears in Bessemer's February 2026 playbook reflects how the SaaS benchmark shifted upward over the 2020–2024 period as public SaaS margins improved.

**4. "Compute margin" vs. gross margin is a definitional trap.** OpenAI's 70% "compute margin" (revenue minus server costs) is frequently misquoted as a gross margin. True gross margin including all COGS (support, human-in-the-loop, etc.) was 33% in 2025.

**5. Bessemer's typology matters for citations.** "Supernovas" (~25% gross margin) are fast-scaling, distribution-focused, often negative-margin in year 1. "Shooting Stars" (~60% margin) are capital-efficient, PMF-proven. Quoting "Bessemer says 25% margins" without this distinction is misleading.

## Sorulmadı ama önemli (§5.5 dökümü)

- **Rakamlar kaynaklar arasında 5-10 kat sapıyor.** LinkedIn CPM: Refine Labs $42.29, Metadata $69.49, Refine Labs 2023 $50, bloglar $31–$38. LinkedIn CPL: Metadata $201, B2B House $125, First Page Sage $310, Sopro $408. **Tek bir "LinkedIn CPL'i şudur" cümlesi kurulamaz** — hangi objective, hangi hedefleme darlığı, hangi lead tanımı belirlemiş. Dışarı bir sayı söyleyeceksen aralık + kaynak + dönem ile söyle.
- **Trend nettir ve tüm kaynaklarda aynı yöne bakıyor:** LinkedIn CPC 2026 Q1'de YoY **−%21.8**, CPM **−%13.7** düşmüş (Refine Labs). Yani "LinkedIn her yıl pahalılaşıyor" anlatısı 2026 verisiyle çelişiyor. Meta CPC de −%22.6. Reddit ise ters yönde patlamış (CPC +%67.3) — Reddit B2B'de artık ucuz kaçış değil.
- **Attribution modeli sonucu belirliyor.** Dreamdata LinkedIn'i %121 ROAS ile zirveye koyuyor (data-driven attribution, closed-won). Metadata aynı LinkedIn'i Facebook'tan daha kötü influenced-pipeline ROI'siyle gösteriyor (202 vs 266). İki rapor da "ölçtük" diyor — model farkı. **Bir platform karşılaştırmasını attribution modelini söylemeden aktarmak yanıltıcı.**
- **Ölçüm penceresi tuzağı:** HockeyStack'e göre pipeline ROI 3. çeyrekte 6.01x'e çıkıyor. Yani 90 günlük pencereyle bakan her benchmark (Metadata'nın Haz-Ağu 2024 penceresi dahil) LinkedIn'i sistematik olarak düşük gösterir.
- **Veri tazeliği:** Metodolojisi açıklanan tek büyük çalışma (Refine Labs $8.2M/308M impression) **2023 verisi**. En taze rakamlar (Refine Labs Q1 2026) ise örneklem sayısını açıklamıyor. Yani "hem taze hem örneklemi açık" LinkedIn benchmark'ı 2026 Ağustos itibarıyla **YOK**.
- **DOĞRULANMADI** — "LinkedIn video envanteri 2025'te +%74" ve "LinkedIn B2B reklam bütçelerinin %39'unu alıyor" (Dreamdata %41 diyor — iki kaynak çelişiyor).

## Rapor 5

# LLM API Cost Control — Primary-Source Findings (fetched 18 Aug 2026)

**Note up front:** the live Anthropic docs show a model lineup (Opus 5, Sonnet 5, Fable 5) newer than any cached table. All numbers below are from the live docs, not memory.

---

## 1. Output caps (`max_tokens` / `max_output_tokens`)

### Anthropic — Messages API
`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/messages`

`max_tokens: number` — listed **without the `optional` marker** that every optional param in that doc carries (e.g. `thinking: optional ThinkingConfigParam`), so by the doc's own convention it is **required**. Exact text:

> "The maximum number of tokens to generate before stopping. Note that our models may stop *before* reaching this maximum. This parameter only specifies the absolute maximum number of tokens to generate. Set to `0` to populate the prompt cache without generating a response. Different models have different maximum values for this parameter."

`max_tokens: 0` is a documented cache-pre-warm mode (no generation). `budget_tokens` (extended thinking, where supported) "Must be ≥1024 and less than `max_tokens`."

**Per-model max output** — `https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/overview`

| Model | API ID | Context | Max output |
|---|---|---|---|
| Claude Fable 5 | `claude-fable-5` | 1M | **128k** |
| Claude Opus 5 | `claude-opus-5` | 1M | **128k** |
| Claude Sonnet 5 | `claude-sonnet-5` | 1M | **128k** |
| Claude Haiku 4.5 | `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` | 200k | **64k** |
| Opus 4.8 / 4.7 / 4.6 | `claude-opus-4-8/-4-7/-4-6` | 1M | 128k |
| Sonnet 4.6 | `claude-sonnet-4-6` | 1M | 128k |
| Sonnet 4.5 / Opus 4.5 | dated IDs | 200k | 64k |

Doc note, verbatim: *"The Max output values in the table apply to the synchronous Messages API. On the Message Batches API, Claude Opus 5, Opus 4.8, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.6, Sonnet 5, and Sonnet 4.6 support up to 300k output tokens by using the `output-300k-2026-03-24` beta header."*

Live capability lookup: `GET /v1/models/{id}` returns `max_input_tokens`, `max_tokens`, `capabilities` (`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/models/list`).

**`stop_reason` values** (all 7, from the same reference): `end_turn`, `max_tokens`, `stop_sequence`, `tool_use`, `pause_turn`, `refusal`, `model_context_window_exceeded`.

### OpenAI
Source: official OpenAPI spec `https://github.com/openai/openai-openapi` (`openapi.yaml`), plus `https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/...` (platform.openai.com 301-redirects there now).

- **Responses API — `max_output_tokens`**: `type: integer`, `minimum: 16`, nullable, **optional**. Description: *"An upper bound for the number of tokens that can be generated for a response, including visible output tokens and reasoning tokens."*
- **Chat Completions — `max_completion_tokens`**: integer, nullable. *"An upper bound for the number of tokens that can be generated for a completion, including visible output tokens and reasoning tokens."*
- **Chat Completions — `max_tokens`**: marked `deprecated: true` in the spec. Verbatim: *"This value is now deprecated in favor of `max_completion_tokens`, and is not compatible with o-series models."*
- **Realtime `max_output_tokens`**: integer 1–4096, or `"inf"`; *"Defaults to `inf`."*

**Per-model max output** (`https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/<id>`):

| Model | Context | Max output |
|---|---|---|
| gpt-5.2 | 400,000 | **128,000** |
| gpt-5.1 | 400,000 | 128,000 |
| gpt-5 | 400,000 | 128,000 |
| gpt-5-mini | 400,000 | 128,000 |
| gpt-5.2-codex | 400,000 | 128,000 |
| o3 | 200,000 | **100,000** |

## 2. Prompt caching

### Anthropic
`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing#prompt-caching` and `https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching`

| Cache operation | Multiplier | Duration |
|---|---|---|
| 5-minute cache write | **1.25x** base input price | 5 min |
| 1-hour cache write | **2x** base input price | 1 hour |
| Cache read (hit) | **0.1x** base input price | same as preceding write |

Doc's own break-even statement: *"a cache hit costs 10% of the standard input price, which means caching pays off after one cache read for the 5-minute duration (1.25x write), or after two cache reads for the 1-hour duration (2x write)."* And: *"These multipliers stack with other pricing modifiers, including the Batch API discount and data residency."*

**Minimum cacheable prompt length** (per model, from the caching doc):
- **512 tokens** — Opus 5, Fable 5, Mythos 5
- **1,024 tokens** — Opus 4.8, Sonnet 5, Sonnet 4.6, Sonnet 4.5, Haiku 4.5
- **2,048 tokens** — Mythos Preview, Opus 4.7, Haiku 3.5
- **4,096 tokens** — Opus 4.6, Opus 4.5

Verbatim: *"Shorter prompts cannot be cached, even if marked with `cache_control`. Any requests to cache fewer than this number of tokens will be processed without caching, and no error is returned."* — silent failure, no error.

**`cache_control` usage:** `{"type": "ephemeral"}` = 5m default; `{"type": "ephemeral", "ttl": "1h"}` = 1h. **Max 4 explicit breakpoints per request.** Two modes: automatic (single top-level `cache_control`) or explicit per-block. Lookback window: **20 blocks max** per breakpoint.

TTL clock, verbatim: *"The lifetime is measured from the **start of the request** that writes or reads the cache entry, not from the end of its response... if a response takes 4 minutes to stream, a follow-up request that reuses the same cached prefix must start within about 1 minute of that response completing."* Refresh on use is free.

Dollar examples: Opus 5 $5 base → $6.25 (5m write) / $10 (1h write) / **$0.50** (hit). Fable 5 $10 → $12.50 / $20 / $1. Sonnet 5 $2 → $2.50 / $4 / $0.20. Haiku 4.5 $1 → $1.25 / $2 / $0.10.

### OpenAI
`https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching`

- **Automatic**: *"Prompt Caching works automatically for eligible requests, with no code changes required."*
- **Discount**: *"Cached input tokens are billed at 0.1× the uncached input token rate"* — i.e. **90% off**. Same 0.1x multiplier as Anthropic's cache read.
- **Minimum prefix**: *"By default, caching is enabled automatically for prompts that are 1,024 tokens or longer."*
- **Lifetime**: GPT-5.6+ — *"The 30-minute lifetime begins when the prefix is written and refreshes whenever the prefix is reused."* Earlier models — *"cached prefixes generally remain active for 5 to 10 minutes of inactivity, up to a maximum of one hour"* (in-memory), or *"up to a maximum of 24 hours"* (extended retention).
- **Params**: `prompt_cache_key` (routing to a shared cache), `prompt_cache_retention` (`"in_memory"` | `"24h"`, earlier models), `prompt_cache_options.ttl` (only `"30m"` on GPT-5.6+), `prompt_cache_options.mode` (`"implicit"` | `"explicit"`), `prompt_cache_breakpoint`.

**Key structural difference:** OpenAI's is automatic and read-discount-only — there is **no cache-write premium**. Anthropic's is opt-in and charges 1.25x/2x to write.

---

## 3. Batch APIs

### Anthropic Message Batches
`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/batch-processing`

- **Discount: 50%.** Verbatim: *"All usage is charged at 50% of the standard API prices"* and *"offers a 50% discount on all usage compared to standard API prices. This applies to input tokens, output tokens, and any special tokens."*
- **Size:** *"A Message Batch is limited to either 100,000 Message requests or 256 MB in size, whichever is reached first."*
- **Turnaround:** *"most batches completing within 1 hour. You can access batch results when all messages have completed or after 24 hours, whichever comes first. Batches expire if processing does not complete within 24 hours."* Expired requests: *"You will not be billed for these requests."*
- **Results retention:** 29 days from `created_at`.
- **`max_tokens` constraint:** *"Each batched request must have `max_tokens` of at least `1`. `max_tokens: 0` (cache pre-warming) is not supported inside a batch."*
- **Cache interaction (asked for explicitly):** *"The pricing discounts from prompt caching and Message Batches can stack... However, because batch requests are processed asynchronously and concurrently, cache hits are provided on a best-effort basis. Users typically experience cache hit rates ranging from **30% to 98%**, depending on their traffic patterns."* Doc recommends the 1-hour TTL for batches: *"Because batches can take longer than 5 minutes to process, consider using the 1-hour cache duration."*
- Endpoints: `POST /v1/messages/batches`, `DELETE /v1/messages/batches/{batch_id}`.

### OpenAI Batch
`https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/batch`

- **Discount: 50%** — *"50% cost discount compared to synchronous APIs"*.
- **Window:** *"for now, the completion window can only be set to `24h`"* — single option.
- **Limits:** *"up to 50,000 requests"* per batch; *"input file can be up to 200 MB in size"*; *"up to 2,000 batches per hour"*; `/v1/embeddings` batches capped at 50,000 inputs.
- **Results retention:** *"The output file will automatically be deleted 30 days after the batch is complete."*

Anthropic allows 2x the requests (100k vs 50k) but a smaller payload cap by MB definition (256 MB body vs 200 MB file).

---

## 4. Runaway agent loops — official guidance

### Anthropic "Building effective agents" (engineering post)
`https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/building-effective-agents` — the only relevant sentence, verbatim:

> "Agents can then pause for human feedback at checkpoints or when encountering blockers. The task often terminates upon completion, but **it's also common to include stopping conditions (such as a maximum number of iterations) to maintain control**."

And on cost: *"The autonomous nature of agents means higher costs, and the potential for compounding errors. We recommend extensive testing in sandboxed environments, along with the appropriate guardrails."* No number is given anywhere in the post.

### Claude Agent SDK — `maxTurns` / `max_turns` exists, **default is `undefined` / `None` (unbounded)**
`https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript` and `.../python`

| SDK | Param | Type | Default | Doc description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TypeScript | `maxTurns` | `number` | **`undefined`** | "Maximum agentic turns (tool-use round trips)" |
| Python | `max_turns` | `int \| None` | **`None`** | "Maximum agentic turns (tool-use round trips)" |

This is the load-bearing finding for cost control: **the Claude Agent SDK ships with no turn limit by default.** You must set it.

Two adjacent guards in the same table, also defaulting to unset:
- `maxBudgetUsd` / `max_budget_usd` — *"Stop the query when the client-side cost estimate reaches this USD value. Compared against the same estimate as `total_cost_usd`."* A **dollar-denominated** loop guard, which is arguably the better control than turn count.
- Subagent-level `maxTurns` exists on `AgentDefinition` — "Maximum number of agentic turns (API round-trips) before stopping".

Termination is observable: `SDKResultMessage.subtype` ∈ `"success"`, `"error_during_execution"`, **`"error_max_turns"`**, **`"error_max_budget_usd"`**, `"error_max_structured_output_retries"`; and `terminal_reason` ∈ `"completed"`, `"max_turns"`, `"api_error"`, `"aborted_streaming"`, `"aborted_tools"`.

### Anthropic stop_reason as a loop signal
`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/handling-stop-reasons` — `pause_turn`: *"A server-tool loop reached its iteration limit. Send the assistant content back to continue."* This is a server-side iteration cap on server tools; blindly re-sending on `pause_turn` without your own counter is itself an unbounded-loop risk.

### OpenAI Agents SDK — `max_turns`, **default 10**
Read from official source, since `openai.github.io` was network-blocked:

- Python: `https://github.com/openai/openai-agents-python/blob/main/src/agents/run_config.py` → **`DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 10`**. `Runner.run(..., max_turns: int | None = DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS, ...)`; passing `None` disables the limit.
- Docstring, verbatim: *"If the max_turns is exceeded, a MaxTurnsExceeded exception is raised unless handled."*
- Exception class, `src/agents/exceptions.py`: `class MaxTurnsExceeded(AgentsException)` — *"Exception raised when the maximum number of turns is exceeded."* (Python name is `MaxTurnsExceeded`, **not** `MaxTurnsExceededError`.)
- JS/TS: `https://github.com/openai/openai-agents-js/blob/main/packages/agents-core/src/runner/constants.ts` → **`export const DEFAULT_MAX_TURNS = 10;`**. Option is `maxTurns?: number | null`. JS class name is `MaxTurnsExceededError`.

**Contrast worth stating plainly:** OpenAI Agents SDK is bounded-by-default at 10 turns; Claude Agent SDK is unbounded by default but gives you a USD-denominated stop that OpenAI does not.

### OpenAI Responses API — `max_tool_calls`
From `openapi.yaml`: *"The maximum number of total calls to built-in tools that can be processed in a response. This maximum number applies across all built-in tool calls, not per individual tool. Any further attempts to call a tool by the model will be ignored."* Integer or null. This is a **per-request** server-side tool-call cap with no Anthropic equivalent.

---

## 5. Token counting before sending

### Anthropic — `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens`
`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/token-counting` · reference `https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/messages-count-tokens`

- Full URL: `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages/count_tokens`. SDK: `client.messages.count_tokens(...)` / `.countTokens(...)`. Response: `{ "input_tokens": 14 }`.
- **Free.** Verbatim: *"Token counting is **free to use** but subject to requests per minute rate limits based on your usage tier."*
- **Rate limits:** Start **2,000 RPM**, Build **4,000 RPM**, Scale **8,000 RPM**. *"Token counting and message creation have separate and independent rate limits. Usage of one does not count against the limits of the other."*
- Accepts the same inputs as message creation: system, tools, images, PDFs, thinking blocks.
- **It is an estimate**, not exact: *"The token count is an estimate. In some cases, the actual number of input tokens used when creating a message might differ by a small amount."*
- **Does not use caching**: *"No, token counting provides an estimate without using caching logic. Although you may provide `cache_control` blocks in your token counting request, prompt caching only occurs during actual message creation."* → you cannot use it to predict cache hits.
- Model-specific: *"Claude 4.7 and later models and Claude Mythos Preview use a newer tokenizer. The same input text produces approximately 30 percent more tokens than on earlier models."* Recount per target model; don't reuse counts across generations.
- Available on Claude API, Claude Platform on AWS, Bedrock, Google Cloud, Microsoft Foundry.

### OpenAI
**There is no OpenAI token-counting API endpoint.** No such path exists in the official OpenAPI spec. The official offering is the client-side `tiktoken` library (`https://github.com/openai/tiktoken`) — local, free, but it does not account for tool/system scaffolding tokens the way Anthropic's endpoint does. Anthropic's docs explicitly warn against using `tiktoken` for Claude. **DOĞRULANMADI:** I did not fetch a doc page where OpenAI states outright "there is no token counting endpoint" — this is an absence-of-evidence conclusion from the OpenAPI spec, which is strong but negative evidence.

---

## DÖKÜM — things I found that you didn't ask about

**Cost-control levers nobody listed:**
1. **`maxBudgetUsd` / `max_budget_usd`** in the Claude Agent SDK — a hard USD ceiling on a query. This is a more direct cost control than any token cap and is probably the single most useful finding here for a cost-control product.
2. **`output-300k-2026-03-24` beta header** — raises batch `max_tokens` to 300,000 on Opus 5/4.8/4.7/4.6 and Sonnet 5/4.6. *"A single 300k-token generation can take over an hour to complete, so plan your batch submissions with the 24-hour processing window in mind."* Standard 50% batch pricing applies.
3. **`max_tokens: 0` cache pre-warming** — documented, free of output tokens, blocked inside batches.
4. **Data residency multiplier:** `inference_geo: "us"` = **1.1x on everything** (input, output, cache writes, cache reads) on Claude 4.6+. Silent 10% cost increase. Earlier models 400 on the param.
5. **Fast mode:** Opus 5 / Opus 4.8 at `$10 in / $50 out` per MTok (2x standard). Not available with the Batch API. Errors on Opus 4.7; on Opus 4.6 it *silently runs at standard speed and standard price*.
6. **Regional/multi-region endpoints on Bedrock and Google Cloud carry a 10% premium** over global endpoints.
7. **Tool-use system prompt overhead is per-model and large:** Opus 4.7 costs **675 tokens** (`auto`/`none`) or **804** (`any`/`tool`) just to have tools attached; Opus 5 is only 286/406. That's a ~2.4x hidden per-request cost difference between two Opus generations. Bash tool adds 325 (Opus 5/4.8/4.7) or 244 (older); text editor +700; computer use +735 plus 466–499 system tokens.
8. **Server-tool pricing:** web search **$10 per 1,000 searches**; web fetch **free** beyond tokens; code execution **1,550 free container-hours/month per org**, then **$0.05/hour/container**, 5-minute minimum billing, and *billed even if the tool isn't called* when files are attached.
9. **Claude Managed Agents** bills a separate **$0.08 per session-hour** on top of tokens, metered only while status is `running`. Batch discount and cloud-platform pricing explicitly do **not** apply to it.
10. **Sonnet 5 is $2/$10 permanently** — the doc says the scheduled 1 Sep 2026 increase to $3/$15 "will not occur." Sonnet 5 is now cheaper than Sonnet 4.6 ($3/$15) while having a bigger max output. Anyone still on Sonnet 4.6 is overpaying by 50%.
11. **OpenAI Batch is not the only OpenAI queue limit** — there are per-model "queued prompt token" caps only visible on the Platform Settings page, not in docs.

**What I could NOT access:**
- `openai.github.io/openai-agents-python/*` and `openai.github.io/openai-agents-js/*` — WebFetch blocked ("Unable to verify if domain is safe"). I worked around this by reading the official GitHub source, which is arguably a stronger primary source, but I did not read the prose docs pages, so any narrative guidance there about *when* to change `max_turns` is unseen.
- `code.claude.com` via WebFetch — blocked the same way; retrieved via `curl` on the `.md` endpoints instead.
- `www.anthropic.com` via WebFetch — blocked; retrieved the Building Effective Agents post via `curl` + HTML strip. Diagrams/images in that post were not readable.
- I did **not** verify OpenAI's per-model max-output table for gpt-4.1, gpt-4o, o4-mini, or the gpt-5.6 line mentioned in the caching doc. The caching doc references **GPT-5.6** with a distinct 30-minute cache TTL — I did not fetch a gpt-5.6 model page, so its context/output limits are unknown to me.
- OpenAI's actual per-model *prices* — I did not fetch the pricing page, so no OpenAI $/MTok figures appear above.
- **DOĞRULANMADI:** whether Anthropic's `max_tokens` is formally flagged `required` in the OpenAPI schema. I inferred it from the docs' optional-marker convention (every optional param says "optional"; `max_tokens` does not). Strong but inferential.
- The `output-300k` beta and the 30%-more-tokens tokenizer change interact in ways I did not test — a 300k-token Fable 5 output is not comparable to 300k on Sonnet 4.6.

## Rapor 6

## Findings

### 1. Sourcegraph Cody — squeezed, then the company literally split in two
- **June 25, 2025**: Sourcegraph stopped accepting new Cody Free / Cody Pro signups. Announcement: https://sourcegraph.com/blog/changes-to-cody-free-pro-and-enterprise-starter-plans
- **July 23, 2025**: Cody Free, Cody Pro, and Cody in Enterprise Starter plans **terminated**. Sourcegraph's own post on X: https://x.com/Sourcegraph/status/1937926440701567166 — *"We're deprecating Cody Free, Cody Pro, and Cody in Starter plans on July 23. Cody Enterprise plans are unaffected."*
- **Did Cody shut down?** Not fully. Cody survives as an **enterprise-only** product (~$59/user/mo, annual). The consumer/prosumer funnel — the part that competed head-on with Claude Code and Copilot — was killed outright. Free/Pro users were redirected to **Amp**.
- **December 2, 2025**: Sourcegraph and Amp split into **two independent companies**. Quinn Slack + Beyang Liu left to found **Amp Inc.** (an independent research lab); **Dan Adler** became CEO of Sourcegraph. Boards (Craft, Redpoint, Sequoia, Goldcrest, a16z) sit on both.
  - Official: https://sourcegraph.com/blog/why-sourcegraph-and-amp-are-becoming-independent-companies
  - Slack's thread: https://x.com/sqs/status/1995923843391574436 — *"@Sourcegraph will be totally focused on code search and code understanding for big codebases, in a world where AI is writing and searching 1000x more code. @AmpCode is becoming Amp Inc., an independent research lab."*
  - Joint quote: *"the two products have totally different distribution engines and target audiences"*; Sourcegraph is *"a mission-critical piece of AI infrastructure software… for enterprises,"* while *"Amp needs to remain on the frontier."*
- **Amp status 2026**: alive and shipping fast. Subscriptions at $20/$200. Notably launched **Amp Free** — an **ad-supported** coding agent (ads in the editor/CLI, user data subsidizes inference). That is a real signal about price pressure from Claude Code/Copilot. Changelog: https://ampcode.com/chronicle · ad model writeup: https://tessl.io/blog/amp-s-new-business-model-ad-supported-ai-coding/
- Sourcegraph headcount ~184 (2026) vs ~194 (2024); revenue reported ~$50M (2025) vs $31M (2024). **UNVERIFIED** — these come from Built In / getlatka aggregators, not Sourcegraph filings.

### 2. Aider (Paul Gauthier) — quietly starved, not dead
Hard numbers pulled directly from GitHub API + PyPI (primary):
- **48,284 stars**, not archived, **1,817 open issues**.
- **Last GitHub release: v0.86.0, 2025-08-09.** Zero tagged releases since.
- PyPI: `0.86.1` on 2025-08-13, then a **six-month gap** to `0.86.2` on **2026-02-12** — the current latest as of Aug 2026.
- **Last commit: 2026-05-22** (merging a community PR adding Claude Opus 4.5 to the model list). **82 commits total in all of 2026** vs a 2025 cadence of multiple releases per month (v0.72 Jan 20 2025 → v0.86 Aug 2025 = 14 minor releases in 7 months).
- Read: cadence collapsed almost exactly when Claude Code went GA. Gauthier is still merging occasional PRs but is not driving it.
- **No public statement from Gauthier about Claude Code competition could be found.** Tag: **UNVERIFIED / not found.** He has not published a "we're winding down" post; there is no such post to cite.
- Repo: https://github.com/Aider-AI/aider · https://pypi.org/project/aider-chat/

### 3. Cline — alive, growing, not squeezed (yet)
- **$27M Series A, July 2025**, led by Emergence Capital, ~$110M post-money. Total raised ~$32M. https://www.crunchbase.com/funding_round/cline-4914-series-a--f99dd09b
- Install base: 1M (Mar 2025) → 2.7M (Jul 2025) → 3.8M (Nov 2025) → **5M (Jan 2026)**. ~**$5M ARR (Aug 2025)**. Source: https://sacra.com/c/cline/ (Sacra is a paid-research aggregator, reasonably reliable but **not primary**).
- Pivot pattern is defensive-but-smart: BYO-key, **inference passed through at cost with zero markup**, monetizing via enterprise (SSO/SLA/support). **CLI 2.0 in Feb 2026** (TUI, headless, JSON output, CI/CD, parallel agents) — i.e. rebuilt itself into a Claude Code shape. **Embeddable SDK, May 2026.**
- **No Series B found as of Aug 2026.** Blog: https://cline.bot/blog/archive

### 4. Continue.dev — DEAD. Acqui-hired by Cursor.
- Raised only **$5.1M** (Seed, Feb 26 2025).
- Mid-2025 pivot to "Continuous AI" — async agents on every PR — an explicit retreat from the autocomplete/chat lane Copilot and Claude Code took.
- **~June 16, 2026**: continue.dev homepage quietly replaced with a note that Cursor bought them. **June 18, 2026**: Cursor announced. **June 19, 2026**: final release `v2.0.0-vscode`; repo went **read-only**. **July 15, 2026**: cloud data deleted.
- Terms undisclosed; effectively an acqui-hire with product shutdown.
- https://thenewstack.io/cursor-acquires-continue-coding/

### 5. Tabnine — the oldest player, sold off
- Founded 2013 as Codota (Dror Weiss / Prof. Eran Yahav); acquired Tabnine 2019 and took the name. Predates Copilot by years.
- **2024 restructuring: ~15 of ~80 employees cut (~18%)**, mostly marketing in Israel/US.
- **July 30, 2026: acquired by Tricentis** (Insight Partners-backed test-automation company). **Terms undisclosed**; Calcalist estimates "tens of millions of dollars" — a soft outcome given Tabnine had raised ~$60M+.
- The pivot is telling: Tabnine is not being kept as a coding assistant. Tricentis is absorbing its **Enterprise Context Engine** into an "Agentic Quality Engineering Platform" — i.e. the coding-assistant product was not the asset.
- https://www.tricentis.com/news/tricentis-acquires-tabnine · https://www.tabnine.com/blog/a-new-chapter-for-tabnine/ · https://www.calcalistech.com/ctechnews/article/1o1nf7vd3

### 6. Kite — the canonical postmortem
- **Exact URL: `https://www.kite.com/blog/product/kite-is-saying-farewell/`**
- Title: **"Kite is saying farewell and open-sourcing its code"**
- **Date: November 2022.** HN submission was **November 20, 2022** (1083 points): https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=33685209. Note: some secondary sources claim Nov 16 2021 — that is **wrong**; all contemporaneous coverage (Slashdot 2022-11-22, devclass 2022-11-21, Gigazine 2022-11-21, TechCrunch 2022-12-10) is November 2022.
- Key quotes from Adam Smith:
  - *"We failed to deliver our vision of AI-assisted programming because we were 10+ years too early to market, i.e., the tech is not ready yet."*
  - *"Our product did not monetize, and it took too long to figure that out."*
  - *"Our diagnosis is that individual developers do not pay for tools."* — engineering managers only pay for *discrete new capabilities*; making devs **18% faster** at writing code did not resonate.
  - They built "the most-advanced AI for helping developers" at the time but it *"fell short of the 10× improvement required to break through."*
- **~$17.3M raised** (YC, Spark Capital, others); **~500,000 developers** using it who would not pay.
- Timing matters for your thesis: **GitHub Copilot went GA June 21, 2022. Kite folded 5 months later.**
- https://techcrunch.com/2022/12/10/with-kites-demise-can-generative-ai-for-code-succeed/ · https://devclass.com/2022/11/21/kite-ai-coding-pulled-down-to-earth-because-our-500k-developers-would-not-pay-to-use-it-now-open-source/

### 7. Replit — the counter-example: not squeezed, exploded
- **Ghostwriter → "Replit AI"** rebrand, **November 2024**. Replit's own reasoning: *the Ghostwriter name "unintentionally implied that AI was a side feature."* https://blog.replit.com/how-we-name-things
- **Replit Agent** launched Sept 2024; **effort-based pricing** replaced flat per-checkpoint pricing (simple change <$0.25, complex tasks bundled and cost more) — drew public backlash. https://replit.com/blog/effort-based-pricing
- ARR: **$300M (end 2025) → $525M (April 2026)**, ~**+1,775% YoY**. **50M users.**
- **March 2026: $400M raise at $9B valuation** (Georgian, a16z, Coatue, 1789, QIA; also Shaq and Jared Leto). Masad became a billionaire (~$2B, Forbes). Target: **$1B ARR by end of 2026.**
- Primary: https://replit.com/news/funding-announcement · https://www.forbes.com/sites/richardnieva/2026/03/11/meet-the-9-billion-ai-company-reimagining-vibe-coding-replit-amjad-masad/
- Lesson for the thesis: Replit survived by **not** being a coding assistant — it owns hosting/deploy/compute, which Claude Code doesn't.

### 8. GitHub Copilot — the free tier as a weapon
- **Dec 18, 2024: Copilot Free launched** in VS Code — **2,000 completions + 50 chat messages/month**, free with a personal GitHub account, choice of **Claude 3.5 Sonnet or GPT-4o**. Announced alongside **150M developers** on GitHub. https://github.blog/changelog/2024-12-18-announcing-github-copilot-free/ · https://github.blog/news-insights/product-news/github-copilot-in-vscode-free/
- **Dec 19, 2024**: free tier extended to JetBrains IDEs.
- **Feb 6, 2025: agent mode** announced (VS Code), plus GA of Copilot Edits.
- **Copilot Workspace: launched April 2024 (technical preview) → sunset May 30, 2025.** Killed as a standalone; its sub-agent architecture / issue-to-PR / async execution model was folded into Copilot coding agent. Discussion thread: https://github.com/orgs/community/discussions/145254
- **May 19, 2025**: Copilot coding agent public preview. **June 24, 2025**: extended to Copilot Business. **Sept 25, 2025: GA for all paid subscribers.** https://github.blog/changelog/2025-09-25-copilot-coding-agent-is-now-generally-available/
- **Feb 25, 2026: GitHub Copilot CLI GA** — the direct Claude Code answer. https://github.blog/changelog/2026-02-25-github-copilot-cli-is-now-generally-available/
- Other sunsets in the wake: **Copilot knowledge bases** (deprecated Nov 1, 2025, replaced by Copilot Spaces — https://github.blog/changelog/2025-08-20-sunset-notice-copilot-knowledge-bases/) and **GitHub App-based Copilot Extensions** (Sept 24, 2025 — https://github.blog/changelog/2025-09-24-deprecate-github-copilot-extensions-github-apps/). Note the second one: GitHub killed the third-party extension surface it had invited startups to build on.

### Startups that died citing platform competition
- **Phind** (AI answer engine for developers): **shut down January 16, 2026, with no sunset period**, roughly a month after raising $10M. Users had until Jan 30 to export; Pro subscribers got prorated refunds. Cited cause: foundation-model providers adding native web search + Copilot/LLMs getting better at coding erased its differentiation; traffic down ~91% from peak. Sources are secondary aggregators — **the shutdown itself is well-attested but the "91% decline" figure is UNVERIFIED.**
- **CodeWhisper** shut down July 2025; **CodeParrot** wound down citing Copilot/Replit expansion. Both **UNVERIFIED** — only found on aggregator/content-farm pages, no primary announcement located.
- **Ryze** (founder Bodnar) publicly said *"Claude just killed our startup"* after Anthropic shipped competing features; close rate fell **70% → 20%**; pivoting to complex AI workflows for large ad agencies. Note: this is **ad-ops, not a coding tool** — use carefully. https://finance.yahoo.com/news/claude-just-killed-startup-sf-173105543.html

## Dump — things I found that you didn't ask for, and things I couldn't confirm

**Unasked but load-bearing:**
- **Microsoft cancelled most internal Claude Code licenses**, targeting **June 30, 2026** (fiscal year end), pushing thousands of Experiences & Devices engineers (Windows, M365, Teams, Outlook, Surface) to GitHub Copilot CLI. Microsoft had only opened Claude Code internally ~Dec 2025; by May 2026 usage was high enough to be seen as internal competition to Copilot CLI and cost-heavy. Devs reportedly unhappy. https://www.windowscentral.com/microsoft/microsoft-cancels-claude-code-licenses-shifting-developers-to-github-copilot-cli-a-move-likely-driven-by-financial-motives · https://www.forbes.com/sites/jonmarkman/2026/06/01/microsoft-ends-claude-code-licenses-as-it-shifts-developers-to-copilot/ — This is a *reverse* data point: the platform owner squeezing out Claude Code, not the other way round. It complicates a simple "Anthropic eats everyone" narrative.
- **SpaceX acquired Anysphere/Cursor for $60B all-stock**, announced **June 16, 2026** (CNBC: https://www.cnbc.com/2026/06/16/spacex-spcx-cursor-acquisition-ipo.html), closed **August 14, 2026**, folded into a "SpaceXAI" unit; ~391M SpaceX Class A shares issued; follows SpaceX's Feb 2026 merger with xAI. Largest startup acquisition on record. This happened in the *same week* Cursor bought Continue. I did not expect this and flag it as very high-salience context for any pitch you make about this market — but I could not fetch CNBC directly, so treat the specifics as **partially verified via search snippets only**.
- **Amp's ad-supported free tier** is the single most interesting economic signal in this whole sweep: a Sequoia/a16z-backed frontier agent lab concluded it cannot charge for the free tier and is selling attention instead.
- Consolidation list surfaced repeatedly: **Windsurf, Supermaven, Continue, Base44, Fine.dev** all acquired; **Phind** shut down. I only verified Continue and Tabnine in this session.

**Could not verify / blocked:**
- WebFetch was **blocked by network policy** for: `sourcegraph.com`, `webflow.sourcegraph.com`, `tabnine.com`, `kite.com`, `github.blog`, `techcrunch.com`, `en.wikipedia.org`, `devclass.com`, `siliconrepublic.com`, and `web.archive.org`. Every claim sourced to those domains above rests on **search-result snippets**, not on me reading the page. The Sourcegraph, Tabnine, and Kite primary blog posts are cited by URL but **I did not read their full text**.
- **No verbatim Quinn Slack quote about Cody's deprecation reason** was found — the deprecation post and the spinout post are separate, and neither publicly says "Claude Code beat us." That causal link is inference, not a quote. Do not attribute it to him.
- **No Paul Gauthier statement about Claude Code** exists that I could find. The Aider decline is documented by commit/release data only.
- **Amp's revenue/ARR/user numbers: not found anywhere.** Amp Inc. shows no disclosed funding. PitchBook/Tracxn have nothing usable.
- **Tabnine acquisition price: undisclosed.** The "tens of millions" figure is Calcalist's estimate, not confirmed.
- **Cline's ARR beyond $5M (Aug 2025) is unknown** — no 2026 figure found.
- Several 2026-dated "review" sites (weavai, aiagentsquare, toolcurrent, intelligenttools, aipedia, bodegaone, checkthat.ai, espressio, serenitiesai) are **SEO content farms**. I used them only for corroboration of dates that appeared in at least one credible source, and I have not cited them as sole evidence for anything above except where tagged UNVERIFIED.

## Rapor 7

# PRODUCT HUNT OY SATIN ALMA — EKONOMİ VE MEKANİK

## 1. SATICI FİYATLARI (oy başına $)

**Bağımsız test — M. Zaib, Medium, 2026, toplam $1,500+ harcama** ([kaynak](https://mohzaib.medium.com/best-product-hunt-upvote-services-in-2026-i-spent-1-500-testing-4-of-them-so-you-dont-have-to-94c92dd51934)). 5 launch, 1'i kontrol grubu:

| Satıcı | Harcama | Oy/$ | Teslim | Sonuç |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kontrol (organik) | $0 | — | — | **87 oy**, top 10 dışı |
| SocialPlug | ~$300 (150 oy) | **$2.00** | anında, ilk saatler | **203 oy**, #8 |
| Upvote.club | minimal (topluluk) | **$1.80** | yavaş, düzensiz | **132 oy**, #14 |
| Signals (signals.sh) | $599.99 (100 oy) | **$6.00** | ~3 saat | **241 oy**, #5 |
| Uprows Hub | $400 (200 oy + 10 yorum) | **$1.75** | gün boyu zamanlanmış | **347 oy**, #3 |

- **socialplug.io** ([servis sayfası](https://www.socialplug.io/services/buy-product-hunt-upvotes)): "Starting from **$2 / Upvote**". Tier tablosu yok, sadece birim fiyat. Max 2,000 oy. Ödeme: Visa/MC/Amex + **Bitcoin, Ethereum, Litecoin, USDT**. Site iddiaları: "10.5M+ upvote teslim edildi", "4.7K+ startup", "100k+ müşteri", "4.8/5, 1000+ yorum". "Upvotes guaranteed to stay" + "gerçek hesaplar" garantisi. Kayıt/login gerekmiyor. Arama snippet'i 50 oy = $100 ve hacimde ~$1.10/oy'a inildiğini söylüyor — **DOĞRULANMADI** (sayfada tier görünmüyor).
- **Signals (signals.sh)**: 20 oy = **$199.99 ($10/oy)**, 200 oy = **$999.99 ($5/oy)**, yorum **$14/adet**. Max kapasite 100 (bir yerde 250 deniyor — çelişkili, **DOĞRULANMADI**). Sipariş ~3 saatte işleniyor, 24 saatte teslim edilmezse tam iade. Hepsi ABD merkezli, geçmişi olan hesaplar + manuel inceleme katmanı. Rakiplerin **3-5 katı** fiyat. (signals.sh domaini fetch'e kapalı, veriler arama snippet'i + Medium testinden.)
- **Uprows Hub (uprowshub.com)**: oy **$1.60'tan** başlıyor. 5 oy = $8 → 300 oy = **$600**. Yorum **$5/adet**. **Sıralama paketi satıyor**: **#5 = $470**, **#4 veya #3 = $800**. Zamanlanmış teslimat sunan tek servis. Ücretsiz deneme: 3 oy + 1 yorum, kart gerekmiyor.
- **Upvote.club** ([sayfa](https://upvote.club/producthunt/buy-producthunt-upvotes)): 5 oy = **$9 ($1.80/oy)**, görev başına **15 oy sert limiti**. Yorum ve takipçi **$0.90'dan**. Puan tabanlı topluluk modeli: gerçek kullanıcılar görev yapar, puan kazanır, kendi launch'ında harcar. Yeni hesaba **13 bedava puan**.
- **smgrowth.net** ([sayfa](https://smgrowth.net/product-hunt-upvotes/)) — ucuz/bot ucu: 50 oy deneme = **$20 ($0.40/oy)**, 100 oy = $20, paketler 15,000+ oy'a kadar, tavan **$2,399**. Yani bulk'ta **~$0.10/oy**. 180 gün "refill" garantisi. 15,000 oy satan bir servis fiyat kırılımını göstermiyor — kalite iddiası inandırıcı değil.
- **Fiverr**: [erikolly gig](https://www.fiverr.com/erikolly/40-product-hunt-followers-or-up-votes-from-usa) — **90 Product Hunt takipçi veya upvote, ABD, $5**, 3-4 saatte teslim → **$0.055/oy**. Fiverr %20 komisyon alıyor. [Fiverr'da 24 Product Hunt servisi](https://www.fiverr.com/gigs/product-hunt) listeleniyor.

**Fiyat aralığı özeti: $0.055/oy (Fiverr) → $0.40 (smgrowth bulk) → $1.60-2.00 (orta segment) → $10 (Signals düşük tier).** 180 kat fark. Fiyat, hesap yaşı/kalitesi ile korele — PH'ın ağırlıklandırma sistemi tam da bunu hedefliyor (§4).

## 2. TELEGRAM / WHATSAPP / SLACK OY TAKAS GRUPLARI

**Indie Hackers "📎Telegram Upvote List for ProductHunt", 9 Şubat 2023** ([kaynak](https://www.indiehackers.com/post/telegram-upvote-list-for-producthunt-23d7bf1232)) — 7 grup listeliyor, **üye sayısı YOK**:
1. t.me/producthunt_heroes
2. t.me/ProductHuntChat
3. t.me/ProductHuntRussia
4. t.me/producthunt007
5. t.me/PHUpvotes
6. t.me/startupconsulting
7. t.me/producthackers

Post'ta mekanik açıklaması yok; sadece "Here is the list of Telegram Groups to get upvotes". Yorumlar "5 ay önce" ve "4 yıl önce" tarihli.

**Diğer bulunanlar:**
- Facebook: **"Product Hunt 🚀 Upvote Promotion 🔥 Exchange Club 🌟"** ([link](https://www.facebook.com/groups/producthunt.exchange/)) — üye sayısı fetch edilemedi (FB login duvarı).
- Telegram @producthunttg: **3,174 abone** — ama bu haber kanalı, takas grubu değil.
- Mekanik (kaynaklarda tarif edildiği kadarıyla): karşılıklı takas — üye kendi launch linkini atar, diğerleri oylar, karşılığında onlarınkini oylar. Upvote.club bunun ürünleştirilmiş hali: puan ekonomisi.
- **PH bilinen vote-swap Telegram/Discord sunucularını izliyor**; bu gruplardan gelen trafik ranking algoritmasında ağır iskonto yiyor.

## 3. MICHIGAN DAILY SORUŞTURMASI

**"Buying prestige on Product Hunt: How tech startups manufacture artificial success"** — Jeffrey Lu (Michigan Daily Data Journalist, lujeff@umich.edu), **7 Ekim 2025** ([URL](https://www.michigandaily.com/web/data/buying-prestige-on-product-hunt-how-tech-startups-manufacture-artificial-success/)).

**Metodoloji:** Bireysel kullanıcıların sahte olup olmadığını ayırt etmeye çalışmak yerine, **ürün bazında oy-zaman (vote vs time) eğrisi** analiz edilmiş. Bulgu: kümülatif oy sayısı **lojistik büyüme** izliyor (launch'ı henüz görmemiş potansiyel oylayıcı oranı hızla düşüyor). Lojistik trendden **saatlik oy kazanma hızı** türetiliyor: beklenen davranış = erken tepe, sonra hızlı sönüm. Bu beklentiden sapan ürünler (gün ortasında ani hız artışı) anomali olarak işaretleniyor.

**Vaka: TwinMind** — LSA freshman Vatsal Shah ve küçük ekibi, 2024 yazından beri geliştirdikleri AI not-alma ürününü **25 Kasım 2024**'te launch etti. PH sayfasına göre **516 upvote, #47** ([PH sayfası](https://www.producthunt.com/products/twinmind)).
- TwinMind CEO'su **Daniel George**, Michigan Daily'ye: launch sonrası **yüzlerce e-posta** aldığını, hepsinin sosyal medya pazarlama operasyonu olduğunu iddia edip para karşılığı oy vaat ettiğini söylüyor.
- **En somut kanıt:** George, satıcılardan birine, TwinMind'ın hemen altındaki ürüne yüzlerce oy üreteceği tehdidinde bulununca, **yaklaşık 1 saat sonra o ürünün oy alma hızı neredeyse ikiye katlandı**. Yani satıcı yeteneğini canlı gösterdi.
- Sonuç cümlesi: TwinMind vakasında sahte oyların farkı yaratıp yaratmadığı kesin söylenemez, ama **sahte oyların herhangi bir günün sıralamasını değiştirebileceği neredeyse kesin**.

**Diğer bulgular:** Başka analizlere göre tahmini bot aktivitesi son yıllarda ciddi arttı ve **hem yorum hem oy sayısında gerçek kullanıcıları geçti**. LLM'lerin hızlı gelişimi kullanıcı manzarasını değiştirdi. Bot teşhis yöntemi olarak: yorumlara "me good LLM" gibi tuhaf ibareler gömme talebi — hiçbir ciddi insanın yazmayacağı bu ifadeler, insan görünen çok sayıda kullanıcıyı bot olarak ifşa etti. PH 2023 başında forumda sorunu kabul etti, sonrasında algoritmik + insan denetim sistemleri ve genişletilmiş kullanıcı doğrulama yayınladı; ayrıca **canlı oy "kesme" (live cutting)** uyguluyor — şüpheli oyları açıklanmayan bir süreçle anında siliyor.

**ERİŞEMEDİĞİM:** Toplam kaç ürün/launch analiz edildiği, veri setinin tarih aralığı, veri kaynağı (scraping mi API mi), anomali eşiği ve kaç ürünün işaretlendiği — **bu sayılar elimde YOK**. michigandaily.com doğrudan fetch'e kapalı (429 → sonra "domain güvenli değil" bloğu), web.archive.org ve r.jina.ai proxy'si de bu ortamda engelli. Yukarıdaki içerik arama motoru snippet'lerinden derlendi — **metodoloji ve TwinMind alıntıları bu nedenle ikinci elden, DOĞRULANMADI.**

## 4. PRODUCT HUNT FORUM THREAD'LERİ

**a) "Vote selling on Product Hunt"** — Mike Kerzhner (PH ekibi), **~5 ay önce (≈Mart 2026)**, **2.5K görüntülenme** ([thread](https://www.producthunt.com/p/producthunt/vote-selling-on-product-hunt)):
- **Ağırlıklı oy sistemi açıkça doğrulandı.** Düşük kaliteli hesap (yeni Gmail, katkı geçmişi yok): **"bu oy 0 puan sayar"**. Doğrulanmış hesap (şirket maili, anlamlı katkı geçmişi): oy "ciddi ağırlık" taşır.
- "We have systems in place to neutralize this type of gaming."
- PH puan ağırlıklarını **açıklamayı reddediyor**: şeffaflık "gaming'i kolaylaştırır".
- **Talep hacmi somut sayılarla:** bir maker launch sonrası **12-15 DM** almış, bir başkası **8 teklif**, bir başkası **10 ayrı talep**. Yani launch başına çift haneli satıcı teması standart.
- Dolandırıcılık paterni: parayı alıp birincilik vaat edip kaybolanlar. Birden fazla kaynak Hindistan pazarından gelen benzer talepleri ve kısa sürede farklı ürünler için tekrar tekrar mesaj atan hesapları işaret ediyor.

**b) "Stop Selling Upvote Bots. You're Damaging the Platform."** — PYO (@durimango), **~1 yıl önce (≈Ağu 2025)**, **317 görüntülenme, 6 upvote, 28 yorum** ([thread](https://www.producthunt.com/p/general/stop-selling-upvote-bots-you-re-damaging-the-platform)):
- **Juan Secchi (PH ekibi):** oy botları etkisiz ve ters teper — *"In most cases, your launch will be negatively impacted by having votes removed or even being unfeatured."* Ayrıca satıcıların launch öncesi **uydurma kanıtlarla** maker'lara ulaştığını söylüyor.
- Yorumcu Chris Horris: "groundbreaking", "life changing" gibi kalıplarla spam yorum sorunu.
- **Bu thread'de fiyat, sayı veya isim verilmiyor.**

**c) "Has anyone experienced a significant removal of upvotes on launch day?"** — Kushal Gandhi, **~1 yıl önce** ([thread](https://www.producthunt.com/p/general/has-anyone-experienced-a-significant-removal-of-upvotes-on-launch-day)):
- **Somut sayı YOK, sıralama düşüşü YOK, ürün adı YOK, PH ekibinden yanıt YOK.** Talebindeki "kaç oy silindi" verisi bu thread'de mevcut değil.
- Filip Panoski: "launch sonrası her hafta oy kaybediyordum, sonra birden bir yığın oyu geri kazandım" → PH'ın bot flag'leme/geri alma döngüsü.
- Nika: "ya bot temizliği ya backend sorunu, destek ile konuş." Elena Mira: destek'e yaz.

**d) PH resmî politikası** — Help Center, **28 Temmuz 2025** ([makale](https://help.producthunt.com/en/articles/11869098-how-does-product-hunt-ensure-fair-voting-and-prevent-spam-or-vote-manipulation)): "gerçek olmayan veya topluluk kurallarına aykırı" oylar siliniyor. "Advanced algorithms monitor unusual voting patterns, repetitive behavior, and potential spam." Sahte hesap ve oy halkaları (voting rings) hedefte. Yaptırımlar: oy silme, içerik silme, hesap kısıtlama, launch sırasında puan dalgalanmasını izleme. **Hiçbir sayısal eşik açıklanmıyor.**

## 5. İSİMLİ CEZALANDIRILMIŞ LAUNCH

- **Startup Gonzo (CCRRR2), no-code directory, 16 Aralık 2023** ([Indie Hackers postu](https://www.indiehackers.com/post/successfully-failed-product-hunt-launch-result-removed-from-featured-page-058b65d8d2)): **300+ upvote** aldı, **4. sıraya** çıkacaktı, ama **featured sayfasından çıkarıldı ve tüm sayfalarda gizlendi**. PH'ın verdiği tek yanıt: *"While there is never a guarantee to be featured on the homepage, I've passed your desire along to our Moderation team."* — **gerekçe açıklanmadı**. Maker'ın PH geçmişi: 10,000 upvote vermiş, 280 günlük streak. Launch yine de 60 yeni ürün gönderimi ve 18 kurs kaydı getirdi. Not: unfeature gerekçesinin oy satın alma olduğu **doğrulanmadı** — maker "directory olduğu için" ve "solo maker'a önyargı" diyor, 4 gün önce başka bir directory 2. olmuş.
- **Daytona**, 27 Mart 2024, PH'ta #3-#4 — bir HN thread'i ([39837948](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=39837948), 6 puan, 5 yorum) organik görünmeyen oy dalgalanmalarını tartışıyor, ama **Daytona hakkında suçlama yok**, ceza yok. Thread'de fiyat/satıcı adı yok.
- **Gerçek şu: hiçbir kaynakta "X ürünü oy satın aldığı için Y tarihinde unfeatured edildi" diye isim + tarih + oy sayısı veren doğrulanmış bir vaka bulamadım.** PH cezaları sessiz uyguluyor, gerekçe yazmıyor (yukarıdaki destek yanıtı bunun kanıtı). Bu, soruşturmacılar için yapısal bir kör nokta.

---

## ★ SORULMAYAN AMA ÖNEMLİ BULGULAR

- **Ekonomi tersine dönüyor:** Zaib testinde $400 harcayan #3 oldu, $600 harcayan #5. **Fiyat ile sonuç korele değil** — kritik değişken **teslimat zamanlaması** (gün boyu dağıtılmış vs anında). Uprows'un tek "zamanlanmış teslimat" sunan servis olması ve en iyi sonucu alması tesadüf değil: PH'ın anomali tespiti tam olarak lojistik eğriden sapmayı arıyor (Michigan Daily metodolojisi ile birebir örtüşüyor). Yani **satıcılar PH'ın tespit modelini tersine mühendislikle çözmüş durumda.**
- **Organik taban çok düşük:** kontrol launch'ı **87 oy** aldı ve top 10'a giremedi. #3 için ~347 oy gerekti. Yani "top 5" bandının fiyatı kabaca **$350-600** — bir Cyberpark/SF pitch'inde "PH #3" ibaresinin nakit karşılığı bu.
- **Sıralama doğrudan fiyatlanıyor:** Uprows Hub açıkça "#5 paketi $470, #3/#4 paketi $800" satıyor. Bu, oy satışından bir adım öte — **sonuç garantisi** satışı. Yani satıcı, PH'ın o günkü eşiğini gerçek zamanlı bildiğini iddia ediyor.
- **PH'ın 0-puan kuralı en önemli tek bulgu:** Kerzhner'in "yeni Gmail hesabının oyu 0 puan sayar" açıklaması, ucuz segmenti (Fiverr $0.055/oy, smgrowth $0.40/oy) **matematiksel olarak değersiz** kılıyor. Bu segment sadece **görünen sayıyı** şişiriyor, sıralamayı değil. Alıcı bunu ölçemiyor çünkü PH ağırlıkları açıklamıyor — **bilgi asimetrisi satıcının iş modeli.**
- **Bot oranı iddiası büyük ama zayıf kaynaklı:** "bot aktivitesi hem yorum hem oyda gerçek kullanıcıları geçti" — Michigan Daily bunu "diğer analizler" diye aktarıyor, birincil kaynak vermiyor. **DOĞRULANMADI**, ama doğruysa PH launch'ının sinyal değeri sıfıra yakın demektir.
- **"me good LLM" tuzağı** — yorumlara gömülü prompt injection ile bot ifşası. Bu, ölçüm/tespit tarafında ucuz ve zekice bir yöntem; kendi ürünlerinde agent itibarı ölçen biri için doğrudan uygulanabilir bir teknik.
- **Kripto ödeme** (SocialPlug: BTC/ETH/LTC/USDT) ve **login gerektirmeme** — sektörün yasal gri alanda konumlandığının işareti, iz bırakmama tasarımı.
- **PH "live cutting" yapıyor** ama geri de alıyor (Panoski'nin "kaybettiğim oylar geri geldi" ifadesi) → false positive oranı ciddi. Yani **temiz bir launch da oy kaybedebilir**, ve maker bunun neden olduğunu asla öğrenemiyor.

## ERİŞEMEDİKLERİM (açıkça)

- **michigandaily.com** — doğrudan fetch: önce HTTP 429, sonra "domain güvenli değil" ağ bloğu. Makalenin tam metnini **okuyamadım**. Dataset boyutu, tarih aralığı, kaç ürün işaretlendiği bilinmiyor.
