# 2. ESSAYS (ben-evans.com/benedictevans)

### ★ "How will OpenAI compete?" — 19 February 2026
https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2026/2/19/how-will-openai-compete-nkg2x

The single most important source. Verbatim:

> "First, the business as we see it today doesn't have a strong, clear competitive lead. It doesn't have a unique technology or product. The models have a very large user base, but very narrow engagement and stickiness, and no network effect or any other winner-takes-all effect so far that provides a clear path to turning that user base into something broader and durable."

> "…today there is no mechanic we know of for one company to get a lead that others in the field could never match. There is no equivalent of the network effects seen at everything from Windows to Google Search to iOS to Instagram, where market share was self-reinforcing and no amount of money and effort was enough for someone else to to break in or catch up."

> "This could change if there was a breakthrough that enabled a network effect, most obviously continuous learning, but we can't plan for that happening. It could also change if there are practical scale effects around access to proprietary data, which today is the dark matter of AI: this could be user data (but it's not clear what scale here would look like, and the existing platform companies have a lot), or vertical data (foundation models don't know what happens inside SAP or Salesforce and don't have millions of spreadsheets from inside investment banks, though you can sometimes buy this or pay people to make it, but so can all the other big players)."

> "…most people aren't benefiting from 'memory' or the other features that the product teams at each company copy from each other in the hope of building stickiness (**and memory is stickiness, not a network effect**)."

> "Usage is a mile wide but an inch deep."

> "In the meantime, when you have an undifferentiated product, early leads in adoption tend not to be durable, and competition tends to shift to brand and distribution."

> "…the browser itself, and the chatbot itself, are just an input box and an output box. The ChatGPT app, like all the chatbot apps, is just a 'thin wrapper' - how *could* you make yours different?"

> "Microsoft won browsers for the first generation of the consumer internet but that turned out not to matter, because the experiences that did matter, and the value capture, were created elsewhere."

> "…people don't build TSMC apps or Intel apps." / "Running a cloud doesn't give you leverage over third part products and services that are further up the stack."

> "…try to turn foundation models themselves into commodity infrastructure sold at marginal cost."

On the "abstraction/agent-protocol" moat thesis (directly relevant to agents/integrations):
> "First, there's a recurring fallacy in tech that you can abstract many different complex products into a simple standard interface - you could call this the **'widget fallacy'**. A decade ago people said 'APIs are the new BD', which was really the same concept, and it mostly failed. This is partly because there's a huge gap between what looks cool in demos and all of the work and thought in the interaction models and the workflows in the actual product: very quickly you'll run into an exception case and you'll need the actual product UI and a human decision. It's also because the incentives are misaligned: no-one wants to be someone else's dumb API call…"

> "…if these are all separate systems plugged together by abstracted and automated APIs, is the user or developer locked into any one of them? … This is much less code than making both an iOS and Android app, and anyway, can't you get the AI to write the code for you? What does *that* do to developer lock-ins?"

The closing frame — his actual definition of a moat:
> "Maybe the word I'm really looking for is **power**. … my medieval history professor, Roger Lovatt, told me that power is the ability to make people do something that they don't want to do, and that's really the question here. Does OpenAI have the ability to get consumers, developers and enterprises to use its systems more than anybody else, regardless of what the system itself actually does?"

> "But do you have a reason why everyone has to use your thing, even though your competitors have built the same thing? And are there reasons why your thing will always be better than the competition no matter how much money and effort they throw at it? That's how the entire consumer tech industry has worked for all of our lives. **If not, then the only thing you have is execution, every single day. Executing better than everyone else is certainly an aspiration, and some companies have managed it over extended periods and even persuaded themselves that they've institutionalised this, but it's not a strategy.**"

> "PSA: a 1:1 relationship between capex and revenue is not a flywheel"

### ★ "Ways to think about token pricing" — 9 July 2026
https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2026/7/9/ways-to-think-about-token-pricing

> "…the question at the end of that remains whether the foundation models have sustainable pricing power, strategic leverage and value capture, or whether they become low-margin commodity infrastructure providers. **At the moment, I think every dynamic we can see points to the latter.**"

> "**Fourth, how much of the value from those high-end use cases is captured by the frontier model itself?** How much needs to be wrapped in **tooling, process, proprietary data, go-to-market, networks, support, and everything else associated with a traditional software company**, even if you do need the big expensive frontier model underneath? Can that model do the whole thing, or is the model, no matter how good, still a piece of infrastructure that you use to make the actual product? … Or do even (or especially) the most sophisticated and high-value use-cases need to sit inside hundreds of new companies that can pick and choose which models to use?"

> "…at the other extreme LLMs look like databases - there'll be millions of them, some very big and some very small, and **the value is in what you build on top - after all, every SaaS company is a 'database wrapper'**."

> "At the moment, everyone is using mostly the same science and mostly the same training data, and getting mostly the same results, and we don't yet know of a network effect or any other winner-takes-all effect that would let one company pull ahead, stay ahead, and do things that others could not, in some sustainable way."

> "…in the last 20 years cellular data traffic has risen by several orders of magnitude, and this has become an enormous industry, with annual revenue of a trillion dollars and capex of $200 billion, but the stocks have gone nowhere, and **all the value was captured by other people further up the stack**."

> "…**analogies don't have predictive value**."

> "…**every path to foundation models having market dominance, strategic leverage, value capture, winner-takes-all effects, or anything else other than becoming commodity infrastructure, requires something to change.**"

> "I've argued at length that I think **chatbots are a poor interface that will struggle to capture value up the stack**."

### ★ "Unbundling AI" — 5 October 2023
https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2023/10/5/unbundling-ai

The clearest statement he has ever made on app-layer moats:

> "Of course, one of the big questions in AI now is around that 'thin' in 'thin ChatGPT wrappers' - how thin? **If you're just an API call with a GUI, you probably don't have much of a moat.** But on the other hand, everyone in tech today is wrapping something else at some level. Snap uses Google Cloud for storage, but we don't call it a 'thin GCP Wrapper'. … A legal software company might use GCP translation and AWS sentiment analysis just as it used AWS data storage, but it used them **to build something that law firms could buy, with a lot of other product around that, and a sales force and understanding of what lawyers needed, and it didn't worry at all about competition from AWS. And then, as part of that product, it might built its own models with its own data doing things that the hyperscalers did not.**"

> "Just as every Unix function became a company, every one of those templates in the 'File/New' screen is a company. **At a certain point, the kinds of control, tooling, suggestion and so on that you want become so specific to the task that they could become an entirely new product and an entirely new company.**"

> "…very quickly you have to memorise a bunch of obscure incantations, much like the command lines that GUIs replaced … and then you pay someone to turn those command into buttons."

### "Building AI products" — 8 June 2024
https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2024/6/8/building-ai-products

> "…with any new technology, we begin by trying to make it fit the problems we already have, while the incumbents try to make it a feature … Then startups use it to unbundle the incumbents (to unbundle search, Oracle or Email), but meanwhile, other startups try to work out what we could build that would be truly native to the new technology."

> "…here we have a general-purpose technology, and yet the way to deploy is to **unbundle it into single-purpose tools and experiences**. … Electric motors are a general-purpose technology, but you don't buy a box of electric motors from Home Depot - you buy a drill, a washing machine and a blender."

> "You wrap the prompt in buttons and UI - in *product*."

> On whether labs will swallow the app layer: "…they might move up through all of those levels of abstraction to the top. **That would leave no room for 'thin GPT wrappers'. Yet I don't think they can really do that yet**…"

### "AI, networks and Mechanical Turks" — 23 November 2025
https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2025/11/23/ai-networks-and-mechanical-turks

This is his direct attack on the proprietary-data / cold-start moat:

> "Of course, this is a network effect, and comes with a cold start problem that's a barrier to entry. How do you provide those recommendations, suggestions and connections before you have users, and how do you get users before you can do that? (This is also a question for all machine learning startups.)"

> "…you might not need to build your own Mechanical Turk. If this kind of knowledge generalises enough, **it might just be an API call from a world model. You can rent the cold start.** Where Amazon or TikTok built recommendation systems by looking at what people do on Amazon and TikTok, that might just be the inference of a general-purpose LLM that anyone can plug into their product. … **You shift the point of leverage.**"

### "Competing in search" — 18 August 2024
https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2024/8/19/competing-in-search

The counter-example of a *real* moat (search), showing what AI lacks:
> "So, search is a virtuous circle. Everyone uses Google because it has the best results, and it has the best results because everyone uses it, and hence it has the money to invest in getting even better results. … In tech this is called a network effect; in competition theory it's called a natural monopoly."

> "Meanwhile, users do not, by and large, change defaults unless the default is much worse that the alternative…" / the court agreed these contracts "freeze the market."

> "Web search is a reinforcement learning engine, where Google sees a lot more of the reinforcement than anyone else, and spends tens of billions of dollars to make sure things stay that way."

### "AI metrics" — 9 June 2025
https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2025/6/9/generative-ais-metrics-question

> "…when you reformulate a Google search and try again, or click on the third link and do or don't come back afterwards, you're giving Google signals that make it better, and **that's a powerful network effect. It's not clear that any LLM providers are really able to leverage this kind of thing yet**, and what they would measure: if I ask a question and don't try again, was that the right result, was it wrong but I thought it was right, or did I give up and go to Google?"

### "Are better models better?" — 22 January 2025
https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2025/1/the-problem-with-better-models
> "They do not 'know' or 'understand' - they approximate." Error rates are "a fundamental property of the technology," not an engineering gap. (Relevant to agent-reliability arguments: he treats unreliability as inherent, therefore a *product-design* problem, not something a better model fixes.)

### Other essays checked, low moat content
- "What kind of disruption?" — 14 March 2025 — https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2025/3/14/what-kind-of-disruption
- "GenAI's adoption puzzle" — 25 May 2025 — https://www.ben-evans.com/benedictevans/2025/5/25/genais-adoption-puzzle (contains zero instances of moat/commodity/network effect/value capture)

Free tier gets news two days late with no column; **Premium is $150/year and is the only way to reach the "complete archive of over 600 issues"** (ben-evans.com/newsletter-archive). The Sunday column — where much of his moat commentary lands weekly — is paywalled. **I could not read any of it.**

# 4. THE ANSWER: what does Evans name as the source of defensibility?

**Headline: Evans does not believe a durable moat has been demonstrated anywhere in the AI stack yet.** His position is not "here are the moats" but "every claimed moat fails a test, and the only honest answer is that value moves up the stack into ordinary software companies." He names candidates as *questions*, not conclusions ("Where is the value capture?" is literally a slide headline).

### Buckets he affirms (weakly, as the least-bad candidates)

| Bucket | Evans' position | Key quote |
|---|---|---|
| **Workflow depth** | **Strongest affirmation.** The recurring answer. Value is in "tooling, process, GUI, workflows" wrapped around a commodity model. His anti-agent-protocol argument is explicitly a workflow argument. | "there's a huge gap between what looks cool in demos and all of the work and thought in the interaction models and the workflows in the actual product: very quickly you'll run into an exception case and you'll need the actual product UI and a human decision" |
| **Proprietary data** | **Named as candidate, then heavily qualified.** Vertical/enterprise data is "the dark matter of AI" but "you can sometimes buy this or pay people to make it, **but so can all the other big players**." And his Nov 2025 essay argues LLMs let you "rent the cold start," destroying the classic data flywheel. | "Proprietary vertical data?" (2025 Autumn slide) |
| **Distribution** | **Affirmed as the decisive weapon — but note: it belongs to incumbents, not startups.** This is his explanation for Gemini/Meta AI share gains and for the Google search case. | "when you have an undifferentiated product, early leads in adoption tend not to be durable, and competition tends to shift to brand and distribution" |
| **Brand** | Affirmed narrowly, at the consumer chatbot layer only. | "The models may be commodities, but ChatGPT is the only 'AI' brand with consumer awareness" (May 2025 slide) |
| **Integrations / GTM / sales force** | Affirmed as part of the "boring normal software company" bundle. | "…a sales force and understanding of what lawyers needed, and it didn't worry at all about competition from AWS" |
| **Scale/capital** | Affirmed only for *infra*, and even then he says it buys "a seat at the table," not leverage. Explicit Boeing/Airbus/TSMC/Rock's Law framing. | "does that get you anything more than a seat at that table?" |

### Buckets he explicitly attacks or does not name

| Bucket | Evans' position |
|---|---|
| **Switching cost / lock-in** | **Directly attacked.** "memory is stickiness, not a network effect." And on agent protocols: "what stops a developer from doing both? This is much less code than making both an iOS and Android app, and anyway, can't you get the AI to write the code for you? What does *that* do to developer lock-ins?" |
| **Agent reliability / evals** | **Not named as a moat at all.** He treats error rate as "a fundamental property of the technology" affecting everyone equally, and benchmarks as "problematic, gamed, saturated" — i.e. non-differentiating. Closest he comes is the widget-fallacy/exception-case argument, which is workflow, not evals. |
| **System of record** | **Not named as a moat.** Appears only descriptively in the 2026 deck ("'Big iron' systems of record - ERP, CRM, HCM etc" as one axis of the software landscape). He names the *data inside* SAP/Salesforce as a possible scale effect, but never argues that owning the SoR is defensible. |
| **Regulatory / compliance** | **Not named as a startup moat.** It appears twice, both negatively: as an enterprise deployment blocker ("Security, privacy, IPR, error rates, legal"), and as a possible *deus ex machina* — "Many people see the pleas for regulation from Anthropic and (sometimes) OpenAI as a front for regulatory capture." |
| **Network effects** | **Repeatedly and emphatically denied for LLMs.** "crucially there are no network effects." Only path he allows: "This could change if there was a breakthrough that enabled a network effect, most obviously continuous learning, but we can't plan for that happening." |

### His skeptical arguments, condensed (all verbatim above)
1. **"We have no moat"** → **"no apparent defensibility"** (May 2025) → **"No apparent moats / No clarity on product or value capture"** (Nov 2025) → **"Models are commodities and have no network effects"** (May 2026). The skepticism *hardened* over three deck cycles.
2. **The chatbot is a browser.** "just an input box and an output box… just a 'thin wrapper'."
3. **Commodity infra doesn't capture value up the stack.** Mobile: trillion-dollar industry, "the stocks have gone nowhere."
4. **The burden of proof is on the moat.** "every path to foundation models having… anything else other than becoming commodity infrastructure, requires something to change."
5. **Execution is not a moat.** "the only thing you have is execution, every single day… but it's not a strategy."
6. **The real test is power**, not the vocabulary of platforms/ecosystems/flywheels: "the ability to make people do something that they don't want to do."

### The practical implication he does state
The 2026 provisional thesis is his affirmative bet: **"Chat is a terrible UX / General use needs 'apps'"** + **"Labs can't build (or generate) all the apps"** + **"Models will just be infra / Innovation will move up the stack."** He is *not* saying app-layer companies have moats — he is saying app-layer companies will exist and will look like **normal software companies** ("Building 'normal' software companies?"), earning their position with product, GTM, proprietary data, workflow and sales, exactly as a legal-software company earned it on top of AWS.

If you need Evans-on-agents, that widget-fallacy paragraph is the load-bearing text.
- **Podcast appearances not fetched** — e.g. Analyse podcast episode literally titled "If AI Models Have No Moat, What Are Investors Buying? with Benedict Evans" (analysepodcast.com). **DOĞRULANMADI** — I saw only the search-result title, no transcript. Also unfetched: Forbes, 19 May 2026, "Benedict Evans Says AI Capex Is Eating The World And Investors Still Have No Map." Secondhand deck summaries also exist at globaldatacenterhub.com and the-ai-corner.com — I did not use them since I had the primary PDFs.

If a pitch needs Evans on *market creation* rather than *defensibility*, that's the material, and it reads much more optimistically than his moat commentary.

## Access note first
`cloud.google.com` WebFetch is blocked in this environment, but `cloud.google.com` now **301-redirects to `docs.cloud.google.com`** (confirmed live on two URLs), which is fetchable. All findings below are from that official Google domain. Fetches were intermittently blocked; every claim below came back from a successful primary fetch unless labelled otherwise.

**Correction to the task's premise:** the "disable billing" example is *not* at `/billing/docs/how-to/notify`. That page is now only the Pub/Sub setup guide. The kill-switch tutorial lives at `/billing/docs/how-to/disable-billing-with-notifications`.

---

## 1. Budgets & alerts
Source: https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/docs/how-to/budgets

- **Default thresholds: 50%, 90%, 100%** — "Default alert thresholds are set at 50%, 90%, and 100% of the budget amount, calculated against *Actual* spend." So yes, 50/90/100, and the default basis is Actual, not Forecasted.
- **Forecasted spend: supported.** "Thresholds can be set for *actual costs* accrued during the budget period, or for *forecasted costs*." Enum values in the API are `CURRENT_SPEND` and `FORECASTED_SPEND` (https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/docs/reference/budget/rest/v1/billingAccounts.budgets).
  - Limitation: "Budgets configured for a custom time range cannot trigger alerts on *Forecasted* costs."
- **Max threshold rules per budget: DOĞRULANMADI.** Not stated on the budgets page, the quotas page, or the REST reference. `gcloud billing budgets create` accepts repeated `--threshold-rule` flags with no documented cap (https://docs.cloud.google.com/sdk/gcloud/reference/billing/budgets/create). Do not publish a number here.
- **Budget quota: 50,000 per billing account.** "Each individual Cloud Billing account can have up to 50,000 budgets" (https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/quotas).
- **Budgets are free**, and critically: "Setting an *alerts-only* budget *doesn't* automatically cap Google Cloud or Google Maps Platform usage or spending." A plain budget is **not** a kill switch.
- **Default email recipients:** holders of `roles/billing.admin` (Billing Account Administrator) and `roles/billing.user` (Billing Account User) on the target billing account. For single-project budgets, Project Owners can also be notified. You can turn this off with `disableDefaultIamRecipients`: "When set to true, disables default notifications sent when a threshold is exceeded."
- **Monitoring notification channels: max 5.** "select up to five *existing* notification channels" (https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/docs/how-to/budgets-notification-recipients). REST ref phrases it as "A maximum of 5 email notifications are allowed."
- **Only Email channels work.** "For Cloud Billing budget alerts, you must configure Email notification channels. Other types of notification channels aren't supported." So no direct Slack/PagerDuty/SMS/webhook channel — Slack must go through Pub/Sub.

## 2. Programmatic notifications (Pub/Sub)
Source: https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/docs/how-to/budgets-programmatic-notifications

Wiring: create a Pub/Sub topic → attach it to the budget via the "Manage notifications" section (or `pubsubTopic` in the API, format `projects/{projectId}/topics/{topic_id}`) → subscribe a Cloud Run function to the topic.

**Message attributes** (key/value on the Pub/Sub message):
- `billingAccountId` — e.g. `01D4EE-079462-DFD6EC`
- `budgetId` — e.g. `de72f49d-779b-4945-a127-4d6ce8def0bb`
- `schemaVersion` — e.g. `1.0`

**Data payload**: "Budget alert details are returned in a base64-encoded UTF-8 string containing a JSON object with the following properties":
- `budgetDisplayName` — "The human-readable name assigned to the budget."
- `costAmount` — e.g. `140.321`, "The amount of costs accrued."
- `costIntervalStart` — e.g. `"2021-02-01T08:00:00Z"`, "The start of the budget alert period."
- `budgetAmount` — e.g. `152.557`
- `budgetAmountType` — `SPECIFIED_AMOUNT` | `LAST_MONTH_COST` | `LAST_PERIODS_COST`
- `alertThresholdExceeded` — e.g. `0.9`, "The highest configured actual cost alert threshold exceeded."
- `forecastThresholdExceeded` — e.g. `0.2`, "The highest forecasted cost alert threshold exceeded."
- `currencyCode` — e.g. `"USD"`

**Frequency — this is the load-bearing detail for a kill switch:** "Budget notifications are sent to the Pub/Sub topic **multiple times per day** with the current status of your budget" and "Pub/Sub notifications are sent **even if the Cloud Billing account has no usage**." So the function must be idempotent — it will be invoked repeatedly, and the threshold fields are simply absent/low when nothing is exceeded.

## 3. Google's own "disable billing" kill switch
Source: https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/docs/how-to/disable-billing-with-notifications

- **Warning, verbatim:** "This tutorial removes Cloud Billing from your project, shutting down all resources. Resources might be irretrievably deleted."
- **Method:** Cloud Billing API `projects.updateBillingInfo` with an empty billing account name.
  - Node: `resource: {billingAccountName: ''}, // Disable billing`
  - Python: `project_billing_info = billing_v1.ProjectBillingInfo(billing_account_name="")`
- **IAM:** the service account needs `roles/billing.admin` (Billing Account Administrator) granted **on the Cloud Billing account itself, not the project**.
- **Prereq:** the Cloud Billing API must be enabled.
- The specific enumeration "Cloud Storage data / Compute Engine instances deleted" — **DOĞRULANMADI** as verbatim text on that page. The related page https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/docs/how-to/control-usage does say: "When you disable billing on a project, all services stop and all resources are eventually deleted."

**Documented softer alternative:** https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/docs/how-to/control-usage — "If you need a more nuanced response, you can selectively control resources... you can stop some Compute Engine resources while leaving Cloud Storage resources intact. Stopping only some resources reduces your costs without completely disabling your environment." Uses Compute API `instancesClient.list()` then `instancesClient.stop()`, filtering `status === 'RUNNING'`. Caveat in the doc: "This code sample only stops instances in a single zone in the specified project." It also recommends custom roles over broad admin roles.

## 4. Cloud Quotas as a softer cap
- https://docs.cloud.google.com/docs/quotas/view-manage — **Yes, you can lower below default:** "To restrict usage of a particular resource, create a *quota override* by changing the quota value to a value less than the default quota value." Also: "Creating a quota override is sometimes referred to as *capping usage*." Decrease adjustments can be requested at project, organization, and folder level (increases are generally project-level only). Caveat: "Quota overrides are not available to all services."
- https://docs.cloud.google.com/docs/quotas/overview — three quota types: **allocation** (how much of a resource is allocated), **rate** (amount per time period), **concurrent** (number of concurrent operations).
- https://docs.cloud.google.com/apis/docs/capping-api-usage — "Depending on the API, you can explicitly cap requests by limiting the *requests per day*, *requests per minute*, or *requests per minute per user*." Managed in Console under APIs & Services → the API → Quotas tab, or IAM & Admin → Quotas. Warning: "Quota limits are not always entirely precise, because there is some latency between when a quota is surpassed and when the enforcement begins." Whether a cap can be set to **0** is **DOĞRULANMADI** (not addressed on that page).

---

## DUMP — things found that you did not ask about

**The big one: native Spend Cap budgets now exist (Preview).** https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/docs/how-to/budgets-spend-caps — this is Google's first-party kill switch and largely obsoletes the DIY Pub/Sub-function pattern for supported services.
- "Spend Caps in Cloud Billing Budgets help reduce unexpected or runaway costs for eligible services." "When a spend cap budget is enforced, usage of your specified services is automatically paused until you *manually* lift the spend cap."
- **Eligible services only:** Gemini API, Gemini Enterprise Agent Platform (formerly Vertex AI), Cloud Run, Cloud Run functions.
- **Scope limit:** "Spend caps are limited to budgets that are scoped to a *single* Google Cloud project and a *single* eligible service." Budget period must be **Monthly, starting the first day of each month**.
- **Does not stop persistent spend:** "Spend caps don't pause any on-going, fixed usage associated with *persistent* resources (such as compute and storage services), which remain active and continue to accrue charges." It blocks "all *new* usage for the specific service in the specified project."
- **Not instant:** "Even though faster than reports, the enforcement of spend caps aren't instant and any cost overages are billed as normal."
- Costs are **gross** — "don't include savings and credits."
- IAM: Billing Account Administrator, or Project Owner on target projects, or Billing Account Costs Manager + Project Editor.
- Status: Preview, under Pre-GA Offerings Terms, "available 'as is' and might have limited support."

**Other unrequested findings:**
- Budget API rate quotas: read-only (`getBudget`, `listBudgets`) **800 calls/min**; writes (`createBudget`, `updateBudget`, `deleteBudget`) **100 calls/min**. Cloud Billing API: 300 calls/min per project, 975 calls/min per organization by default. (https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/quotas)
- Project Owner budget notification for single-project budgets is itself flagged **Preview**.
- Per-user API capping falls back to authenticated principal or client IP, which the doc notes is weak against shared public IPs and IP rotation.
- There is a separate `listen-to-notifications` guide (https://docs.cloud.google.com/billing/docs/how-to/listen-to-notifications) I did not open.
- A "Cost Anomaly Detection" feature is GA (Google Cloud blog result) — not a primary doc, unread, **DOĞRULANMADI**.

**Could not access / not verified:**
- `cloud.google.com` direct fetches were blocked throughout; all content came via the `docs.cloud.google.com` redirect target. Several fetches failed randomly and needed 2–3 retries — if you re-run this, expect flakiness, not absence of content.
- Max threshold rules per budget: genuinely not documented anywhere I checked (budgets page, quotas page, REST reference, gcloud reference).
- Whether an API quota override can be set to exactly 0.
- The verbatim Cloud Storage/GCE deletion enumeration on the disable-billing page.
- Gemini Enterprise overage/spend-control doc (https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini/enterprise/docs/manage-costs-overview) appeared in results but was not opened.

## Rapor 25

# CACHING FUNDAMENTALS — PRIMARY SOURCE DOSYASI

---

## 1. HTTP CACHE-CONTROL

### MDN — https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Cache-Control

Response directives, MDN'in kendi kelimeleriyle:

| Directive | Semantics |
|---|---|
| `max-age=N` | Response N saniye **fresh**. Süre, response'un **origin'de üretildiği andan** sayılır — cache'e ulaştığı andan değil. Negatif/non-integer değer `0` sayılır. |
| `s-maxage=N` | Sadece **shared cache** (proxy/CDN). Private cache **yok sayar**. Shared cache için `max-age`'i ve `Expires`'ı override eder. |
| `no-cache` | "The response can be stored in caches, but the response must be validated with the origin server before each reuse, even when the cache is disconnected from the origin server." — **saklamayı engellemez**, yeniden kullanımdan önce validation zorunlu kılar. |
| `no-store` | "Any caches of any kind (private or shared) should not store this response." |
| `private` | "The response can be stored only in a private cache (e.g., local caches in browsers)." |
| `public` | "The response can be stored in a shared cache." `Authorization` header'lı response'lardaki kısıtı override eder. |
| `must-revalidate` | Fresh iken kullanılabilir; stale olunca origin'e validate edilmeden kullanılamaz. Validation başarısızsa **504** döner. |
| `proxy-revalidate` | `must-revalidate` ama sadece shared cache için. |
| `immutable` | Fresh iken içerik değişmeyecek → gereksiz conditional request'i önler. Versiyonlu/hash'li URL pattern'i ile. |
| `stale-while-revalidate=N` | "The cache could reuse a stale response while it revalidates it to a cache." |
| `stale-if-error=N` | "The cache can reuse a stale response when an upstream server generates an error, or when the error is generated locally." |
| `no-transform` | Ara katman içeriği dönüştüremez (ör. resim sıkıştırma). |
| `must-understand` | Cache, status code'a göre cache gereksinimini anlıyorsa saklar. `must-understand, no-store` kombinasyonu fallback için. |

**AI kodunun en sık kaçırdığı iki ayrım:**
- `no-cache` ≠ `no-store`. no-cache **saklar**, sadece her kullanımdan önce doğrular. Gerçekten saklanmasın isteniyorsa `no-store` gerekir.
- `private` vs `public`: kullanıcıya özel / login sonrası içerik `private` olmadan CDN'e düşer.

Request-side, tarayıcı desteği notu (MDN): `max-stale`, `min-fresh` **major browser'larda desteklenmiyor**; request `stale-if-error` **hiçbir tarayıcıda desteklenmiyor**. `only-if-cached` cache'te yoksa **504** döner.

### RFC 5861 — https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5861.txt

**§3 — stale-while-revalidate** (verbatim):
> "When present in an HTTP response, the stale-while-revalidate Cache-Control extension indicates that caches MAY serve the response in which it appears after it becomes stale, up to the indicated number of seconds."

```
stale-while-revalidate = "stale-while-revalidate" "=" delta-seconds
```

> "If a cached response is served stale due to the presence of this extension, the cache SHOULD attempt to revalidate it while still serving stale responses (i.e., without blocking)."
> "If delta-seconds passes without the cached entity being revalidated, it SHOULD NOT continue to be served stale, absent other information."

**§3.1 örnek — kritik nokta:** `Cache-Control: max-age=600, stale-while-revalidate=30` → 600s fresh, sonra **30s daha** stale servis edilebilir. RFC'nin kendi uyarısı:
> "Generally, servers will want to set the combination of max-age and stale-while-revalidate to the longest total potential freshness lifetime that they can tolerate. For example, with both set to 600, the server must be able to tolerate the response being served from cache for up to 20 minutes."

Ve stampede'e bağlanan cümle:
> "Since asynchronous validation will only happen if a request occurs after the response has become stale, but before the end of the stale-while-revalidate window... If the window is too small, or traffic is too sparse, some requests will fall outside of it, and **block until the server can validate the cached response**."

**§4 — stale-if-error**:
```
stale-if-error = "stale-if-error" "=" delta-seconds
```
> "In this context, 'an error is any situation that would result in a 500, 502, 503, or 504 HTTP response status code being returned.'"

Hem request hem response extension olarak kullanılabilir. §4.1 örneği: `max-age=600, stale-if-error=1200` → 900s sonra origin 500 dönerse cache eski 200'ü `Age: 900` ile döner; **age > 1800** olunca error'ı write-through eder.

### RFC 9111 (HTTP Caching, RFC 7234'ü obsolete eder) — https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9111.html

Section numaraları:
- §3 Storing Responses in Caches
- §4.1 Calculating Cache Keys with the Vary Header Field
- §4.2 Freshness · §4.2.1 Calculating Freshness Lifetime · §4.2.3 Calculating Age · §4.2.4 Serving Stale Responses
- §4.4 Invalidating Stored Responses
- §5.2.1.1 max-age (request) · §5.2.1.4 no-cache (request) · §5.2.1.5 no-store (request)
- §5.2.2.1 max-age (response) · §5.2.2.2 must-revalidate · §5.2.2.4 no-cache · §5.2.2.5 no-store · §5.2.2.7 private · §5.2.2.9 public · §5.2.2.10 s-maxage

**§4.2.4 stale tanımı:** "A 'stale' response is one that either has explicit expiry information or is allowed to have heuristic expiry calculated, but is not fresh according to the calculations in Section 4.2."

**§4.2.4 kuralı:** "A cache MUST NOT generate a stale response unless it is disconnected or doing so is explicitly permitted by the client or origin server."
→ Yani `stale-while-revalidate` / `stale-if-error` **açık izin** olmadan stale servis edilmez. AI kodu "CDN nasılsa stale döner" varsayar; standart bunu yasaklıyor.

**§5.2.2.2 must-revalidate:** stale response, origin tarafından başarıyla validate edilene kadar kullanılamaz; "if a cache is disconnected, the cache MUST generate an error response rather than reuse the stale response."

---

## 2. VERCEL

### CDN Cache — https://vercel.com/docs/caching/cdn-cache

**Function response'unun cache'lenmesi için Cache-Control'de şunlardan **biri** olmalı:** `s-maxage=N` / `s-maxage=N, stale-while-revalidate=Z` / `s-maxage=N, stale-while-revalidate=Z, stale-if-error=Z`.
Sadece `max-age` yeterli değil — bu, AI'ın ürettiği Next.js route handler'larında en yaygın sessiz hata.

**Cacheable response criteria (hepsi sağlanmalı):**
- Method `GET` veya `HEAD`
- `Range` header yok
- `Authorization` header yok
- Status ∈ {200, 404, 410, 301, 302, 307, 308}
- Content length **≤ 10MB**
- `set-cookie` header **yok** ← sessiz cache-kill #1
- Cache-Control'de `private` / `no-cache` / `no-store` yok
- `Vary: *` yok (Vercel bunu `Cache-Control: private` gibi işler)

**Limits:**
- Max cacheable response size: **streaming functions 20MB, non-streaming 10MB**
- Max cache time: **1 yıl** (`s-maxage`, `max-age`, `stale-while-revalidate`)
- "cache times are best-effort and not guaranteed" — nadiren istenen asset regional cache'ten evict olabilir
- `proxy-revalidate` ve **`stale-if-error` server-side caching için DESTEKLENMİYOR** (Vercel'de). RFC'de var, Vercel'de yok.

**Targeted headers (RFC 9213):**
- `CDN-Cache-Control` → Vercel CDN + diğer CDN'ler, tarayıcı etkilenmez
- `Vercel-CDN-Cache-Control` → sadece Vercel; **tarayıcıya dönmez, diğer CDN'lere forward edilmez**
- Tarayıcıya default dönenler: `Cache-Control` ve `CDN-Cache-Control`
- **Gotcha:** `CDN-Cache-Control` olmadan `Cache-Control` set edersen, Vercel CDN response'u tarayıcıya göndermeden önce `s-maxage` ve `stale-while-revalidate`'i **strip eder**.

**Vary:** Vercel cache key'ine `Accept` ve `Accept-Encoding` zaten dahil. `Vary` sadece caching-enabling directive ile birlikte etki eder.

### Cache status — https://vercel.com/docs/caching/cache-status

`x-vercel-cache` değerleri: `HIT` · `MISS` · `BYPASS` (cache'e hiç bakılmaz) · `STALE` (eski servis + arka planda regenerate) · `PRERENDER` (build-time static storage) · `REVALIDATED` (entry silinmişti, **foreground**'da yeniden üretildi, tam latency ödenir).

MISS reason'ları: **Cold**, **Request collapsed**, **Error** (cache-storage read hatası, uygulama hatası değil).
BYPASS reason'ları: Draft Mode (`__prerender_bypass` cookie), Prerender Bypass (`experimentalBypassFor`), Crawler (Googlebot/Bingbot/facebookexternalhit/Twitterbot/Slackbot).
STALE reason'ları: Time-based revalidation, Tag-based invalidation, **Revalidation error** (regenerate patladı → son iyi response servis edilmeye devam, sonraki request'te retry).

### Request Collapsing — https://vercel.com/docs/incremental-static-regeneration/request-collapsing

Vercel'in stampede korumasının resmi adı. Mekanizma: uncached path'e aynı anda gelen request'ler **aynı region içinde tek function invocation'a** collapse edilir; ilki origin'e gider, kalanlar bekler, hepsine aynı sonuç döner. Dokümanın kendi örneği: 1.000 eşzamanlı request → 1 invocation + 999 bekleyen.
**STALE servis ederken de uygulanır** — yani eşzamanlı background revalidation'lar da tek invocation'a collapse edilir.
Desteklenen: ISR ve Image Optimization.

### Data Cache — https://vercel.com/docs/caching/runtime-cache/data-cache

(Next.js 14 ve altı; 15+ için Runtime Cache.)

| Property | Limit |
|---|---|
| Item size | **2 MB** (daha büyüğü cache'lenmez, sessizce) |
| Tags per item | **128** |
| Max tag length | **256 bytes** |

- **Regional** — her function region'ının bağımsız cache'i var
- production/preview cache'leri hiç paylaşılmaz
- Hobby & Pro: **takımdaki tüm projeler tek cache'i paylaşır** (bir proje diğerinin entry'lerini evict edebilir; purge hepsini siler). Enterprise: proje başına ayrı.
- Deployment'lar arası kalıcı; build time'da güncellenmez
- On-demand/tag revalidation **tüm region'lara 300ms içinde** yayılır
- Eviction: **LRU**

ISR cache: "persists content for 31 days, or until you revalidate it", deployment'a scope'lu (search snippet — DOĞRULANMADI, ISR doküman sayfasını fetch edemedim).

---

## 3. CLOUDFLARE

### Default cache behavior — https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/concepts/default-cache-behavior/

**Max cacheable file size (verbatim):** "Free, Pro and Business customers have a limit of **512 MB**. For Enterprise customers the default maximum cacheable file size is **5 GB**."

**Default olarak cache'lenen uzantılar:** 7Z, AVI, AVIF, APK, BIN, BMP, BZ2, CLASS, CSS, CSV, DOC, DOCX, DMG, EJS, EOT, EPS, EXE, FLAC, GIF, GZ, ICO, ISO, JAR, JPG, JPEG, JS, MID, MIDI, MKV, MP3, MP4, OGG, OTF, PDF, PICT, PLS, PNG, PPT, PPTX, PS, RAR, SVG, SVGZ, SWF, TAR, TIF, TIFF, TTF, WEBM, WEBP, WOFF, WOFF2, XLS, XLSX, ZIP, ZST. (+ robots.txt)

**"The Cloudflare CDN does not cache HTML or JSON by default."** ← API response'u Cloudflare'in otomatik cache'leyeceğini varsayan AI kodu burada yanılır.

Cache **edilmez**: `private`, `no-store`, `no-cache`, `max-age=0`; `Set-Cookie` varsa; non-GET request'lerde.
Cache **edilir**: `public, max-age>0` veya gelecekteki `Expires`.
`max-age` ve `Expires` birlikteyse **max-age kullanılır**.

**Directive/expires yoksa default Edge TTL:**

| Status | Default TTL |
|---|---|
| 200, 206, 301 | **120 dakika** |
| 302, 303 | **20 dakika** |
| 404, 410 | **3 dakika** |

"Cloudflare respects the origin web server's cache headers... unless an Edge Cache TTL cache rule overrides the headers."

### Tiered Cache — https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/how-to/tiered-cache/

Data center'lar hiyerarşiye bölünür; lower-tier önce upper-tier'a sorar, **"only the upper-tier can ask the origin for content"** → origin'e giden bağlantı sayısı tüm network'ten birkaç data center'a iner. Bu, coğrafi dağıtık bir stampede-azaltma mekanizması.

| Feature | Free | Pro | Business | Enterprise |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tiered Cache | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Smart Topology | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Generic Global | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✓ |
| Regional Tiered Cache | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✓ |
| Custom Topology | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✓ |

Public cloud origin'ler (AWS/GCP/Azure/Oracle) için "cloud region hint" verilebilir.

### Cache Reserve — https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/advanced-configuration/cache-reserve/

"Cache Reserve is a large, persistent data store implemented on top of **R2**." Upper-tier kalıcı katman; eviction'ı ve origin egress ücretini engeller. Default retention **30 gün**, asset istendiğinde timer sıfırlanır.

**Uygunluk:** Cloudflare standartlarına göre cacheable olmalı · **minimum TTL 10 saat** (Cache-Control / Edge Cache TTL / Cache Rules ile) · **`Content-Length` response header zorunlu** · image transformation variant'ları uygun değil · R2 dosya boyut limitlerine tabi.

**Fiyat:** Storage **$0.015/GB-ay** · Class A (write) **$4.50/milyon** · Class B (read) **$0.36/milyon**. Doküman örneği: 1.000 asset × 1 GB, ayda 1.000 okuma ≈ **$19.86**.

"Cache Reserve is designed for use with Tiered Cache enabled for maximum origin shielding." Tiered Cache olmadan storage operation maliyeti artar.

### Cache Rules — https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/how-to/cache-rules/

Tüm planlarda mevcut, plan başına farklı rule limiti. "what is eligible to cache, how long it should be cached and where" kontrol edilir; custom cache key desteği var ve custom cache key **purge davranışını değiştirir**.
Ayrıntılı setting listesi (Edge TTL modları, Browser TTL, origin error page passthru, serve-stale) — **DOĞRULANMADI**, `developers.cloudflare.com/rules/cache-rules/settings/` ve `edge-browser-cache-ttl` sayfalarını fetch edemedim (aşağıda "erişemediklerim").

## 4. REDIS / UPSTASH

### Upstash pricing — https://upstash.com/docs/redis/overall/pricing

| Plan | Fiyat | Max cmd/s | Max data | Max request | Max record | Aylık bandwidth |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Free** | $0 | 10.000 | **256 MB** | **10 MB** | **100 MB** | **10 GB** |
| Pay as You Go | $0.20 / 100K komut | 10.000 | 100 GB | 10 MB | 100 MB | ilk 200GB free, sonra $0.03/GB |
| Fixed 250MB | $10/ay | 10.000 | 250 MB | 10 MB | 100 MB | 50 GB |
| Fixed 1GB | $20/ay | 10.000 | 1 GB | 10 MB | 200 MB | 100 GB |
| Fixed 5GB | $100/ay | 10.000 | 5 GB | 20 MB | 300 MB | 500 GB |
| Fixed 10GB | $200/ay | 10.000 | 10 GB | 30 MB | 400 MB | 1 TB |
| Fixed 50GB | $400/ay | 10.000 | 50 GB | 50 MB | 500 MB | 5 TB |
| Fixed 100GB | $800/ay | 16.000 | 100 GB | 75 MB | 1 GB | 10 TB |
| Fixed 500GB | $1.500/ay | 16.000 | 500 GB | 100 MB | 5 GB | 20 TB |

**Free tier komut limiti: aylık 500K** (günlük limit yayınlanmamış). Aşılınca: e-posta bildirimi, "we may rate limit depending on the case."
**Max concurrent connections rakamı hiçbir primary sayfada yayınlanmamış** — pricing tablosunda yok, troubleshooting sayfası (https://upstash.com/docs/redis/troubleshooting/max_concurrent_connections) limitin varlığını söylüyor ama **sayı vermiyor**. Sayı arayan olursa: yayınlanmamış.

### Redis key eviction — https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/reference/eviction/

`maxmemory` 0 = limitsiz (64-bit default). **32-bit sistemlerde implicit 3GB limiti.**

`maxmemory-policy` seçenekleri: `noeviction` (yeni veri yazan komutlar **hata döner**, okumalar çalışır) · `allkeys-lru` · `allkeys-lrm` · `allkeys-lfu` · `allkeys-random` · `volatile-lru` · `volatile-lrm` · `volatile-lfu` · `volatile-random` · `volatile-ttl`.
**`volatile-*` politikaları, TTL'li key yoksa `noeviction` gibi davranır.** ← "TTL koymayı unuttum + volatile-lru" = production'da yazma hatası.

- Redis LRU **gerçek LRU değil, örneklemeli yaklaşım**. `maxmemory-samples 5` default; 10'a çıkarmak gerçek LRU'ya çok yaklaşır (ek CPU karşılığı). Redis 3.0'dan beri iyi aday havuzu tutuluyor.
- LFU (Redis 4.0+): Morris counter, 0-255 aralığı. Default: sayaç ~1 milyon istekte doyar, **dakikada bir decay**. `lfu-log-factor 10`, `lfu-decay-time 1`.
- LRM (Redis **8.6**+): timestamp **sadece write'ta** güncellenir, read'te değil.
- Hit rate formülü: `keyspace_hits / (keyspace_hits + keyspace_misses) * 100`. `EXISTS` key bulamazsa **miss sayılır**.
- Replication/persistence buffer'ı `maxmemory` hesabına **dahil değil** (`mem_not_counted_for_evict`, INFO memory) → maxmemory'yi RAM'in altında bırak.
- `expire` değeri saklamak bellek maliyeti olduğu için `allkeys-lru`, `volatile-lru`'dan daha bellek verimli.

## 5. CACHE STAMPEDE / THUNDERING HERD

### Tanım (Vattani, Chierichetti, Lowenstein — VLDB 2015, s.886, verbatim)

> "The cache stampede problem (also called **dog-piling**, **cache miss storm**, or **cache choking**) is a situation that occurs when a popular cache item expires, leading to multiple requests seeing a cache miss and regenerating that same item at the same time."

Ölçeği veren cümle:
> "The number of requests seeing the cache miss (and therefore contributing to the stampede) depends not only on the request rate, but also on the time needed to recompute the item. For example, if the cache item is accessed 10 times per second, and the recomputation of the item takes 3 seconds, then **30 requests** will recompute the item."

Ve neden kaskad olduğu:
> "A cache stampede is often referred to as a **cascading failure** because several concurrent re-computations will increase the time of each individual recomputation by bogging down the system, in turn causing even more requests to be part of the stampede."

**Kaynak:** Proceedings of the VLDB Endowment, Vol. 8, No. 8, s. 886–897, 2015. PDF: http://www.vldb.org/pvldb/vol8/p886-vattani.pdf · DOI: https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.14778/2757807.2757813

### Paper'ın saydığı 3 mitigasyon ve kendi eleştirileri (s.886–887)

1. **External re-computation** — ayrı bir background process periyodik olarak yeniden üretir. "prevents cache stampedes all together" ama daemon'ı ayakta tutma/monitoring/kod tekrarı yükü; çok item olunca hangisinin ne zaman yeniden hesaplanacağını takip etmek gerekir ve **hiç istenmemiş item'ları da yeniden üretebilir**.
2. **Locking** — cache miss'te key için lock alınmaya çalışılır, sadece alan yeniden üretir. Paper'ın saydığı maliyetler: lock için **ekstra bir write** (write sayısını ikiye katlar), lock TTL'ini ayarlamak zor ("high enough to recompute the item, but less than the re-computation frequency"), ve **fault-tolerant değil**: "if the request acquiring the lock fails while re-generating the item, no item or a stale item will be served until the lock expires and a new lock is acquired."
3. **Probabilistic early expiration** — her request bağımsız olarak, expiry'ye yaklaştıkça artan olasılıkla erken yeniden üretir. "the effect of the stampede is mitigated as less requests will expire at the same time." Zayıflığı: dağılım seçimi ve garanti yokluğu.

### XFETCH — Figure 3, s.891 (verbatim pseudocode)

```
function XFetch(key, ttl; β = 1)
    value, Δ, expiry ← CacheRead(key)
    if !value or Time() − Δβ log(rand()) ≥ expiry then
        start ← Time()
        value ← RecomputeValue()
        Δ ← Time() − start
        CacheWrite(key, (value, Δ), ttl)
    end
    return value
end
```

Değişkenler:
- **Δ (delta)** = son yeniden hesaplamanın **gerçekte sürdüğü süre** (recompute başlangıç/bitiş farkı), cache değerinin yanında saklanır
- **rand()** ∈ (0,1] uniform → `−log(rand())` bir Exp(1) örneği; `−Δβ log(rand())` gap dağılımı D = Exp(1/β)'dan örnekleyip **Δ ile ölçeklemek** demektir
- **β** default **1**; "already provides effective prevention against cache stampedes. It can be increased for even better guarantees against stampedes, if earlier expirations are not a concern."
- Tetikleme koşulu: `now + gap ≥ expiry` → expiry'ye yaklaştıkça tetiklenme olasılığı artar, her worker bağımsız karar verir
- **λ = 1/β, request rate'e bağlı DEĞİL.** Paper'ın vurgusu: "the parameter λ needs *not* to depend on the rate of requests in order to effectively reduce stampedes. This property makes our solution very attractive in that, in its simplest form, it requires no parameter tuning to perform effectively." ← Tuning gerektirmemesi, mühendislik açısından en önemli özellik.
- Implementation notu: cache server expiry'yi vermiyorsa expiry de değere gömülür: `(value, Δ, Time() + ttl)`. Erken expiration'ların gap'inin birikmemesi için write'tan hemen önce `ttl ← ttl + (expiry − Time())`.

**Teoremler:**
- **Theorem 6 (Uniform):** `U(0,ξ)` en fazla `(n/(2ξ), ξ)`-effective. Yani stampede'i √n'e indirmek için √n/2 kadar erken expire'a izin vermek gerekir. Paper'ın örneği: günde bir yeniden hesaplanan, 1 dakika süren, dakikada 10.000 request alan bir item için stampede'i 100'e indirmek **50 dakika erken** expiration ister — pratikte kabul edilemez.
- **Theorem 7 (Exponential):** `Exp(λ)` `((e^λ − 1)(1/λ + 1/ε), (1/λ) log n)`-effective. λ=1'de: early expiration gap `log n`, stampede boyutu `e − 1/e ≈ 2.4`. Aynı 10.000-request örneğinde: stampede 2.4 process, erken expiration `log_e(10.000) ≈ 9.2 dakika`. (50 dk → 9.2 dk, 100 → 2.4)
- **Theorem 8 (Optimality):** n'den bağımsız herhangi bir D için, early expiration gap ≤ (1/λ)log n ise beklenen stampede boyutu ≥ `e^Ω(1/ε)`. Yani **exponential dağılım optimal**, sabit gap ile sabit stampede mümkün değil.

**Deney (§6):** goodreads.com'da bir haftalık gerçek trafik, saatlik popüler-tag istatistiği; recompute ~10s, inter-arrival ~0.07s (σ 0.25s), process rate n ≈ 140. Exp(λ=1) ile stampede'lerin çoğu **1 veya 2**, **hiçbiri 8'den büyük değil**; `U(0,ξ=10)` ortalama ~10 ve ara sıra 20+ tehlikeli stampede. β = 1/λ'yı **1.5'e çıkarmak ortalama stampede'i 2'nin altına** düşürüyor. Recompute 1 dakikaya çıkarıldığında (n ≈ 840) Exp(λ=1) hâlâ **10'un üstünde stampede üretmiyor**, uniform ise ξ=10 ve ξ=20 için **80 ve 50** boyutunda stampede'ler veriyor. Burst testinde (n_low=50, n_high=500, p=0.1) exponential "practically immune", uniform ξ=10/ξ=20 için pikler **70 ve 45**.

Not: Paper, Memcached'i "which do not provide locking mechanisms, or protection against stampedes" diye işaretliyor; Perl CHI ve Drupal'ın **uniform** dağılım kullandığını, uniform'un "far from being optimal" olduğunu söylüyor.

### Facebook — "Scaling Memcache at Facebook", NSDI 2013 (Nishtala et al.), §3.2.1 Leases

Paper iki farklı problemi tek mekanizmayla çözüyor:
- **Stale set:** eşzamanlı memcache güncellemeleri yeniden sıralandığında, bir web server cache'e **güncel olmayan** bir değer yazar.
- **Thundering herd:** aynı key'e çok sayıda client aynı anda gelir, key ya yeni invalidate edilmiştir ya hiç cache'lenmemiştir; hepsi DB'ye iner.

**Lease mekanizması:** cache miss yaşayan client'a memcached bir **lease token** verir; client değeri set ederken bu token'ı geri sunar; memcached token'ı doğrulayarak verinin yazılıp yazılmayacağına karar verir. memcached, lease süresi boyunca o key'e bir **delete** gelip gelmediğini hatırlar — geldiyse **set reddedilir** (stale set çözümü). Herd tarafında memcached token'ı **key başına belirli bir oranda** (rapor edilen: 10 saniyede bir) verir; token alamayan client kısa süre bekleyip tekrar okur, o zamana kadar değer genelde cache'tedir.

**Ölçüm:** leases olmadan tepe DB sorgu hızı **17K/s**, leases ile **1/s**.

⚠️ **DOĞRULANMADI:** Bu bölümün rakamları ve tanımları arama sonuçlarından derlendi — USENIX PDF'i (403) ve tüm mirror'ları bu oturumda fetch edilemedi. Birincil URL: https://www.usenix.org/conference/nsdi13/technical-sessions/presentation/nishtala — "10 saniyede bir token" oranını ve tam alıntıları PDF'ten teyit et. 17K/s → 1/s rakamı birden fazla bağımsız özet kaynağında aynı çıktı.

### Request coalescing — mekanizma mekanizma

**nginx `proxy_cache_lock`** — https://nginx.org/en/docs/http/ngx_http_proxy_module.html (verbatim, default'lar dahil)

| Directive | Default | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| `proxy_cache_lock` | **off** | "only one request at a time will be allowed to populate a new cache element identified according to the proxy_cache_key directive by passing a request to a proxied server. Other requests of the same cache element will either wait for a response to appear in the cache or the cache lock for this element to be released" |
| `proxy_cache_lock_age` | **5s** | "If the last request passed to the proxied server for populating a new cache element has not completed for the specified time, **one more request may be passed** to the proxied server." (Deadlock'a karşı emniyet valfi) |
| `proxy_cache_lock_timeout` | **5s** | Süre dolunca request proxied server'a geçer, **ama response cache'lenmez.** (1.7.8 öncesinde cache'lenebiliyordu.) |
| `proxy_cache_use_stale` | **off** | `error \| timeout \| invalid_header \| updating \| http_500 \| http_502 \| http_503 \| http_504 \| http_403 \| http_404 \| http_429 \| off`. **`updating`** = güncellenmekte olan stale response servis edilebilir → "This allows minimizing the number of accesses to proxied servers when updating cached data." |
| `proxy_cache_background_update` | **off** | "Allows starting a background subrequest to update an expired cache item, while a stale cached response is returned to the client." **Not: `proxy_cache_use_stale updating` de açık olmalı** — ikisi birlikte kurulmazsa sessizce çalışmaz. |
| `proxy_cache_revalidate` | **off** | `If-Modified-Since` / `If-None-Match` ile conditional revalidation |

nginx 1.11.10'dan beri response header'ındaki `stale-while-revalidate` ve `stale-if-error` da destekleniyor, **directive parametrelerinden düşük öncelikli**.

Kritik ayrım: **`proxy_cache_lock` yeni bir cache element doldururken (cold miss)**, `proxy_cache_use_stale updating` ise **var olan bir element güncellenirken** korur. AI kodu genelde ikisinden hiçbirini yazmaz; yazdığında da sadece birini yazar.

**Go `singleflight`** — https://pkg.go.dev/golang.org/x/sync/singleflight
"Package singleflight provides a duplicate function call suppression mechanism." `Group.Do(key, fn)` — verilen key için **aynı anda tek bir execution** in-flight olur; duplicate gelen çağıran orijinalin bitmesini bekler ve **aynı sonucu alır**. Dönüş değeri `shared bool`, `v`'nin birden fazla çağırana verilip verilmediğini gösterir. `DoChan` kanal döner, `Forget(key)` key'i unutturur (gelecek çağrılar orijinali beklemez).
⚠️ **DOĞRULANMADI** — pkg.go.dev bu oturumda fetch edilemedi (önce HTTP 500, sonra domain doğrulama hatası); yukarıdaki ifade arama sonucundaki doc alıntısına dayanıyor. Uyarı: singleflight **tek process içinde** koalese eder; N replika = N origin call. Dağıtık koruma için lock/lease/CDN katmanı gerekir.

**Vercel Request Collapsing** — yukarıda (§2). Region içinde, ISR + Image Optimization için, STALE revalidation'da da geçerli.

**Cloudflare Concurrent Streaming Acceleration** — https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-concurrent-streaming-acceleration/
⚠️ **DOĞRULANMADI** — blog.cloudflare.com bu oturumda fetch edilemedi (domain doğrulama engeli, 3 deneme). Arama sonucundan derlenen mekanizma: cache fill sırasında bekleyen request'ler **key başına cache lock**'ta bloklanmak yerine, **in-flight response stream'ine bağlanır** ve body'yi üretilirken alır. Başarı metriği "cache lock wait time"; bir OTT müşteride P99'da **1.5 saniyeden ~0'a** düşmüş, bu doğrudan 1.5s end-to-end latency kazancı. Live video / Stream Delivery bağlamında duyuruldu. Rakamları ve plan kapsamını blog'dan teyit et.

**Cloudflare Tiered Cache** — coğrafi coalescing: "only the upper-tier can ask the origin" (§3).

---

# DÖKÜM — sorulmayan ama önemli

**Vercel'de sessizce cache'i öldüren şeyler** (AI kodunun rutin olarak ürettiği):
- Response'ta `set-cookie` varsa **hiç cache'lenmez**. Auth middleware'i session cookie refresh ediyorsa tüm cache ölür ve hiçbir yerde hata görünmez.
- Sadece `max-age` yazmak Vercel CDN'de cache'lemez; `s-maxage` şart.
- `CDN-Cache-Control` yoksa Vercel `s-maxage` + `swr`'yi tarayıcıya giderken siler → tarayıcıda gördüğün header, CDN'in gördüğü header değil. Debug ederken bu yanıltır.
- `Authorization` header'lı request hiç cache'lenmez — API'ye Bearer token gönderiyorsan CDN cache'i devre dışıdır.
- Vercel **`stale-if-error`'ı desteklemiyor**. RFC'de var, MDN'de var, Vercel'de yok. AI kodu bunu yazar, hiçbir şey olmaz.

**Vercel Data Cache'in Hobby/Pro tuzağı:** takımdaki **tüm projeler tek cache'i paylaşır** ve tek LRU havuzunu. Bir proje diğerinin entry'lerini evict edebilir, purge herkesinkini siler. Bu bir güvenlik/izolasyon sorunu değil ama performans regresyonunun görünmez kaynağı.

**Data Cache 2MB item limiti** — büyük fetch response'u sessizce cache'lenmez; `unstable_cache`'e büyük JSON koyan kod hiç cache hit almaz ve kimse fark etmez.

**`REVALIDATED` vs `STALE` ayrımı (Vercel):** `revalidateTag()` **lifetime olmadan** çağrılırsa entry **silinir** → sonraki request foreground'da tam latency öder. Lifetime ile çağrılırsa invalidate olur → STALE, arka planda yenilenir. Aynı görünen iki API çağrısı, tamamen farklı latency profili. Bu, "revalidate ettim ama site yavaşladı" şikayetinin kaynağı.

**Cloudflare `Set-Cookie` + cache:** Cloudflare de `Set-Cookie` varsa cache'lemez. Aynı tuzak iki platformda.

**Cloudflare `max-age=0`** cache etmeme sebebi olarak açıkça listeleniyor — `no-cache` ile eşdeğer muamele görüyor.

**Cache Reserve'in 10 saatlik minimum TTL'i** — kısa TTL'li her şey Cache Reserve'e hiç girmez; parasını ödeyip faydasını görmeme riski. Ayrıca `Content-Length` yoksa (chunked/streaming response) eligible değil.

**Redis `volatile-*` + TTL'siz key = `noeviction`** → maxmemory dolunca yazma komutları **hata döner**, sessizce evict etmez. Uygulama 500 vermeye başlar. AI kodu `volatile-lru` yazıp TTL koymayı unutur.

**Redis 32-bit implicit 3GB limiti** — nadir ama küçük container/eski imajlarda ısırır.

**Redis maxmemory replication buffer'ı saymaz** — maxmemory'yi makinenin RAM'ine eşitlersen OOM olursun. `mem_not_counted_for_evict` ile ölçülmeli.

**`EXISTS` miss sayılır** — hit rate metriğini `EXISTS` çağrıları bozar; "cache hit oranımız düşük" teşhisinde bu yanıltır.

**RFC 5861 §3/§4 "warning header" diyor** ama `Warning` header'ı RFC 9111 tarafından deprecate edildi. 2010 tarihli RFC'nin bu kısmı güncel değil. **DOĞRULANMADI** — RFC 9111'in Warning'i kaldıran bölümünü (§5.5 civarı) bu turda primary olarak çekmedim.

**RFC 9111 §4.2.4'ün sert kuralı** başlı başına önemli: cache **disconnected değilse** ve **açık izin yoksa** stale üretemez. `stale-while-revalidate` bu iznin ta kendisi. AI kodu "CDN eski içeriği döner nasılsa" varsayımıyla resilience tasarlar; direktif yazılmadıysa standart bunu yasaklıyor.

**Paper'ın locking eleştirisi mühendislik olarak en değerli kısım:** lock yaklaşımı **write sayısını ikiye katlar** ve **fault-tolerant değildir** (lock'u alan process çökerse lock TTL'i dolana kadar herkes bekler). Bu, "mutex koyarım olur biter" refleksinin bedeli — XFetch'in ekstra write'ı ve tek hata noktası yok.

**XFetch'in gerçekten kritik detayı:** Δ **ölçülür, tahmin edilmez**. Kod her recompute'ta süreyi ölçüp değerle birlikte yazar. Bu yüzden yavaşlayan bir backend otomatik olarak daha erken pre-fetch tetikler — kendini ayarlayan bir sistem. AI'ın ürettiği "probabilistic early expiration" implementasyonları neredeyse her zaman sabit bir pencere kullanır ve bu özelliği kaybeder.

## Erişemediklerim (fetch engellendi / 403)

Oturumun ortasında birçok domain için `Unable to verify if domain ... is safe to fetch` hatası başladı (muhtemelen rate limit). Çekemediklerim:
- **USENIX memcache PDF'i** — 403; cs.cmu.edu, MIT 6.824 notları (TLS cert hatası), usenix.org sayfası hepsi başarısız. Facebook lease bölümü **arama derlemesi**, birincil değil.
- **blog.cloudflare.com** — Concurrent Streaming Acceleration yazısı, 3 deneme, hepsi engellendi.
- **pkg.go.dev / github.com / raw.githubusercontent.com** — singleflight doc'u ve kaynak kodu birincil olarak alınamadı.
- **developers.cloudflare.com/rules/cache-rules/settings/** ve **/cache/how-to/edge-browser-cache-ttl/** — Cache Rules'un tam ayar listesi, Edge TTL modları ("Respect origin TTL" vs override), Browser TTL, "Serve stale content while revalidating" ayarı, plan başına rule limitleri **alınamadı**. Cloudflare tarafındaki en büyük boşluk bu.
- **vercel.com/docs/incremental-static-regeneration** — ISR'nin tam dokümanı çekilemedi; 31 günlük ISR retention rakamı arama snippet'inden, teyit edilmeli.
- **upstash.com/docs/redis/...** ikinci tur — max concurrent connections rakamı **hiçbir yerde yayınlanmamış** görünüyor; troubleshooting sayfası limitin varlığını kabul ediyor ama sayı vermiyor.
- Cloudflare **Workers Cache API** (`caches.default`) doküman sayfasını hiç açamadım — Workers'ta cache yazıyorsan orada ayrı limitler var (özellikle response boyutu ve `cache.put` kısıtları), bu dosyada yok.

## Rapor 26

## HACKER NEWS ÖN SAYFA SPIKE — DOĞRULANMIŞ RAKAMLAR (birincil kaynak, yazarın kendi analytics'i)

**Luke Hsiao, 722 puan / 319 yorum, ~48 saat ön sayfa** — https://luke.hsiao.dev/blog/2023-hn-traffic/
- 7 günde **~160.000 pageview, ~97.600 tekil ziyaretçi**. Tek yazı 152.567 pageview.
- Spike öncesi baseline: **günde 80–200 pageview**. Verbatim: *"My page views before the spike ranged from about 80 to 200 views. There was a sharp drop, and then a slow decline down to about 650 views yesterday."*
- 85 GB bant genişliği (Netlify free tier 100 GB'ın yarısını bir mailde yaktı).
- **Sızıntı oranı feci:** blogu okuyanların yalnızca **%3,3'ü** sitedeki bir sonraki sayfaya (ana sayfa) geçti. Şirket ana sayfasına giden referral: **~110 kişi = yazıyı okuyanların %0,072'si.** Bu, "HN → ürün" hunisinin en net ölçülmüş kaybı.

**Harrison Broadbent, 208 puan / 169 yorum** — https://harrisonbroadbent.com/blog/hacker-news-traffic-spike-anatomy/
- 20 saatte **15.000 tekil ziyaretçi, 18.000 pageview**; pik 308 eşzamanlı kullanıcı, 1.750 pageview/saat (gönderimden 3 saat sonra).
- Verbatim: *"Over 50% of the traffic spike has already passed"* — gönderimden **8 saat sonra**.
- Sonraki **72 saatte toplam sadece 3.000 pageview** (yani kuyruk, pikin ~%17'si).
- 233 GB trafik, Netlify'da **110 $ fatura**. Yan ürün: 150+ backlink, 20+ tweet.

**RoyalSloth, iki yazı aynı anda ön sayfada** — https://blog.royalsloth.eu/posts/how-much-traffic-comes-from-the-front-page-of-hackernews/
- İlk 20 saat ~85.000 istek / ~43.000 tekil. **Ön sayfadaki her yazı ≈ 20.000 tekil istek.**
- Gün 1: 93.275 istek → **Gün 2: 46.390 istek** (yaklaşık **%50 düşüş, tek günde**).
- Sunucu maliyeti 3,04 €/ay Hetzner, CPU %0–5'te boşta durdu. (Ölçek algısı için: bu trafik altyapısal olarak hiçbir şey değil.)

**Marco TM, 68 puan, ön sayfada 17. sıra, ~1 saat** — https://marcotm.com/articles/stats-of-being-on-the-hacker-news-front-page/
- Ön sayfadayken **dakikada 35–40 tekil ziyaretçi**; ikinci sayfaya düşünce **dakikada 10**.
- Bu maruziyetten toplam **3.500–4.000 ziyaretçi**; 4 günde kümülatif ~8.000.
- Baş parmak kuralı olarak verdiği tavan: dakikada 50 tekil = saatte 3.000 = günde 72.000 (bu bir **ekstrapolasyon**, ölçüm değil).

**Nick Lafferty, ~#5 sıra, 270 yorum** — https://nicklafferty.com/blog/what-happens-when-you-re-on-the-front-page-of-hacker-news/
- **18.000 ziyaretçi** (normal günü 50 ziyaretçi → **360x spike**).
- Dönüşüm çıktısı: **100+ newsletter abonesi, 400+ LinkedIn profil görüntüleme, 15 bağlantı isteği.** 18.000 → 100 abone = **%0,55**.

**Aidlab (Show HN postmortem, IH)** — https://www.indiehackers.com/post/front-page-of-hn-the-full-postmortem-traffic-lessons-surprises-cbe9e0a7f6
- Bozunma eğrisi verbatim: *"Oct 13 (launch day): ~468 active users (350 from HN and GitHub). Oct 14-16: around 100-150 (~30 from HN). Then a slow decline to a long tail of a few dozen daily visits for about a week."*
- Ürün sayfalarına 170+ tekil ziyaretçi, **doğrudan satış: 0**. Müşteri geri bildirimi: 0. Kazanım: 4 inbound sorgu (araştırma ekipleri), 8 LinkedIn daveti (3'ü VC).

**HN spike özeti:** #1/ön sayfa = 15k–100k ziyaretçi bandı; trafiğin **yarısı ilk 8 saatte** biter, gün 2 gün 1'in ~%50'si, 72 saat sonra kuyruk pikin %10–17'si. Ürüne dönüşüm ölçülen her vakada **%0,07–%0,6** arası.

## PRODUCT HUNT — TEK TEK POSTMORTEM RAKAMLARI

**Leadspicker / Tomas Blatak — bu dosyanın en sert veri noktası** (birincil analiz: Tetriz.io, https://www.tetriz.io/blog/is-product-hunt-dying/)
- Haz 2023: featured, **300 upvote, günün 7. ürünü → 91 ödeme yapan müşteri.**
- Eyl 2024: featured DEĞİL, günün büyük kısmında en çok upvote alan, **612 upvote → 1 ödeme yapan müşteri.** Verbatim: *"Guess how many paying customers Leadspicker got from the launch? Only one!"*
- Yani upvote 2x arttı, müşteri **91'den 1'e** düştü. Değişen şey ürün değil, platform.

**Syften kurucusunun derlediği eski launch raporları** — https://syften.com/blog/product-hunt-launch/
- *"One maker reported **$74.55 over four days from 1000 visitors**"*
- *"**450 upvotes, 100 signups, and 2000 visitors, but no paying customers**"*
- *"Task Pigeon reported nearly **2000 visitors and 138 signups**, again with no clear revenue result"*
- *"An Indie Hacker reported **1023 visits, 102 accounts, and 2 paying customers at $5/month** after emailing 3830 users"* → 10 $/ay MRR.
- Yazarın kendisi: *"I got over 1000 visitors myself. Tens of curious hunters signed up. I spent hours talking with them. **None of them paid.**"*

**Edward Sturm, 5 launch, gün gün trafik** — https://edwardsturm.com/articles/product-hunt-results/
- Speek, #8: **590 tekil** (önceki gün 18) → ertesi gün **421**.
- Reverb, #9: gün 1 **1.755 tekil**, 481 yönlendirme → gün 2 **114 yeni kullanıcı**. Sonuç: **7 işletme** beta için yazdı.
- Commit Club #5: **286 ziyaretçi → 10 kayıt, 1 ödeyen.**
- Fintech First Users, #3-5 bandı — **7 günlük tam bozunma eğrisi:** 826 → 296 → 268 → 483 → 379 → 197 → **106**. (Gün 2 = gün 1'in %36'sı; gün 7 = **%12,8**.)
- Commit Club 2. launch, **#2 (ChatGPT'ye kaybetti)**: 7 günde 1.317 ziyaretçi → **85 kullanıcı, dönüşüm %6,45**.

**Oxlo.ai, ilk launch'ta #2 (ilk 4 saatte #1)** — https://medium.com/@info_94282/we-hit-2-on-product-hunt-as-first-time-launchers-heres-what-it-actually-brought-us-9c977e87eb6c
- Launch günü ve sonraki 2 gün kayıt geldi. Verbatim: ***"But nothing converted. That was the hard part."*** #2 olmasına rağmen newsletter'a girmedi, featured edilmedi. Sayısal metrik paylaşmıyorlar — kendi ifadeleriyle *"Operation success, patient failed."*

**IH — hayal kırıklığı launch'ı, #8** — https://www.indiehackers.com/post/aftermath-of-our-disappointing-product-hunt-launch-a81fea3a52
- 2 günde **564 ziyaret → 56 kayıt** (%9,9 kayıt dönüşümü, gelir yok). 12.000 takipçili bir hunter kullanmalarına rağmen bir önceki launch'ın **yarısı kadar** trafik.

**Dip noktası vakaları (sıfıra yakın):** 2 upvote / 0 satış (https://www.indiehackers.com/post/launched-on-product-hunt-yesterday-2-upvotes-0-sales-what-the-data-actually-said-259e99ca68), 1 kayıt / 0 $ MRR (https://www.indiehackers.com/post/product-hunt-is-done-i-have-1-sign-up-and-0-mrr-heres-how-i-m-thinking-about-finding-my-first-paying-customer-a057ad34dc), 4 hafta hazırlık / 3 upvote (https://dev.to/ykiki/4-weeks-1-saas-3-upvotes-an-honest-product-hunt-postmortem-53h6), ~200 upvote'ta takılıp bir mutfak blenderının altında bitirmek (https://www.indiehackers.com/post/notes-from-a-failed-product-hunt-launch-1332b8cade).

**Retention'ın gerçek şekli — itch.io launch haftası postmortem'i** (PH + Show HN aynı gün) — https://itch.io/t/6645181/
- *"~100 players in 2.5 days (**55 new on launch day, halving each day since**)... And the hard one: **D1 retention is 2%**. Yes, two percent."*
- Kalanların oturumu 11,6 dk'dan 80 dk'ya çıkıyor — yani spike, ürünü seven %2'yi bulmuyor, geri kalan %98'i bir kere deneyip gidiyor. Show HN'i **vote manipulation** şüphesiyle saatler içinde flaglendi (launch linkini etrafta paylaştığı için).

## AGREGAT VERİ (platform seviyesi)

**Tetriz.io, featured oranının çöküşü — birincil analiz** — https://www.tetriz.io/blog/is-product-hunt-dying/
- Eyl 2023: günde ortalama **47 launch featured** → Eyl 2024: günde **16** (%66 düşüş).
- *"Last year between 60% and 98% of launches were featured. The share dropped to 25% from the 25th of January [2024]. In September 2024 only 10% of launches are featured."*
- Yani 2026'da PH'e çıkmanın beklenen değeri **%10 featured olma olasılığıyla çarpılıyor**. Featured değilseniz Tetriz'in tavsiyesi: *"stopping promoting your launch"* — ilk 2 saatte featured olup olmadığınıza bakıp bırakın.
- Ahrefs tahminlerine göre PH'in organik trafiği **Tem 2023'te zirve** yaptı, o günden beri düşüyor.

**Tetriz, 60+ startup'a launch yaptırmış danışman, churn üzerine** — https://www.tetriz.io/blog/what-to-do-after-a-product-hunt-launch/
- *"It is quite common for users from Product Hunt to **churn after 1 month**, so you want to check the retention data for this cohort."* — 1/2/3 aylık churn'e bakmadan PH reklamına (min. **1.000 $/ay**) girmeyin diyor.
- Featured bir launch'ın taban beklentisi: *"at least **300 visits** to your website"*.

**MySignature.io anketi (2024-25, "dozens of services" — top 10'a girmiş launch'lar)** — https://mysignature.io/blog/product-hunt-launch/
- Kullanıcı kazanım dağılımı: **%24,5 → 100-500 kullanıcı; %26,5 → 500-1.000; %22,4 → 1.000-2.000; sadece %10,2 → 2.000-5.000.**
- **%16'sı kayıtlarda hiçbir artış görmediğini** söylüyor (%50 "biraz artış", %30 "ciddi artış").
- **%42'si satışta artış gördü** (yani çoğunluk görmedi); %56'sı hiç basın/medya ilgisi almadı.
- %56 memnun, %61 tavsiye ediyor. Yazarın kendi uyarısı: 1.000-10.000 kullanıcı bile **250 saat hazırlık** harcandıysa başarı sayılır mı?
- Aynı yazıda kurucu itirafı: PH trafiğinin dönüşümü ürüne göre **beklenenden %30 yukarı ile %50 aşağı** arasında savruluyor.

## DOĞRULANMADI — zayıf/ikincil kaynaklı, ama saklıyorum (çoğu SEO-slop, muhtemelen uydurma)

- **"Sıraya göre trafik tablosu"** (#1 = 5.000-10.000+ ziyaretçi / 50-300 lead; #5 = 2.000-4.000; #10 = 1.000-2.500; alt sıra = 500-1.000; hepsi %1-3 lead dönüşümü) — https://awesome-directories.com/blog/product-hunt-launch-guide-2025-algorithm-changes/ "Demand Curve analizi" diyor ama **Demand Curve'ün PH playbook URL'i 404 veriyor**, doğrulayamadım. **DOĞRULANMADI.**
- **"Gün 2 = gün 1'in %20-36'sı, gün 3-7 = %10'un altı"** — aynı sayfa bunu MySignature anketine atfediyor, **MySignature yazısında böyle bir veri yok** (ben kaynağı çekip baktım). **DOĞRULANMADI — yanlış atıf.** Ancak Edward Sturm'un gerçek 7 günlük eğrisi (826→296→...→106) bu aralıkla tesadüfen uyuşuyor, yani rakam yanlış değil ama kaynağı sahte.
- **"82% of SaaS products see traffic return to pre-launch levels within 2 weeks"** — tek kaynağı https://prems.ai/blog/product-hunt-launch-strategy-2026, hiçbir birincil atıf yok. **DOĞRULANMADI, muhtemelen uydurma.** Bu cümleyi dışarıda kullanma.
- **"PH dönüşümü %3,1 vs Indie Hackers %23,1" (OpenHunts 2024)** ve **"PH SaaS için %1-6, n=1.077 (MySignature)"** — https://www.shno.co/marketing-statistics/product-hunt-launch-statistics ve awesome-directories üzerinden dolaşıyor. **n=1.077 iddiası yanlış:** MySignature kendi yazısında *"we surveyed dozens of services"* diyor. Aggregator sayıyı şişirmiş. **DOĞRULANMADI.**
- **"Top launch'lar ziyaretçinin %15-25'ini kayda, kayıtların %35-45'ini 7 günde aktivasyona, aktiflerin %5-10'unu 30 günde ödemeye çeviriyor"** — https://blog.innmind.com/how-to-launch-on-product-hunt-in-2026/ ve türevleri; hiçbir veri seti/örneklem yok. **DOĞRULANMADI.** Elimdeki gerçek postmortem'ler (2.000 ziyaretçi → 0 ödeyen; 1.023 → 2 ödeyen) bu bandın çok altında.
- **"Reply.io: 2 günde 10.000 ziyaretçi, 600 kayıt, ~60 ödeyen müşteri, ~60.000 $ LTV"** — sadece ikincil derlemelerde geçiyor, Reply.io'nun kendi yazısını bulamadım. **DOĞRULANMADI.** (Doğruysa bile 10.000 → 60 ödeyen = **%0,6**.)
- **"Günde ortalama 11 ürün launch ediliyor" (Waitlister, Eyl 2024)** — shno.co üzerinden. Tetriz'in aynı ayda **16 ürün featured** demesiyle çelişiyor (featured sayısı toplam sayıdan büyük olamaz). **DOĞRULANMADI, muhtemelen "featured" ile karışmış.**
- HN thread'i "Product Hunt Upvotes Are Worthless" (54 puan, https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=40844727) — sayı yok ama bir anekdot kayda değer: *"A founder once shared that launching on producthunt, getting to #1 and acquiring thousands of users ultimately **killed his company**. He explained that it gave them a **false sense of product market fit** that they fundraised against."* İkinci el anlatı, **DOĞRULANMADI**, ama tezin en keskin ifadesi.
- Ayrıca aynı ürünle ilgili: PH'te **ücretli upvote / "supportive review" talebi** spam'i — bir kullanıcı tek gönderiden sonra **4 saat içinde 3 ayrı teklif** aldığını yazıyor. İlgili eski thread: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=30509760 (Tell HN: Product Hunt is full of shill accounts, paid upvotes and spam).

## SORULMADI AMA ÖNEMLİ (döküm)

1. **Spike'ın maliyeti negatif olabiliyor.** Harrison Broadbent HN spike'ından **110 $ Netlify faturası** aldı, sıfır gelir. Statik site + CDN değilsen 15k ziyaretçi para yakar.
2. **Analytics spike'ı sistematik olarak eksik ölçüyor.** HN kitlesinin büyük kısmı ad-blocker kullanıyor; Broadbent bandwidth/sayfa-boyutundan geri hesaplayıp gerçek sayıyı **~30.000** buluyor (ölçtüğü 15k'nın 2 katı). Yani "kaç kişi geldi" bile güvenilir değil; "kaç kişi kaldı" tek anlamlı metrik.
3. **PH'te ödül artık sıralama değil, featured olmak.** Leadspicker vakası bunu tek başına kanıtlıyor: featured+300 upvote (91 müşteri) > featured-değil+612 upvote (1 müşteri). Sıralama peşinde koşmak yanlış oyun.
4. **SEO/backlink, launch'ın en dayanıklı çıktısı.** Sturm 5 launch'ın hepsinde arama sıralamasında kalıcı yükseliş bildiriyor; Broadbent **150+ backlink** aldı. Spike ölür, backlink kalır. Launch'ı "müşteri kanalı" değil "backlink + press-release ikamesi" olarak fiyatlamak elimdeki veriyle tutarlı tek konum.
5. **Show HN'de kendi linkini paylaşmak flag yedirir.** itch.io postmortem'i tam bunu yaşadı. Launch koordinasyonu yaparken bu operasyonel bir risk.
6. **Kurucular kötü sayıyı yayınlamıyor — seçilim yanlılığı devasa.** PH'in kendi forumundaki "gerçek dönüşümünüz neydi?" thread'inde (https://www.producthunt.com/p/general/what-s-your-real-conversion-outcome-from-a-product-hunt-launch) **tek bir kurucu bile somut sayı vermedi**; en somutu *"there wasn't much in terms of statistical increases either"*. Yani yayınlanmış medyan, gerçek medyandan yukarıdadır.

## BULAMADIKLARIM (boşluklar)

- **Product Hunt'ın kendi yayınladığı resmî istatistik yok.** Ortalama top-5 trafiği, günlük toplam launch sayısı, dönüşüm — PH bunları yayınlamıyor; her "PH verisi" üçüncü taraf scrape'i (Tetriz, Waitlister, OpenHunts) ve hiçbiri metodoloji yayınlamıyor.
- **Kohort bazlı 7/30/90 gün retention'ı tam olarak yayınlayan tek bir PH postmortem'i bulamadım.** En yakını itch.io'nun D1 %2'si ve Aidlab'ın gün gün aktif kullanıcısı. 30/90 günlük PH kohort eğrisi literatürde **yok** — kurucular ya ölçmüyor ya yayınlamıyor.
- **Indie Hackers / MicroConf'un launch kanalı üzerine yayınlanmış anketi bulunamadı** (MicroConf State of Independent SaaS raporları var ama PH launch spike'ını ayrı kırmıyor — bu turda erişemedim, ayrıca kazılabilir).
- **Erişemediğim kaynaklar (fetch bloklu):** thehackstack.substack.com "A successful Product Hunt launch: The Numbers", taishi.substack.com (#1 PoD sonrası "2 hafta sonra trafik öldü, MRR 0 $" iddiası — ikinci elden geldi, **DOĞRULANMADI**), happysupport.ai'ın "5 founders, 8 questions" derlemesi (7 günlük kayıt 71–450, medyan ~115; ödeme dönüşümü %0–20, 4'ün 3'ü ≤%4 — bu rakamlar arama snippet'inden, **birincil doğrulama yapılamadı**).
- **Klasik (2015-2018) "PH bize hiçbir şey getirmedi" yazılarının çoğu link-rot olmuş**; Syften'ın derlediği 4 rapor bu döneme ait ama orijinal URL'leri yazıda verilmemiş.

## Rapor 27

Araştırma tamam. İşte bölüm bölüm sonuçlar.
