## A) ASYMMETRIC JWT SIGNING KEYS

**Primary:** https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/signing-keys · https://supabase.com/blog/jwt-signing-keys (published **14 July 2025**) · https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/jwts

### Algorithms
| Alg | Detail |
|---|---|
| **ES256** | NIST P-256 curve. Docs: *"Elliptic Curves are a faster alternative than RSA, while providing comparable security."* Recommended. |
| **RS256** | RSA 2048. Widely supported, slower; docs steer you to P-256. |
| **HS256** | HMAC shared secret (legacy). *"Not recommended for production applications"* — revocation and compliance problems. |

### JWKS endpoint
```
GET https://<project-id>.supabase.co/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json
```
Cached **10 minutes** at Supabase's edge, plus client-side caching. Docs warn: *"It's important to be aware of the cache expiry time to prevent unintentionally rejecting valid user access tokens"* — and to **wait at least 20 minutes before revoking a signing key**.

### Rotation flow (4 states)
`Standby` → `In Use` → `Previously Used` → `Revoked`. Docs: *"Each action on a key is reversible (except permanent deletion)."* And: *"If you've made a mistake or need more time to adjust your application, you can move a revoked key to standby."* **So yes, reversible.**

### Why it matters — local verification
The blog names three symmetric-JWT problems: (1) you had to call `supabase.auth.getUser()` to validate a session, adding a network round trip; (2) server-side apps depended on Auth-server availability, which *"slow[s] edge-hosted applications"*; (3) shared secrets are a compliance risk — a leaked secret lets an attacker forge tokens and bypass RLS entirely. The payoff: *"You can now verify tokens at any place in your application, without relying on the Auth server."*

**No latency numbers published.** The July 2025 announcement contains no ms figures — only qualitative "faster." The JWT docs do add a directional warning: avoid HS256 verification from edge-located servers because *"latency to your regional Auth server can be substantial"*; route to geographically-aligned infra instead. **DOĞRULANMADI: any published Supabase benchmark in ms.**

### `getClaims()`
`supabase.auth.getClaims()` replaces `getUser()` for verification. With symmetric keys it still contacts the Auth server; with asymmetric keys it *"use[s] the Web Crypto API to verify tokens directly,"* auto-discovering and caching the public key from JWKS. Caveat, verbatim: *"The supabase.auth.getClaims() method is meant to be used only with JWTs issued by Supabase Auth."* Custom/third-party JWTs need a normal JWT library (e.g. `jose`).

### Migration path from the legacy shared secret
1. Import the existing legacy HS256 secret into the new key system (project keeps using it).
2. Supabase auto-generates an asymmetric **standby** key pair.
3. **Rotate** — new JWTs are signed asymmetrically; **users stay signed in**, existing JWTs stay valid.
4. **Revoke** the legacy secret after the token expiry window. After this, *"Only JWTs signed with the new asymmetric key will be accepted."*

**What breaks:** anything verifying with the legacy secret directly (hand-rolled `jwt.verify(token, SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET)` — extremely common in vibecoded backends). Docs: *"Make sure your app does not directly rely on the legacy JWT secret."* Also **Edge Functions with the "Verify JWT" setting must be reconfigured.**

### Defaults timeline (relevant to "which project am I on?")
- Asymmetric key support first shipped **7 October 2024** (Q4 2024).
- Projects created after **1 May 2025** got an RSA asymmetric key by default.
- **From 1 October 2025, all new projects use asymmetric JWTs by default.**
Source: https://supabase.com/changelog/29289-supabase-auth-asymmetric-keys-support-in-2025 (via search index — I could not fetch the changelog page directly, **DOĞRULANMADI on exact wording**, dates consistent across two Supabase-hosted sources).

---

## B) API KEY RENAME: anon → publishable, service_role → secret

**Primary:** https://supabase.com/docs/guides/api/api-keys · https://supabase.com/docs/guides/getting-started/api-keys · https://supabase.com/docs/guides/getting-started/migrating-to-new-api-keys · https://github.com/orgs/supabase/discussions/29260

| Type | Format | Privilege | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Publishable | `sb_publishable_...` | Low | *"Safe to expose online: web page, mobile or desktop app"* |
| Secret | `sb_secret_...` | Elevated | *"full access to your project's data, bypassing Row Level Security"* |
| `anon` (legacy) | JWT | Low | older version of publishable |
| `service_role` (legacy) | JWT | Elevated | older version of secret |

**Are publishable keys JWTs? No.** This is the semantic change — the new keys are opaque tokens, not JWTs. Enforcement still runs through the Postgres roles `anon` / `authenticated` + RLS; the key itself no longer carries claims. Secret keys map to `service_role`, which has the Postgres `BYPASSRLS` attribute.

**Three things that break (from the migration guide, verbatim):**
1. *"You can't send a publishable or secret key in the `Authorization: Bearer ...` header. Send it on the `apikey` header instead."*
2. Edge Functions *"don't verify the `apikey` header for the new keys. Use `verify_jwt = false` and authorize in code."*
3. *"Public Realtime connections are limited to 24 hours unless the connection is upgraded with user-level authentication through Supabase Auth or a supported third-party auth provider."*

**Coexistence:** *"Creating publishable and secret keys adds them alongside your existing `anon` and `service_role` keys without affecting them — your legacy keys keep working."* Disabling legacy keys is a separate, explicit Dashboard action (Settings → API Keys). Incremental migration, no big-bang cutover.

### Deprecation timeline (from discussion #29260)
| Date | Milestone (verbatim where quoted) |
|---|---|
| Jun 2025 | *"Early preview and Introduction of new API keys."* New projects get both; existing projects opt in manually. |
| Jul 2025 | *"Full feature launch of new API keys."* Dashboard + docs pivot to new keys. |
| **1 Nov 2025** | *"We will start sending you monthly reminders to migrate off legacy API keys."* Projects **restored** after this date come back **without** legacy `anon`/`service_role`. New projects no longer have them available. |
| **Late 2026 (TBC)** | *"Legacy API keys will be deleted and removed from the Docs / Dashboard."* App breaks if unmigrated. |

**Status as of Aug 2026:** the only date the docs commit to is *"deprecated by the end of 2026."* The final removal date is still marked **TBC** in Supabase's own announcement — there is **no hard, confirmed day-level cutoff**. The "December 31, 2026" figure that circulates is an inference from "end of 2026," not a Supabase-stated date — **DOĞRULANMADI**. Search-index evidence says monthly migration reminders were live as of **March 2026** (**DOĞRULANMADI** — secondary, I could not open a Supabase page confirming it).

Historical note: this slipped once. In Dec 2024 a maintainer wrote *"Changes to Supabase API Keys will not be released in Q4 2024 because it needs further development work."* Treat "late 2026" as soft.

## C) AUTH RATE LIMITS — FULL TABLE, VERBATIM

**Primary:** https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/rate-limits

| Operation | Path | Limited By | Customizable | Limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Endpoints that trigger email sends | `/auth/v1/signup` `/auth/v1/recover` `/auth/v1/user` | Sum of combined requests project-wide | **Custom SMTP only** | **2 emails per hour** with the built-in email provider. *"You can only change this with a custom SMTP setup."* Limit applies to `/auth/v1/user` only when updating the email address. |
| Send One-Time-Passwords (OTP) | `/auth/v1/otp` | Sum of combined requests project-wide | Yes | Defaults to **30 OTPs per hour** |
| Send OTPs or magic links | `/auth/v1/otp` | Last request of the user | Yes | Defaults to **60 second** window before a new request to the same user |
| Signup confirmation request | `/auth/v1/signup` | Last request of the user | Yes | Defaults to **60 second** window |
| Password reset request | `/auth/v1/recover` | Last request of the user | Yes | Defaults to **60 second** window |
| Verification requests | `/auth/v1/verify` | IP Address | **No** | **360 per hour** (bursts up to 30) |
| Token refresh requests | `/auth/v1/token` | IP Address | **No** | **1800 per hour** (bursts up to 30) |
| Create or verify an MFA challenge | `/auth/v1/factors/:id/challenge` `/auth/v1/factors/:id/verify` | IP Address | **No** | **15 per hour** (bursts up to 30) |
| Anonymous sign-ins | `/auth/v1/signup` | IP Address | **No** | **30 per hour** (bursts up to 30). Only applies when called **without** email or phone in the body |

**Correction to your brief:** you said 4/hour for built-in SMTP. **The docs today say 2 emails per hour.** Supabase lowered this (it was 4/hour, and 30/hour before that). If you're quoting a number publicly, quote **2**.

Configurable = the top five (project-wide email/OTP counts and the per-user 60s windows). Hard caps = the four IP-based ones (`/verify`, `/token`, MFA, anonymous sign-in) — **not adjustable at all**. SMS limits are not in this table version; **DOĞRULANMADI: current SMS-per-hour default.**

The `/auth/v1/token` = 1800/hr per IP cap is the one that bites vibecoded apps behind a shared NAT/corporate proxy or a server-side render layer where every user shares one egress IP.

## D) RLS PITFALLS

**Primary:** https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/postgres/row-level-security · https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/database-linter · https://supabase.com/blog/supabase-security-2025-retro

### The core sentence
*"A table in an exposed schema without RLS is readable and writable by anyone with your publishable key."* The `public` schema is exposed by default.

**Table Editor auto-enables RLS. Raw SQL / SQL Editor does not.** This is the exact gap AI codegen falls into — the model writes `create table ...` in a migration and never emits `alter table ... enable row level security;`.

### Grants ≠ policies (under-known)
Two independent checks: **grants** decide whether a role may perform the operation at all; **policies** restrict which rows. A table protected only by policies is still vulnerable if grants were never revoked. You must set both.

### service_role
*"It bypasses RLS, so keep it server-side."* Postgres `BYPASSRLS` attribute. Its replacement `sb_secret_...` is identical in blast radius.

### Security Advisor lint rules (the "Splinter" linter, open source)
Full rule list, ERROR-level ones bolded:

| ID | Rule | Level |
|---|---|---|
| 0001 | unindexed_foreign_keys | INFO |
| **0002** | **auth_users_exposed** — user data exposed through a view | **ERROR** |
| 0003 | auth_rls_initplan | WARN |
| 0004 | no_primary_key | INFO |
| 0005 | unused_index | INFO |
| 0006 | multiple_permissive_policies | WARN |
| 0007 | policy_exists_rls_disabled | INFO |
| 0008 | rls_enabled_no_policy | INFO |
| 0009 | duplicate_index | WARN |
| **0010** | **security_definer_view** — view bypasses RLS | **ERROR** |
| 0011 | function_search_path_mutable | WARN |
| 0012 | auth_allow_anonymous_sign_ins | INFO |
| **0013** | **rls_disabled_in_public** — table publicly accessible | **ERROR** |
| 0014 | extension_in_public | WARN |
| **0015** | **rls_references_user_metadata** | **ERROR** |
| 0016 | materialized_view_in_api | WARN |
| 0017 | foreign_table_in_api | WARN |
| 0018 | unsupported_reg_types | WARN |
| **0019** | **insecure_queue_exposed_in_api** | **ERROR** |
| 0020 | table_bloat | WARN |
| **0021** | **fkey_to_auth_unique** | **ERROR** |
| 0022 | extension_versions_outdated | WARN |
| **0023** | **sensitive_columns_exposed** | **ERROR** |
| 0024 | permissive_rls_policy | WARN |
| 0025 | public_bucket_allows_listing | WARN |
| 0026 | pg_graphql_anon_table_exposed | WARN |
| 0027 | pg_graphql_authenticated_table_exposed | WARN |
| 0028 | anon_security_definer_function_executable | WARN |
| 0029 | authenticated_security_definer_function_executable | WARN |

Key texts: **0013** — *"Anyone with your project URL can read, edit, and delete all data in this table because Row-Level Security is not enabled."* **0010** — *"A view in the public schema runs with elevated privileges and ignores Row-Level Security."* Fix with `with (security_invoker=on)`. **0002** — views referencing `auth.users` bypass RLS by default.

### What Supabase has shipped in response (Security Retro 2025)
- Dashboard-created tables get RLS on by default; **warning labels** on RLS-off tables; **email alerts to project owners** when unprotected tables are created.
- Dashboard Assistant generates RLS policy SQL from plain-text requirements.
- **GitHub Secret Scanning integration**: secret keys found in public GitHub repos are *"immediately revoke[d]"* with owner notification. This is the direct answer to leaked-service_role-in-a-public-repo.
- Weekly security emails to org owners with advisor findings; advisor exposed via the **Supabase MCP server**.
- HackerOne VDP: **139 reports resolved from 96 researchers**; **42 reports in the last 90 days**; **median first response 8 hours**.

Supabase has **not** published a named study measuring how many public Supabase projects have RLS off. **DOĞRULANMADI** — the retro is a posture/mitigation post, not measurement.

### Third-party measurement of RLS-off in the wild
| Claim | Number | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lovable-generated apps scanned | **303 endpoints across 170 projects** readable by anyone with the anon key | Matt Palmer / Matan Getz, CVE-2025-48757 | Primary researcher; the 303/170 figure comes from secondary write-ups summarising his scan — **DOĞRULANMADI at that granularity** (his own post at mattpalmer.io does **not** publish sample size; it says "all versions" affected) |
| Lovable apps with RLS disabled on ≥1 table | **70%** | "a 2025 analysis," unattributed | **DOĞRULANMADI** — no primary source located; do not quote |
| Vibe-coded apps with serious vulns/misconfigs | **20%** ("1 in 5 organizations") | Wiz Research, 16 Sept 2025 | Vendor research, **wiz.io blocked**, figure consistent across 4 secondary sources — **DOĞRULANMADI on methodology/sample** |
| "91.5% of vibe-coded apps have vulnerabilities," Q1 2026 | 91.5% | softwareseni.com | **DOĞRULANMADI** — content-marketing blog, no visible methodology. Do not quote. |
| "380,000 apps expose corporate data" | 380k | ai2.work | **DOĞRULANMADI** — do not quote |

**Separate vendor marketing from measurement:** the Wiz 20% figure is vendor research done *in collaboration with Lovable* — the four categories it names are client-side authentication, hardcoded secrets, insecure data-access policies, and exposure of internal applications. Useful taxonomy, but the denominator is Wiz's own customer telemetry, not a random sample of the internet.

## E) BASE44 AUTH BYPASS — Wiz Research, July 2025

**Post URL:** https://www.wiz.io/blog/critical-vulnerability-base44 — **I could not fetch it; wiz.io is blocked in this environment.** Everything below is reconstructed from SecurityWeek and Veracode (both fetched) plus search-index extracts of The Hacker News and Dark Reading. **Treat the endpoint paths as DOĞRULANMADI-by-me** — but they appear identically worded across THN and Dark Reading extracts, which both quote the Wiz post.

### Mechanism
Two authentication endpoints were exposed with **no authentication and no authorization checks**:
```
POST api/apps/{app_id}/auth/register     # register a new user: email + password
POST api/apps/{app_id}/auth/verify-otp   # verify the user with a one-time password
```
The `app_id` is **not a secret**: it is *"hardcoded in each application's URI and manifest.json file path"* — visible in the app URL and in `/manifest.json`. So the entire attack is: read the app_id off the public URL → POST to the register endpoint → POST to verify-otp → you now hold a **verified** account inside a private app.

### Why SSO didn't help
The bypass sat *underneath* the auth layer. Registration succeeded **even when user registration was disabled** on the app. Because Base44 treated a verified account as authorized, the enterprise SSO gate was never consulted. Net result per Wiz: it *"bypasses all authentication controls, including Single Sign-On (SSO) protections, granting full access to all the private applications and data contained within them."*

### Scope
- Wiz confirmed the flaw against *"several enterprise applications."*
- Base44 is *"reportedly used by thousands of enterprises"*; the founder had reported **20,000+ users** earlier in 2025.
- Affected app types: internal HR systems, private knowledge bases, company chatbots, internal comms — containing PII.
- **Wiz did not publish a count of affected apps or users.** The "could have affected every app built on the platform" framing (TechRepublic headline) is journalistic, not a Wiz number. **DOĞRULANMADI: any specific app/user count.**

### Timeline
| Date | Event |
|---|---|
| ~mid-June 2025 | Wix acquires Base44 |
| **9 July 2025** | Wiz Research discovers the flaw and responsibly discloses |
| **within 24 hours** | Wix patches |
| ~end July 2025 | Wiz publishes |

**Abuse:** Wix's investigation found *"it had not been exploited in the wild prior to the fix being rolled out."* I could not locate a standalone Wix/Base44 official response page — no verbatim Wix statement URL found. **DOĞRULANMADI.** Wiz researcher quote that did survive: *"What made this vulnerability particularly concerning was its simplicity – requiring only basic API knowledge to exploit."* Base44's own auth docs: https://docs.base44.com/developers/skills/base44-sdk/references/auth

### Related: Lovable / CVE-2025-48757 (the Supabase RLS one)
**NVD:** https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-48757
- Description, verbatim: *"An insufficient database Row-Level Security policy in Lovable through 2025-04-15 allows remote unauthenticated attackers to read or write to arbitrary database tables."*
- **CVSS 9.3 CRITICAL** (`CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:C/C:H/I:L/A:N`), score from MITRE as CNA — **NVD has not done its own assessment**. CWE-863 Incorrect Authorization. Published **29 May 2025**. Tagged **"Disputed"** and "Exclusively Hosted Service."
- **The vendor disputes it**, arguing customers bear responsibility for protecting their own application data.
- Researcher timeline (mattpalmer.io): discovered **20 Mar 2025**, vendor notified **21 Mar 2025**, acknowledged **24 Mar 2025**, Lovable's own disclosure **15 Apr 2025**, public disclosure **29 May 2025** (~70 days). **Patch: "None available."**
- Exposure classes: PII from user tables, API keys and third-party access tokens, financial/transaction/subscription records.
- Refs: https://mattpalmer.io/posts/CVE-2025-48757/ · https://mattpalmer.io/posts/statement-on-CVE-2025-48757/ · https://docs.lovable.dev/changelog · https://gist.github.com/lhchavez/625ee42a6c408a850d35e50f8e649de9

### Related: Guardio "VibeScamming" (April 2025)
**Primary:** https://guard.io/labs/vibescamming-from-prompt-to-phish-benchmarking-popular-ai-agents-resistance-to-the-dark-side
VibeScamming Benchmark v1.0 scored three systems on resistance to being driven into building phishing infrastructure. **Higher = more resistant.** **ChatGPT 8/10, Claude 4.3/10, Lovable 1.8/10.** Guardio's framing: Lovable *"stood out in all the wrong ways"* — pixel-perfect scam pages, live hosting, evasion techniques, and admin dashboards to track stolen credentials, all from prompts. Claude *"started with solid pushback but proved easily persuadable"* under "ethical"/"security research" framing. Lovable later partnered with Guardio to add a safe-browsing engine.

**No Wiz research found specifically on Vercel v0 or Bolt.** The Sept 2025 Wiz piece names Lovable, Bolt and Base44 as the platform category but the collaboration and remediation guidance were with **Lovable** only. **DOĞRULANMADI: any dedicated v0 or Bolt security research from Wiz.**

---

## BULDUĞUM AMA SORMADIĞIN ŞEYLER

**1. Lovable had a SECOND, much bigger incident — April 2026, four months ago. This is the freshest and most quotable thing in the whole sweep.**
- **BOLA** (Broken Object Level Authorization). Same researcher: **Matt Palmer** (@weezerOSINT).
- Reported to Lovable via HackerOne **3 March 2026**. Publicly disclosed on X **21 April 2026** after waiting **48 days**.
- Verbatim from the researcher: *"I made a Lovable account today and was able to access another user's source code, database credentials, AI chat histories, and customer data"* — **five API calls from a free account**.
- Exposed: source code, **database credentials**, AI chat histories, customer data. Since Lovable apps embed Supabase/Stripe/Google API credentials, this is a supply-chain exposure, not just a privacy one.
- Lovable patched in March **only for projects created after November 2025** — every project created **before Nov 2025** stayed exposed. Root cause traced to Feb 2026, when Lovable *"accidentally re-enabled access to chats on public projects during backend permissions unification."*
- **HackerOne closed the follow-up report as a duplicate.** Lovable, verbatim: the reports *"were closed without escalation because our HackerOne partners thought that seeing public projects' chats was the intended behaviour."*
- Lovable's response arc: *"We did not suffer a data breach"* → blamed unclear documentation → called it *"intentional behaviour"* / "by design" → finally *"We understand that pointing to documentation issues alone was not enough here. We'll do better."*
- Named customers on the platform: Uber, Zendesk, Deutsche Telekom.
- Source (fetched): https://www.theregister.com/2026/04/20/lovable_denies_data_leak/ · also https://thenextweb.com/news/lovable-vibe-coding-security-crisis-exposed (blocked) · https://www.computing.co.uk/news/2026/security/lovable-flaw-exposed-source-code-credentials-and-ai-chats
- **Note the date collision:** El Reg's URL is dated 20 April 2026 but describes disclosure on "Monday, April 21, 2026," and it dates the HackerOne report to "March 3, 2025" while the surrounding narrative is 2026. One of those is a typo in the source. **DOĞRULANMADI: exact report date, 3 Mar 2025 vs 3 Mar 2026.** Verify before quoting.

**2. Grants vs policies.** Almost nobody knows this. A table with perfect RLS policies is still exposed if the original `GRANT` to `anon` was never revoked. AI codegen never touches grants. This is a sharper, less-said point than "turn on RLS."

**3. The `Authorization: Bearer` break.** The single most likely thing to silently break a vibecoded app during the key migration: new keys must go in the `apikey` header, and Edge Functions don't verify `apikey` for new keys at all (`verify_jwt = false`, authorize in code). That's a footgun that turns a key rename into an authz hole if someone half-migrates.

**4. Lint rules 0023, 0024, 0025** (`sensitive_columns_exposed`, `permissive_rls_policy`, `public_bucket_allows_listing`) look like recent additions and map exactly to AI-generated failure modes — including public Storage buckets, which nobody in this conversation has mentioned but which leak just as hard as a table.

**5. The 20-minute revocation rule.** JWKS edge cache is 10 min; docs say wait **at least 20 minutes** before revoking a signing key. Revoke too fast and you sign out valid users. Not obvious, easy to get wrong in a rotation runbook.

**6. Self-hosted Supabase does not have asymmetric key support at parity** — there's an open issue (supabase/supabase#41843). **DOĞRULANMADI**, I only saw the issue title.

### COULDN'T ACCESS
- **wiz.io** (both the Base44 post and the "Risks in 20% of Vibe-Coded Apps" Sept 2025 report) — domain blocked. Exact request/response bodies from the Base44 post remain unverified by me; endpoint **paths** are corroborated across two independent outlets quoting Wiz.
- thehackernews.com, darkreading.com, infosecurity-magazine.com, thenextweb.com, vulners.com, vibegraveyard.ai — all blocked.
- supabase.com/docs/guides/database/database-advisors returned a transient block on one attempt; I got the full lint list from `/database-linter` instead.
- Supabase changelog page for the Oct 2025 asymmetric-by-default switch — not directly fetched.
- No official Wix/Base44 statement page located.
- SMS-per-hour rate limit row — not present in the version of the rate-limits page I retrieved.

### SPOTIFY VPN / COUNTRY RESTRICTION

What actually happened:
- Spotify did NOT enforce a rule requiring you to be in your signup country for individual accounts. No announced policy enforcement comparable to Netflix's password sharing crackdown.
- The practical friction tightened in 2025: **foreign cards largely rejected** for new sign-ups in Turkey, India, Argentina. Gift cards remain a workaround.
- Spotify confirmed in **May 2025** that VPN use per se does not result in bans (per community sources). Risk exists for Family/Duo plans (home address verification).
- **USD pricing switch for Turkey: NOT confirmed.** Spotify continues billing in TRY as of August 2026. No official statement or credible press confirming a switch was planned. Turkey's competition authority (Rekabet Kurumu) opened an **antitrust probe into Spotify's pricing practices** in September 2025 — this may be partly why Spotify is cautious about further changes.
- The rumor of a USD switch circulates on VPN-guide sites but has no sourced confirmation.

Source: Spotify Community forums, Turkish Minute (Sep 25, 2025 antitrust probe), various VPN-guide analyses.

# AWS Budgets as a solo-dev spend kill switch — primary-source findings

**Headline caveat, from AWS itself:** a budget is *not* a spend cap. "There can be a delay between when you incur a charge and when you receive a notification... You might incur additional costs or usage that exceed your budget notification threshold before AWS Budgets can notify you." Budget data updates **up to 3 times a day, typically 8–12 hours apart**. — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/budgets-managing-costs.html

## 1. Budgets: counts, cost, thresholds

**Pricing** — https://aws.amazon.com/aws-cost-management/aws-budgets/pricing/
- Monitoring + notifications: **free**, no per-budget charge. ("You can monitor and receive notifications on your budgets free of charge.")
- The famous "2 free" is **only about action-enabled budgets**: "Your first two action-enabled budgets are free (regardless of the number of actions you configure per budget) per month." Each subsequent action-enabled budget = **$0.10/day** (~$3.00–3.10/mo). No cap stated.
- Budget Reports (emailed): **$0.01 per delivered report**.

**Quotas** — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/management-limits.html (verified directly, verbatim table)
| Free budgets with actions per account | 2 |
| Actions per budget | 10 |
| Budget actions per account | 100 |
| Total budgets per management account | 20,000 |
| Budgets using a custom billing view | 150 |
| Budget reports max / budgets per report / email recipients per report | 50 / 50 / 50 |

**Notifications per budget** — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/aws-cost-management/latest/APIReference/API_budgets_Notification.html (verified directly)
- **"A budget can have up to ten notifications."**
- **"A notification can have one SNS subscriber and up to 10 email subscribers, for a total of 11 subscribers."**
- Doc conflict: `API_budgets_CreateBudget.html` prose says "up to five notifications" while its own schema says `Maximum number of 10 items`. **Use 10; treat "5" as a stale doc bug.** Exceeding → `CreationLimitExceededException`, HTTP 400.

**Actual vs forecasted are NOT separate pools.** `NotificationType` is a required field with `Valid Values: ACTUAL | FORECASTED` — each is one of your ten slots. So a 10-slot budget could be e.g. 5 actual + 5 forecasted thresholds.

**Thresholds:** always a percentage by default; `ThresholdType: PERCENTAGE | ABSOLUTE_VALUE`; `ComparisonOperator: GREATER_THAN | LESS_THAN | EQUAL_TO`. You *can* exceed 100% — "maximum limit for your threshold is 1,000,000% above the budgeted amount" (schema says max `15000000000000`; the two disagree — DOĞRULANMADI which binds).

**Forecast mechanics** — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/budgets-best-practices.html
- **~5 weeks of usage data required.** "If you set a budget to alert based on a forecasted amount, this budget alert isn't triggered until you have enough historical usage information." **A brand-new account cannot use forecasted alerts at all** — this is the single most important fact for a solo dev spinning up a fresh account.
- Forecasts up to **3 years** ahead; forecasting only begins when the current date is within **12 months** of the end date.
- **Actual alerts fire once per budget period.** Forecast alerts "might alert more than once" (exceed → dip → exceed).
- No confidence-interval mechanic is documented — DOĞRULANMADI as a concept, don't claim one.
- Recipients restated here: "up to 10 email addresses and one Amazon SNS topic per alert."

## 2. Budgets → SNS → Lambda

**Hard constraint:** "Amazon SNS topics must be in the same account as the Budgets you're configuring. Cross-account Amazon SNS isn't supported." — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/budgets-sns-policy.html

**The exact topic policy statement** (same URL), inserted after `"Statement": [`:
```json
{
  "Sid": "AWSBudgetsSNSPublishingPermissions",
  "Effect": "Allow",
  "Principal": { "Service": "budgets.amazonaws.com" },
  "Action": "SNS:Publish",
  "Resource": "{{your topic ARN}}",
  "Condition": {
    "StringEquals": { "aws:SourceAccount": "{{<account-id>}}" },
    "ArnLike": { "aws:SourceArn": "arn:aws:budgets::{{<account-id>}}:*" }
  }
}
```
Spelling matters: `SNS:Publish` (capitalized prefix, string not array), and the SourceArn has an **empty region segment** (`budgets::`) because budgets is a global-ARN service.

Steps: SNS console → Topics → Create topic → Access policy → **Advanced** → paste above → Create → copy ARN from Details → **Edit** → replace `{{your topic ARN}}` → Save. The chicken-and-egg is in AWS's own numbering.

Three documented failure modes: "Please comply with SNS ARN format" (ARN syntax); **"Invalid SNS topic"** (missing the resource policy above); **"The SNS topic is encrypted"** — SSE-KMS topics silently don't work without extra KMS grants, and AWS's own troubleshooting says **disable encryption**. The KMS key-policy JSON is referenced in the doc but did not render on repeated fetches — **DOĞRULANMADI**.

**The payload is the weak spot.** There is **no formal JSON schema for the budget notification message anywhere on docs.aws.amazon.com** — searched, not found. `Sns.Message` is human-readable prose, and the two AWS blog examples disagree in layout:
- https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/mt/smart-budgeting-using-lambda-and-service-catalog/ → `"Message": "AWS Budget Notification

Dear AWS Customer,

You requested that we notify you when your Actual Cost for your budget \"BudgetAlert\" is greater than $50000. Your Actual Cost for this budget is now $50393..."`
- https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/messaging-and-targeting/establishing-finops-management-integrating-aws-budgets-with-whatsapp-using-aws-end-user-messaging/ → labelled layout: `Budget Name: Budget-Alert-EUM...`, subject prefixed `[Test]`

**Design consequence: do not `JSON.parse` the Message, do not regex the dollar figure as truth.** Treat arrival as the signal; pull the number from `DescribeBudget` if you need it.

**SNS → Lambda** — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/with-sns.html
Easiest: add the trigger from the *Lambda* console (Configuration → Triggers → Add trigger → SNS) — "Lambda automatically sets up the necessary permissions and subscriptions." Doing it from the SNS side does **not** create the resource policy. Manual = two calls:
```
aws lambda add-permission --function-name example-function \
  --source-arn arn:aws:sns:us-east-1:123456789012:topic \
  --statement-id function-with-sns --action "lambda:InvokeFunction" \
  --principal sns.amazonaws.com

aws sns subscribe --protocol lambda --region us-east-1 \
  --topic-arn arn:aws:sns:... --notification-endpoint arn:aws:lambda:...
```
Envelope: `event.Records[].Sns.Message` (key is `Sns`, not `SNS`; Message is a **string**). Also `Sns.Subject`, `Sns.MessageId`, `Sns.TopicArn`.

Two operational traps on that page: **"Lambda asynchronous invocations process each event at least once"** — AWS "strongly recommends" idempotent code, and if your Lambda does something destructive on a budget alert, duplicate delivery fires it twice; dedup on `Sns.MessageId`. And **retries run "at increasing intervals over several hours"**, so an alert can land long after the breach. FIFO topics are not supported.

## 3. Budget Actions

**Action types** — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/budgets-controls.html — apply an **IAM policy**, attach an **SCP**, or target specific **EC2 or RDS instances**. SCPs only from a management account; you cannot target EC2/RDS in another account.

**Triggers on both** — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/budgets-action-configure.html — "choose Actual to create an alert for actual spend. Or, choose Forecasted." Threshold can be absolute value or % of budgeted amount. **"You can apply multiple budget actions to a single alert."** Every alert must have at least one of {email, SNS topic, budget action}.

**Approval:** field *"Do you want to automatically run this action when this threshold is exceeded"* → **Yes** = auto, **No** = manual run from the Alert details page. You get notified either way, regardless of preference.

**Lifecycle** — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/budgets-action-review.html — statuses **Standby / Requires approval / Completed / Reversed**. Reversed = undone, and "AWS Budgets will no longer evaluate the action for the remaining budgeted period." **Reset** re-arms it. Console action history shows only **60 days**; full history via CloudTrail or `DescribeBudgetActionHistories`.

**IAM role trust policy** — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/billing-example-policies.html
```json
{"Version":"2012-10-17","Statement":[{
  "Effect":"Allow",
  "Principal":{"Service":"budgets.amazonaws.com"},
  "Action":"sts:AssumeRole",
  "Condition":{
    "ArnLike":{"aws:SourceArn":"arn:aws:budgets::123456789012:budget/*"},
    "StringEquals":{"aws:SourceAccount":"123456789012"}}}]}
```
Managed policy for the *service role*: **`AWSBudgetsActions_RolePolicyForResourceAdministrationWithSSM`** (EC2/RDS start-stop gated by `aws:CalledVia: ssm.amazonaws.com`, scoped to SSM documents `AWS-StartEC2Instance`, `AWS-StopEC2Instance`, `AWS-StartRdsInstance`, `AWS-StopRdsInstance`). **It does NOT cover IAM/SCP attach** — for that you add an inline policy with `iam:Attach*/Detach*{Group,Role,User}Policy` and `organizations:AttachPolicy/DetachPolicy`. Human admin policy: `AWSBudgetsActionsWithAWSResourceControlAccess` (note: grants `budgets:*` and a broad `iam:PassRole` — privilege-escalation-adjacent, don't hand out casually). — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/billing-permissions-ref.html

**Cost of actions:** 2 action-enabled budgets free/month, then $0.10/day each. **DOĞRULANMADI:** whether that $0.10 is prorated or charged per day of existence.

## 4. Cost Anomaly Detection as complement

- **Free** — but there is **no CAD pricing page**; the claim rests on the FAQ ("no effort or cost") at https://aws.amazon.com/aws-cost-management/aws-cost-anomaly-detection/faqs/ plus the launch announcement. Requires Cost Explorer enabled.
- **Auto-configured since 27 Mar 2023** for new Cost Explorer users: AWS-services monitor + daily email to the account's primary address, alerting when spend is **>$100 AND >40% above expected**. Pre-2023 accounts must configure manually. — https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2023/03/aws-cost-anomaly-detection-configured-cost-explorer-users/
- **Channels** — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/getting-started-ad.html — **Individual alerts require an SNS topic** (email alone insufficient); Daily summaries = email, top 10 by cost impact, generated 00:00 UTC; Weekly summaries = email. Slack/Chime via SNS → Amazon Q Developer in chat applications. Up to two thresholds per subscription combined with AND/OR.
- **Quotas** (verified table): 100 alert subscriptions/account, **10 email recipients + 1 SNS topic per subscription**, 502 monitors per subscription, 1 AWS-managed services monitor + 1 additional managed monitor (2 total, mgmt acct), 500 customer-managed monitors.
- **Latency: up to 24 hours to detect.** Needs **10 days minimum** historical data. Runs ~3×/day on **net unblended cost**, Usage charge type only.
- **Killer limitation for an AI-infra solo dev:** CAD's **unsupported services list includes AWS Marketplace — and AWS states this explicitly covers "third-party LLMs like Anthropic Claude models on Amazon Bedrock (which appear under the legal entity 'Anthropic, PBC')... To get alerts for these charges, use AWS Budgets."** Also unsupported: AWS Support, WorkSpaces, Cost Explorer, **Budgets itself**, Shield, Route 53, ACM. — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cost-management/latest/userguide/manage-ad.html

---

## DÖKÜM — not asked, but load-bearing

1. **Budget actions do not stop autoscaling.** "If a budget action is used to stop an Amazon EC2 instance in an Auto Scaling Group (ASG), Amazon EC2 Auto Scaling restarts the instance, or launches new instances." You need a second action stripping the launch-config role's permissions. This breaks the naive "auto-shutdown on breach" story.
2. **Nothing here stops serverless spend.** No action type can halt Lambda, Bedrock, or S3. The only real lever is a Deny IAM policy / SCP. For a solo dev, the honest kill switch is: forecasted-threshold alert → action attaching a scoped `Deny` policy — not "stop instances."
3. **SNS subscriptions must be manually confirmed** or notifications silently never arrive. Check SNS console → Subscriptions → filter `budget` → status `PendingConfirmation` → "Request confirmation."
4. **EC2/RDS stop runs through SSM Automation**, so SSM must be functional in the region; the managed policy's `aws:CalledVia` condition means the role can't call `ec2:StopInstances` directly.
5. `AWSBudgetsActions_RolePolicyForResourceAdministrationWithSSM` was **updated 2026-04-07** to require automation-execution/document permissions — copied older snippets are stale.
6. **Opt-in region trap** for SNS→Lambda: in Hong Kong/Bahrain/Milan/Cape Town the Lambda policy principal must be `sns.<region>.amazonaws.com`, not `sns.amazonaws.com`. — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/sns/latest/dg/lambda-prereq.html
7. Creating your first budget **auto-enables Cost Explorer**; graphs can take 24h to populate.
8. Custom-period budgets **don't auto-renew**; end date must be within 3 years; end date exclusive; 00:00 UTC only.
9. A billing-view `INVALID_FILTER` health status **silently pauses budget spend updates**.
10. AWS's own JS/TS samples do `JSON.stringify(record.Sns.Message)` on an already-string field — double-encodes it.
11. Budgets endpoint: `budgets.amazonaws.com`, global, signed against us-east-1.

## Could not access / verify
- **KMS key policy JSON for SSE-encrypted budget SNS topics** — referenced in budgets-sns-policy.html, body never rendered across 3 fetches. **DOĞRULANMADI.** Workaround per AWS's own troubleshooting: use an unencrypted topic.
- **No canonical schema for the delivered budget notification message.** Only contradictory blog examples. The only way to settle it is to fire a test alert in a live account and capture the CloudWatch log line — not done here.
- **DOĞRULANMADI:** whether FORECASTED triggers work for *usage* budgets and for every action type (docs describe the UI generically). Also the API-level enums for actions (`ActionType`, `ApprovalModel`, `ExecutionRoleArn`) — API Reference for actions not opened.
- **DOĞRULANMADI:** proration of the $0.10/day action-enabled-budget charge.
- **Retrieval hazard worth knowing:** `billing-quotas.html` returned plausible-but-wrong numbers (invented "100 CAD monitors" and a fake quota-increase section). `management-limits.html` is canonical and I re-fetched it directly to confirm every quota cited above. Also `billing-limits.html` returns an empty redirect shell.
- The doc conflict on notifications-per-budget (5 vs 10) is unresolved *in AWS's own docs*; I verified 10 from the authoritative type page, but the quotas table lists no notifications-per-budget row at all, so there's no third source to break the tie.

# TOPIC A: AI BROWSERS

## OpenAI Atlas — launched 21 Oct 2025, SHUT DOWN 9 Aug 2026 (292 days)

This is the single biggest finding, and it landed nine days ago.

- **Launch: 21 Oct 2025**, macOS only, worldwide. Windows/iOS/Android "coming soon." — https://openai.com/index/introducing-chatgpt-atlas/ , https://www.engadget.com/ai/openais-ai-powered-browser-chatgpt-atlas-launches-on-macos-today-170735742.html
- Features at launch: ChatGPT sidebar with page context, **Browser Memories**, **Agent Mode** (Plus/Pro only — freemium). Browsing data not used for training by default. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ChatGPT_Atlas
- Feature timeline: **Nov 2025** vertical tabs + iCloud Keychain passkeys; **Jan 2026** tab groups + "Auto" mode switching between ChatGPT answers and Google results. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ChatGPT_Atlas
- **Mar 2026**: OpenAI announces merging Atlas + ChatGPT desktop + Codex into one desktop app. — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ChatGPT_Atlas
- **Shutdown announced ~10 Jul 2026, effective 9 Aug 2026.** Official OpenAI help article title: "Evolving Atlas into ChatGPT for browser-based agentic work" — https://help.openai.com/en/articles/20001371-evolving-atlas-into-chatgpt-for-browser-based-agentic-work (returned 403 to fetch; title and content confirmed via secondary reporting)
  - https://www.techradar.com/pro/openai-shuts-down-its-atlas-browser-after-not-even-a-year
  - https://www.techrepublic.com/article/news-chatgpt-atlas-shutdown-migration/
  - https://piunikaweb.com/2026/07/10/openai-shutting-down-chatgpt-atlas-browser/
  - https://9to5mac.com/2026/08/04/openai-explains-what-will-happen-when-chatgpt-atlas-shuts-down-this-weekend/
- Migration terms: bookmarks do **not** transfer automatically; open tabs and history may not carry over. Users told to export before 9 Aug. — TechRepublic (above)
- **Lifespan: 292 days.** — https://finance.biggo.com/news/db37c811-b53d-4b4e-8eca-c3f353d7fe1b (domain blocked to direct fetch; figure appears in title + search index, and is arithmetically correct: 21 Oct 2025 → 9 Aug 2026 = 292 days)

**Adoption numbers — weak, and that's the story.**
- No credible third-party MAU/market-share figure for Atlas exists. OpenAI never published one. — https://axis-intelligence.com/browser-market-share-statistics/
- As of **Jul 2026**, six major browsers = 97.34% of measured sessions; Atlas sits inside the remaining **2.66%** long tail alongside all other AI-first browsers. — same source
- **All AI browsers combined never reached 1% of global market share.** — https://finance.biggo.com/news/9b38e76e-8ac0-4389-8a1b-29dea82c1329 (secondary, SEO-adjacent) — **DOĞRULANMADI as a precise measurement**, but consistent with the Axis 2.66% long-tail figure.
- Enterprise download signal (the one real early datapoint): Cyberhaven found **27.7% of enterprises** had at least one employee download Atlas, and Atlas saw **62x more corporate downloads than Comet**. — https://www.cyberhaven.com/blog/browser-agent-security-risk-chatgpt-atlas . Note the tension: strong enterprise download curiosity, still killed in 292 days. Downloads ≠ retention.
- **UNVERIFIED / likely SEO slop**: claims of "175% QoQ growth in Q1 2026" and "25–100M potential users in 2026" come from presenc.ai and digitalapplied.com, both content-farm domains with no methodology. Do not cite these.
- Windows: **never got a full standalone release**; entered public beta mid-2026 and died with the product. — https://www.datastudios.org/post/chatgpt-atlas-for-windows-release-date-features-agent-mode-and-browser-memory-explained (**DOĞRULANMADI**, weak source; but Metaculus had an open question "Will Atlas browser be released for Windows before September 1, 2026?" as late as 2026, which corroborates that no Windows release had shipped — https://www.metaculus.com/questions/43525/)

## Perplexity Comet — the opposite arc, free and alive

- **Launch: 9 Jul 2025**, macOS + Windows, **Perplexity Max only ($200/mo)**. Waitlist "in the millions." Srinivas called it "early Gmail launch vibes." — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comet_(browser) , https://www.storyboard18.com/digital/perplexitys-new-ai-browser-comet-launches-in-india-exclusively-for-pro-users-81456.htm

## The Browser Company — Arc dead, sold, Dia survives inside Atlassian

- **27 May 2025**: Josh Miller publishes "Letter to Arc members 2025." Active Arc development permanently frozen; security patches only, no new features. — https://browsercompany.substack.com/p/letter-to-arc-members-2025 (fetch blocked) ; mirror: https://rogerwong.me/2025/05/letter-to-arc-members-2025 ; coverage: https://9to5mac.com/2025/05/27/mac-browser-arc-being-discontinued-in-favor-of-new-dia-app/ , https://techcrunch.com/2025/05/27/the-browser-company-mulls-selling-or-open-sourcing-arc-browser-amid-ai-focused-pivot/
- **Miller's exact diagnosis — the "novelty tax":** *"For most people, Arc was simply too different, with too many new things to learn, for too little reward… On top of that, Arc lacked cohesion in both its core features and core values."*
- **The damning usage numbers from the letter**: Spaces (Arc's most-praised feature) used by only **5–12% of users**; calendar preview by **0.4%**. — https://www.ghacks.net/2025/05/27/arc-browser-has-been-discontinued-but-the-companys-building-a-new-browser-dia/
- TechCrunch (27 May 2025) reported the company was **mulling selling or open-sourcing Arc**. It never did either; it sold the whole company instead.
- **Dia launched 11 Jun 2025** (announced Dec 2024). — https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dia_(web_browser)
- **Atlassian acquisition — all three details verified:**
  - **Announced 4 Sept 2025**, **~$610M all-cash**, inclusive of TBC's cash balance, funded from Atlassian's balance sheet. — https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20250904645125/en/Atlassian-Enters-Into-Definitive-Agreement-to-Acquire-The-Browser-Company-of-New-York (primary press release, also filed as Atlassian 8-K: https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1650372/000165037225000040/ex991pressrelease.htm)
  - **Closed 21 Oct 2025** — the *exact same day Atlas launched*. — https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20251021473486/en/Atlassian-Completes-Acquisition-of-The-Browser-Company-of-New-York
  - Not material to Atlassian FY2026/FY2027 financials. Miller stated TBC would operate independently and keep building Dia.
- **After the deal**: Dia is being repositioned as an **enterprise/knowledge-work browser**, which drew user backlash. — https://www.thurrott.com/cloud/web-browsers/326351/it-looks-like-atlassian-is-going-to-ruin-dia-for-everyone , https://www.reworked.co/collaboration-productivity/inside-atlassians-plan-to-make-dia-an-ai-browser-built-for-real-work/
- **Dia was NOT shut down.** As of Aug 2026 it is active. Arc still runs with security patches only.

# TOPIC B: AI SEARCH / RESEARCH WRAPPERS

## The two OpenAI dates that reset the category

- **ChatGPT Search: 31 Oct 2024.** Launched to Plus and Team users first. Model = fine-tuned GPT-4o, post-trained with synthetic data distilled from o1-preview. — https://openai.com/index/introducing-chatgpt-search/ , https://www.cnbc.com/2024/10/31/openai-launches-chatgpt-search-competing-with-google-and-perplexity.html , https://searchengineland.com/chatgpt-search-officially-launches-447919
  - **16 Dec 2024**: all logged-in users. **5 Feb 2025**: everyone, **no signup required**. — https://techcrunch.com/?p=2932195
- **Deep Research: 2 Feb 2025.** Pro tier first ($200/mo), Plus and Team "next," web first then mobile/desktop within the month. Agent browses autonomously 5–30 min across hundreds of sources. — https://openai.com/index/introducing-deep-research/ , https://cdn.openai.com/API/docs/deep_research_blog.pdf
- **Coda: legacy Deep Research mode removed 26 Mar 2026** — the original o3-based agent was retired and folded into the main model. — https://findskill.ai/blog/openai-deep-research-legacy-removal/ (**DOĞRULANMADI**, weak source; corroborated by OpenAI community complaint thread https://community.openai.com/t/final-notice-deep-research-feature-removed-10-days-ignored-escalation-failure/1263635). Even the feature that killed the wrappers got absorbed.

## Perplexity — funding vertical, traffic flat, the squeeze is real

**Funding ladder (dates, amounts, valuations):**
| Date | Amount | Valuation | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2024 start | — | $520M | https://aifundingtracker.com/perplexity-ai-valuation-growth-strategy/ |
| Apr 2024 | — | $1B | https://synthedia.substack.com/p/perplexity-enterprise-debuts-alongside |
| Jun 2024 | — | $3B (SoftBank Vision Fund 2) | aifundingtracker |
| Dec 2024 | $500M | $9B | aifundingtracker |
| **14 May / Jun 2025** | **$500M** (Series E, led by Accel) | **$14B** | https://www.cnbc.com/2025/05/12/perplexity-funding-round-comet.html , https://tracxn.com/d/companies/perplexity/ |
| Jul 2025 | $100M extension | **$18B** | https://finance.yahoo.com/news/perplexity-ai-achieves-18bn-valuation-113151944.html |
| Sept 2025 | $200M | **$20B** | https://techfundingnews.com/perplexity-raises-200m-at-20b-valuation-ai-search/ |
| 5 Jun 2026 (reported) | ~$200M | ~$20B | The Information, via https://www.leadgen-economy.com/blog/perplexity-comet-200m-comet-plus-publisher-revenue-share/ |
| Jan 2026 | — | **$23B** | https://valueaddvc.com/blog/perplexity-ai-valuation-revenue-2026-23b-450m-arr' — **DOĞRULANMADI** |

Total raised: **$1.72B over 11 rounds, 63 investors.** — Tracxn.
**No evidence of a $50B valuation.** Highest credible figure is $20B (Sept 2025), with a weakly-sourced $23B claim for Jan 2026.

**Revenue:**
- **2024: ~$34M revenue, ~$65M cash burned** — financials seen by **The Information**. This is the single hardest unit-economics number available. — reported via https://www.techcrunch.com/2025/07/02/perplexity-launches-a-200-monthly-subscription-plan/ and The Information
- **Perplexity spent 164% of its 2024 revenue on AWS + Anthropic + OpenAI alone.** — Ed Zitron, https://www.wheresyoured.at/why-everybody-is-losing-money-on-ai/ . It paid its suppliers more than its customers paid it.
- ~$100M ARR early 2025 → **$450M+ ARR by Mar 2026** (FT-reported, +50%). — https://seekingalpha.com/news/4573154
- Sacra: **$500M annualized, Apr 2026, +335% YoY.** — https://sacra.com/c/perplexity/
- Projected **$656M ARR by end-2026.** — https://sherwood.news/tech/perplexity-has-been-left-behind-in-the-ai-wars/

**The squeeze — this is the evidence you wanted:**
- **US website traffic broadly FLAT: fewer than 4M added visitors, Feb 2025 → Feb 2026**, while Claude more than quadrupled its web users. — Sherwood News (above)
- Scale gap, Feb 2026 US monthly website visits: **ChatGPT 1 billion, Gemini 276 million, Perplexity a rounding error.** — Sherwood
- **US daily active user share collapsed 65% in five months: 6% (Oct 2025) → 2% (Mar 2026).** — https://seranking.com/blog/ai-traffic-research-study/
- **Global web traffic share 2.0% (Mar 2026) → ~1.3% (data to 26 May 2026)**, Similarweb. — https://www.similarweb.com/blog/marketing/geo/gen-ai-stats/
- US share of AI traffic: **11.42% (2025) → 6.85% (2026).** — SE Ranking
- Category context (Similarweb, Jun 2025 → May 2026): ChatGPT **76% → 53%**; Gemini **<9% → 27–28%**; Claude **~2% → ~9%**. Perplexity is not gaining in the reshuffle — Gemini and Claude are.
- Product retrenchment: **Perplexity abandoned advertising** and refocused on subscription/enterprise revenue (Feb 2026) — Srinivas: "we will focus more on revenue." — https://www.adsx.com/blog/perplexity-updates-february-2026 , https://finviz.com/news/313893/
- **Perplexity Pages — quietly gutted.** The standalone "Create a Page" button was **removed in early 2026**; only thread-conversion remains, marked "temporarily retired." Launched May 2024, never fully rolled out. — https://www.perplexity.ai/help-center/en/articles/10352968-perplexity-pages , https://liaxinweng.substack.com/p/perplexity-pages-why-it-failed-and

The pattern: **valuation up 38x, revenue up ~15x, consumer traffic flat-to-down.** The growth moved to enterprise seats. The consumer search wrapper stopped compounding.

## Phind — SHUT DOWN 16 Jan 2026, five weeks after raising $10.4M

This is the cleanest wrapper-death case in the whole research.

- **Shut down 16 Jan 2026**, no sunset period, no advance warning. — https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46595637
- **Founder Michael Royzen's exact words:** *"I've made the difficult decision to shut down Phind. This Friday, January 16th, will be our last day of search operation. You will get a prorated refund on that date if you are a paid user."*
- **He gave no reason.** The HN thread's most notable feature is the absence of explanation — no funding, market, or technical rationale offered. Users had until **30 Jan 2026** to export chat history before servers were wiped.
- **Raised $10.4M Series A on 3 Dec 2025, led by Bessemer Venture Partners** — then shut down **44 days later**. — https://neuronfeed.com/startups/phind , https://intelligenttools.co/blog/improved-phind-shutdown-post
- Y Combinator-backed; had served **500,000+ developers**.
- **Search interest down 91% from its April 2024 peak** by early 2026, as GPT-4/Claude/GitHub Copilot absorbed the coding-question use case. — https://intelligenttools.co/tools/phind (**DOĞRULANMADI** — Google Trends–derived, secondary source, not a traffic measurement)
- **Odd loose thread**: a viral post claims Phind didn't just die quietly but **turned into a hedge fund** — *"THIS is what happened to Phind??? I thought it was a quiet death, not turning into a hedge fund."* — https://x.com/michael_chomsky/status/2044523667800134081 . **DOĞRULANMADI**, single tweet, but if true it reframes the story from "failure" to "founder redeployed the capital." Worth chasing before you say anything publicly about Phind.

## The research-wrapper cohort — who lived, who died

**DIED / PIVOTED:**
- **Phind** — shut down 16 Jan 2026 (above).
- **You.com** — **pivoted hard away from consumer search to enterprise API/agents** after ChatGPT hit. Now sells search infrastructure to **DuckDuckGo, Harvey AI, Windsurf, Databricks**. Customers have built ~**100,000 internal agents**. Raised $50M in 2024 explicitly to fund the pivot; **became a unicorn (>$1B) in 2025**. — https://thenewstack.io/how-you-com-survived-chatgpt-with-a-pivot-to-enterprise-ai/ , https://techstartups.com/2024/06/18/ai-startup-you-com-is-raising-50m-in-funding-to-pivot-from-ai-powered-search-engine-to-ai-assistant-market/ . Note the framing in the headline: **"How You.com *Survived* ChatGPT"** — survival required abandoning the consumer product entirely.
- **scite** — **acquired 27 Nov 2023 by Research Solutions for $20.9M** (agreement 24 Nov 2023 at $14.8M + earnout, 50% cash / 50% stock, earnout paid quarterly over two years). Pre-dates Deep Research. — https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/research-solutions-announces-acquisition-of-scite-301997608.html , https://www.marketscreener.com/quote/stock/RESEARCH-SOLUTIONS-INC-15295099/news/Research-Solutions-Inc-completed-the-acquisition-of-Scite-Inc-for-20-9-million-45517866/

**SURVIVED, and specifically by NOT being a chat wrapper:**
- **Exa (formerly Metaphor)** — the standout. **$250M Series C announced 20 May 2026**, led by a16z, **$2.2B post-money**, tripled from **$700M in fall 2025**. **5,000+ company customers, 400,000+ developers**. Prior: $85M Series B. — https://exa.ai/blog/announcing-series-c (primary), https://www.pymnts.com/news/artificial-intelligence/2026/exa-raises-250-million-for-ai-powered-search-infrastructure/ . Exa sells **to** the agents rather than competing with them — the search API is infrastructure, not a wrapper.
- **Genspark** — **$250M ARR in 2026** (from $50M in 2025), **$2.6B post-money**, **$535M raised across 4 rounds**. Hit $100M ARR within 9 months of its Apr 2025 launch; **$300M Series B (28 Jan 2026)**, then **$100M extension to $485M at $2.6B (17 Jun 2026)** led by Sozo Ventures, Korea Mirae Asset, UpHonest. Added $150M ARR in Q1 2026 alone. — https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260617758937/en/ , https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260128322682/en/ , https://getlatka.com/companies/genspark.ai . Genspark went *agentic workspace*, not *search*.
- **Elicit** — alive. **$31M raised total**, **$22M Series A early 2025** co-led by Spark Capital and Footwork (Jeff Dean among backers). **2M+ researchers** as of 2026; **57 employees as of 30 Jun 2026**. Spun out of nonprofit Ought in 2023. — https://tracxn.com/d/companies/elicit/ , https://startupintros.com/orgs/elicit . Elicit publicly **benchmarked itself against ChatGPT Deep Research and claims to win** on structured synthesis (Reports mode covers up to 500 sources; Systematic Review workflow with inclusion/exclusion screening) — https://elicit.com/blog/elicit-reports-eval/ . That is a defense-by-depth response to commoditization, not a founder admitting defeat.
- **Consensus** — alive. **$45.6M raised, 29 employees**, hit **$1M revenue Nov 2024**, **closed 2025 at 8x prior-year revenue**, **8M+ users across 10,000+ universities by 2026**, 220M papers indexed. — https://getlatka.com/companies/consensus.app , https://pitchbook.com/profiles/company/104563-63 , https://pasqualepillitteri.it/en/news/1291/
- **Andi** — alive. **PCMag Best Free AI Search Engine, Mar 2026** — third consecutive year. — https://andiai.com/
- **Kagi** — alive, bootstrapped, public benefit corp. **~50,000 paying subscribers as of Jun 2026**, ≈**$6M ARR** at ~$10/mo average. Crossed $1M revenue in 2023. Shipped Kagi Translate and opened its Search API preview in 2026. — https://help.kagi.com/kagi/company/history.html , https://www.primointeractive.com/blog/kagi-paid-search-marketers
- **Sourcely** — alive, 200M+ papers. — https://www.sourcely.net/ . No funding/revenue data found.

## Founder commentary on being commoditized — the honest answer

**I could not find a single named founder in this cohort explicitly saying "Deep Research commoditized us."** I searched for it directly, several ways. What exists instead:

- **Royzen's silence** is itself the finding. The Phind founder shut down a YC-backed, 500K-developer, freshly-funded company and **published no post-mortem**. The "foundation models added web search and ate our moat" explanation is **journalist inference, not founder testimony** — every source stating it (intelligenttools.co, aitrendtool.com, toolsforhumans.ai) is a low-quality AI-generated tool directory with no quoted source. **Treat the causal claim as DOĞRULANMADI.** The dates are solid; the *why* is not.
- The nearest thing to a real quote is aggregate commentary: OpenAI's 2024 product cadence (GPT Store, Operator, Tasks, Canvas, Search) *"directly cannibalized at least 200 funded 'GPT wrapper' startups in 2024 alone."* — https://ideaproof.io/failures/ai-startups — **DOĞRULANMADI**, methodology unpublished.
- Sector framing exists but from investors, not operators: https://www.nfx.com/post/ai-wrapper-dead-verticalization-startups , https://saanyaojha.substack.com/p/revenge-of-the-wrappers , https://analyticsindiamagazine.substack.com/p/openai-just-killed-thousands-of-startups
- **The one founder-authored, on-the-record admission of a consumer AI product failing is Josh Miller's Arc letter**, and his stated cause is **not** commoditization — it is **retention and the novelty tax**. That is a materially different and more useful failure mode: Arc didn't die because OpenAI copied it; it died because 88–95% of its own users never touched its signature feature.

---

# ALSO FOUND (unasked, load-bearing)

1. **Atlassian closed the TBC acquisition on 21 Oct 2025 — the exact day Atlas launched.** Same-day collision. Nobody in the coverage connects these; the juxtaposition is yours to use.
2. **Atlas's shutdown reason is consolidation, not stated failure.** OpenAI's framing is "evolving Atlas into ChatGPT for browser-based agentic work" — the browser became a *mode* inside an app. The lesson isn't "AI browsers don't work," it's "the browser was never the unit; the agent was."
3. **Even OpenAI killed its own Deep Research** — legacy mode retired 26 Mar 2026, absorbed into the main model. The feature that allegedly commoditized a dozen startups was itself commoditized by the next model.
4. **Perplexity abandoned advertising entirely (Feb 2026)** — the monetization model most consumer AI search startups were counting on, dropped by the best-funded player in the category.
5. **Perplexity paid 164% of 2024 revenue to AWS + Anthropic + OpenAI.** For a wrapper argument this is the strongest single number in the entire research: your COGS is your competitor's revenue.
6. **The winners in both topics sold infrastructure, not interface.** Exa ($2.2B, sells search API to agents), Island ($4.8B, enterprise security browser), Genspark ($2.6B, agentic workspace), You.com (unicorn only after abandoning consumer). Every consumer-facing AI browser or search wrapper either died, went free, or went flat.
7. **Brave is the counter-example that proves the point** — 117.56M MAU (May 2026), grown steadily, and its AI (Leo, Nov 2023) is a *feature* of a privacy browser with an independent reason to exist, not the product. It also launched a **$59.99 one-time paid tier (Jun 2026)** — one-time, not subscription.
8. **Comet's $200/mo → free in 85 days** is a hard data point on what a consumer AI browser can charge: nothing. Opera Neon is testing $19.90/mo; no adoption data published.
9. **Atlassian ran 2026 layoffs (~1.6K jobs cited)** — https://layoffhedge.com/company/atlassian — **DOĞRULANMADI**, aggregator source. Relevant only if you claim anything about Dia's resourcing post-acquisition.
10. **Search-quality warning**: the 2026 web on these topics is heavily polluted with AI-generated SEO content farms (presenc.ai, digitalapplied.com, aibusinessweekly.net, intelligenttools.co, resourcera.com, datarefs.com, getpanto.ai). They confidently publish contradictory numbers — e.g. Perplexity MAU given variously as 45M, 100M, and 230M for overlapping 2026 periods. **Every Perplexity user-count figure in circulation is unreliable.** Use only Similarweb, Sacra, FT/The Information, and company primaries.

# COULD NOT FIND

- **Any Atlas usage/MAU/market-share figure from OpenAI or a credible measurement firm**, at any point in its 292 days. It launched and died without a public number.
- **Any explicit founder statement** — from Phind, Elicit, Consensus, Genspark, Andi, Sourcely, Kagi, You.com or anyone else — attributing damage to OpenAI's Deep Research. Requested explicitly; does not appear to exist in public.
- **Michael Royzen's reason for shutting down Phind.** Never published. The "commoditized by foundation models" narrative is entirely secondary-source inference.
- **Verification of the "Phind became a hedge fund" claim.** One tweet, no follow-up, no podcast, no filing found.
- **SigmaOS's exact shutdown date** and any official announcement — only a passing TechCrunch mention (22 Jul 2026).
- **Comet's user count from a Perplexity primary source.** The 10M figure is secondary only.
- **You.com traffic data** in any Similarweb 2026 breakdown.
- **Similarweb traffic-collapse data for Elicit, Consensus, Genspark, Sourcely, Andi or Kagi.** No traffic reporting exists for these — they are too small to appear in the panels. The "collapse" narrative for research wrappers is **not supported by traffic data I could find**; the only measured collapse is **Perplexity's** (65% US DAU share drop) and the only confirmed death is **Phind's**.
- **Perplexity 2025/2026 leaked financials.** Only the 2024 numbers ($34M revenue / $65M burn) leaked to The Information. No gross margin, churn, or CAC/LTV figures exist publicly for 2025-2026 — the squeeze evidence is traffic-side, not P&L-side.
- **Strawberry Browser** funding, users, or company details — only review directories.
- **Kagi's 2026 formal financials** — private, bootstrapped, no filings.
- **Confirmation that any browser startup died *specifically because of* Atlas.** The causality runs the other way: SigmaOS folded into ChatGPT's ecosystem, Sidekick was bought pre-Atlas (May 2025), Arc was frozen pre-Atlas (May 2025), and Atlas itself died first. **Atlas was not a killer; it was the highest-profile casualty.** If you were planning to write "startups died after Atlas launched," the data says the opposite and you should not say it.
